Formal Integration of Derived Foliations
Lukas Brantner, Kirill Magidson, Joost Nuiten

Abstract
. Frobenius’ theorem in differential geometry asserts that every involutive subbundle of the tangent bundle of a manifold M M integrates to a decomposition of M M into smooth leaves. We prove an infinitesimal analogue of this result for locally coherent qcqs schemes X X over coherent rings. More precisely, we integrate partition Lie algebroids on X X to formal moduli stacks X → S ^ X\rightarrow\widehat{S} , where S ^ \widehat{S} is the formal leaf space and the fibres of X → S ^ X\rightarrow\widehat{S} are the formal leaves. We deduce that deformations of X X -families of algebro-geometric objects are controlled by partition Lie algebroids on X X . Combining our integration equivalence with a result of Fu, we deduce that Toën–Vezzosi’s infinitesimal derived foliations (under suitable finiteness hypotheses) are formally integrable.



1.
Introduction

A smooth algebraic foliation
\mathcal{F}
on a smooth complex variety
X

is a subbundle

E_{\mathcal{F}}\subseteq T_{X}

which is closed under the commutator bracket of vector fields, i.e. a locally free Lie algebroid whose anchor map is the inclusion of a subbundle.
We call
\mathcal{F}

algebraically integrable
if there is a decomposition

X=\coprod_{\alpha}\mathcal{F}_{\alpha}

of
X

into smooth complex subvarieties
\mathcal{F}_{\alpha}
– the leaves – whose tangent vectors span
E_{\mathcal{F}}\subset T_{X}
. Note that algebraic integrability is a highly nontrivial condition.
One can approach the problem of integrating a given smooth foliation
\mathcal{F}
in three steps.

First, we integrate
\mathcal{F}

formally
, which means that for every point
x\in M
, we find power series

f_{1},\ldots,f_{k}\in\mathbb{C}[[z_{1},\ldots,z_{n}]]

in local coordinates
z_{1},\ldots,z_{n}
such that a local vector field
\alpha
lies in
E_{\mathcal{F}}

if and only if
\alpha.df_{i}=0
for all
i
.

The formal leaf passing through
x
is then encoded by the
functor

\mathcal{F}^{\wedge}_{x}:A\mapsto\{a_{1},\ldots a_{n}\in A\ |\ \forall i:\ f_{i}(a_{1},\ldots a_{n})=0\}

from local Artinian
\mathbb{C}
-algebras to sets.
Varying
x\in X
, we obtain a ‘quotient’ map of prestacks

p:X\rightarrow\widehat{S}

to the formal leaf space such that
\mathcal{F}^{\wedge}_{x}
sends
A
to the set of (isomorphism classes of) dotted lifts

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spec(\mathbb{C})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spec(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{S}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{x}}

Next, we check the local convergence of these power series
to obtain complex manifolds
\mathcal{F}_{x}
passing through all points of
X
, thereby verifying Frobenius theorem for complex manifolds.

Finally, we examine whether or not the various leaves
\mathcal{F}_{x}
are algebraic subvarieties of
X
. If this is the case, we obtain a submersion of Deligne–Mumford stacks
p:X\rightarrow S
such that
X\rightarrow\widehat{S}
is the formal completion of
X
along
p
.

Figure 1.
A foliation (depicted by tangent vectors) along with its formal leaves (in gray) and leaves (in black).

In this article, we generalise the first step to more general Lie algebroids on more general schemes.
More precisely, we replace complex varieties by locally coherent qcqs schemes
X
over
coherent rings
R
,
both possibly derived, and formally integrate
partition Lie algebroids

\rho:E\rightarrow T_{X/R}[1]

with
\rho
not necessarily injective and
E
not
necessarily locally free.

The result of our integration procedure is a
formal moduli stack on
X

– around each point
x
in
X
, we obtain a formal moduli problem
\mathcal{F}_{x}^{\wedge}
encoding the formal leaf passing
through
x
, which can be non-smooth, stacky, and derived.

Example 1.1
.

Let us illustrate our integration procedure in the following example suggested by Abramovich. Consider a foliation on
\mathbb{A}^{2}
generated by a single vector field, such as

v=(x+y)\frac{\partial}{\partial x}+y^{2}\frac{\partial}{\partial y}.

First, assume that the base field
k
has characteristic 0. Then this foliation can be encoded by the free Lie algebroid generated by
v
in
T_{\mathbb{A}^{2}}
. To integrate it, we note that a (formal) curve
\gamma(t)=(x(t),y(t))
in
\mathbb{A}^{2}
is tangent to
v
if
x\textquoteright(t)=x(t)+y(t)
and
y\textquoteright(t)=y(t)^{2}
.
Setting
x(t)=\sum_{n}a_{n}t^{n}
and
y(t)=\sum_{n}b_{n}t^{n}
, we obtain the equations

(n+1)a_{n+1}=a_{n}+b_{n}\qquad\qquad\qquad(n+1)b_{n+1}=\sum_{i+j=n}b_{i}b_{j}.

Solving recursively in terms of
a_{0}
and
b_{0}
, we obtain elements
x(t),y(t)\in k[a_{0},b_{0}][\![t]\!]
, which give rise to a map
k[x,y]\to k[a_{0},b_{0}][\![t]\!]
and hence a map

\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{a}\times\mathbb{A}^{2}\to\mathbb{A}^{2}.

Here
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{a}=\mathrm{Spf}(k[\![t]\!])
denotes the formal additive group. This map in fact determines an action of
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{a}
on
\mathbb{A}^{2}
, and the formal moduli stack
\mathbb{A}^{2}\to S
corresponding to the Lie algebroid generated by the vector field
v
is the homotopy quotient of this action, i.e., the geometric realisation of the simplicial diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{a}\times\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{a}\times\mathbb{A}^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{a}\times\mathbb{A}^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{2}.}

The formal leaf passing through any point
z
is
\mathcal{F}^{\wedge}_{z}\cong\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{a}
. For a generic point
z
the map
\mathcal{F}^{\wedge}_{z}\to\mathbb{A}^{2}
is a formal immersion, but at the origin the map is simply constant.

When
k
has characteristic
p>0
, the foliation gives rise to the free
partition
Lie algebroid on one generator
v
. To integrate it to a formal moduli stack, we replace
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{a}
by

\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{a}^{\sharp}=\mathrm{Spf}(k\langle t\rangle^{\wedge})=\colim_{m}\Spec\big(k\langle t\rangle/(\gamma_{n}(t)\colon n>m)\big),

where
k\langle t\rangle^{\wedge}
denotes the completion of the free divided power algebra on one generator
t
. This time, we set
x(t)=\sum_{n}a_{n}\gamma_{n}(t)
and
y=\sum_{n}b_{n}\gamma_{n}(t)
, and the equations
x\textquoteright(t)=x(t)+y(t)
and
y\textquoteright(t)=y(t)^{2}
become

a_{n+1}=a_{n}+b_{n}\qquad\qquad\qquad b_{n+1}=\sum_{i+j=n}{n\choose i}b_{i}b_{j}.

Solving recursively, we obtain elements in
k[a_{0},b_{0}]\langle t\rangle^{\wedge}
, which give rise to an action
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{a}^{\sharp}\times\mathbb{A}^{2}\to\mathbb{A}^{2}
.
Taking the homotopy quotient of this action as above gives the desired formal moduli stack
\mathbb{A}^{2}\to S
, and the formal leaf passing through any point is
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{a}^{\sharp}
. Note that, in contrast to the characteristic zero case, these leaves are all singular.

Remark 1.2
.

The theory of partition Lie algebroids on
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-schemes is more subtle than that of Lie algebroids on
\mathbb{Q}
-schemes. For example, consider the formal moduli stack
\mathbb{A}^{1}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}/\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
obtained as the quotient of the weight
n
action of the formal multiplicative group
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\to\mathbb{A}^{1};\ (z,x)\mapsto z^{n}x
. Explicitly, this action is given by the ring map

k[x]\to k[x][\![t]\!];\qquad x\mapsto(1+t)^{n}x,

where
z=1+t
. The formal moduli stack
\mathbb{A}^{1}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}/\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
arises as the integration of a partition Lie algebroid with underlying anchor map
\rho\colon\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\to T_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
sending the generator to a multiple of the Euler vector field
n\cdot x\frac{\partial}{\partial x}
. These partition Lie algebroids exhibit the following phenomena that are not present over
\mathbb{Q}
:

(1)

The partition Lie algebroids for the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-actions of weight
n
and weight
nl
are equivalent when
l
is coprime to
p
: they are identified by restricting the action along the étale map
(-)^{l}\colon\mathbb{G}_{m}\to\mathbb{G}_{m}
. However, the partition Lie algebroids of weights
p^{k}
are not equivalent.

(2)

For the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action of weight
p^{k}
, the underlying anchor map
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\to T_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
is zero even though the
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
-action on
\mathbb{A}^{1}
is non-trivial.

The second point shows that it is not possible to obtain the formal moduli stack
\mathbb{A}^{1}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}/\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
as the formal integration of a classical Lie algebroid or
p
-restricted Lie algebroid, as considered in
[
Eke87
]
: since the anchor map is zero, any such integration would come with a retraction to
\mathbb{A}^{1}
.

Formal moduli stacks
X\rightarrow\widehat{S}
also arise in deformation theory, where they capture
infinitesimal derived deformation functors of
X
-indexed families of algebro-geometric objects

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X.}

Our integration equivalence then implies that every such deformation functor is governed by an essentially unique partition Lie algebroid on
X
, generalising various earlier equivalences in characteristic
0
(
[
Dri
,
Del
,
Hin01
,
DAG X
,
Pri10
,
Hen18
,
GR17a
,
Nui19b
]
) and
p
(
[
BM
,
BW20
]
).

Foliations on smooth complex varieties
X
can also be defined as quotient bundles

\Omega_{X}\twoheadrightarrow\Omega_{\mathcal{F}}

inducing maps of commutative differential graded algebras

(\Lambda^{\ast}\Omega_{X},d_{\mathrm{dR}})\rightarrow(\Lambda^{\ast}\Omega_{\mathcal{F}},\overline{d}_{\mathrm{dR}}).

This definition was generalised by Toën–Vezzosi
[
TV23
]
to the non-smooth derived setting – they call the resulting structure an

infinitesimal derived foliation
.
Fu

[
Fu24
]
recently constructed an equivalence between almost perfect
infinitesimal derived foliations and dually almost perfect partition Lie algebroids. Combining Fu’s equivalence with our main result, we deduce that almost perfect infinitesimal derived foliations (in the sense of Toën–Vezzosi) are formally integrable.

1.1.
Statement of results

A Lie algebroid on a smooth manifold
X

consists of an anchor map

\rho:E\rightarrow T_{X}

of vector bundles
together with a bilinear Lie bracket
[-,-]
on the space
\Gamma(X,E)
of global sections such that for all global sections
s_{1},s_{2}\in\Gamma(M,E)
and all smooth functions
f
, we have

[s_{1},fs_{2}]=\rho(s_{1})(f)\cdot s_{2}+f[s_{1},s_{2}].

Lie algebroids were first introduced by Pradines
[
Pra67
]
and have since found numerous applications.

In this article, we will first generalise the theory of Lie algebroids the setting of algebraic geometry over
very general bases,
and then formally integrate them.

Setup.

Let us fix a coherent base ring
R
, i.e. a commutative ring whose finitely generated ideals are also finitely presented.
Let
X
be a quasi-compact quasi-separated (‘qcqs’) derived
R
-scheme which is locally coherent,
meaning that it admits an open cover by affine derived schemes

\Spec(B)
with
\pi_{0}(B)
coherent and
\pi_{i}(B)

finitely presented over
\pi_{0}(B)
.

Every such scheme admits a quasi-coherent cotangent complex
L_{X/R}
and a tangent complex

T_{X/R}=L_{X/R}^{\vee}.

This tangent complex belongs to the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X}=\Ind(\mathrm{Coh}_{X}^{\mathrm{op}})
of pro-coherent sheaves on
X
, which is the natural home of duals of quasi-coherent sheaves.
Pro-coherent sheaves were first introduced by Deligne
[
Har66
, Appendix]
; we refer to
Section

B.2
for further details.

Partition Lie algebroids.

In
Section

3
, we
construct the
\infty
-category of
partition Lie algebroids

\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,X/{R}}

on the scheme
X
. These homotopical objects exhibit many expected properties:

(1)

(Forgetful functor) There is a monadic forgetful functor
\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,X/{R}}\rightarrow(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X})_{/T_{X/R}[1]}
; hence partition Lie algebroids are maps of pro-coherent sheaves

\mathfrak{g}\xrightarrow{\ \ \rho\ \ }T_{X/R}[1]

equipped with additional Lie algebraic structure.

(2)

(Global sections) Taking derived global sections gives rise to a functor

\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,X/{R}}\rightarrow(\mathrm{LieAlg}^{\pi}_{\Delta,{R}})_{/R\Gamma(X,T_{X/R}[1])},

where
\mathrm{LieAlg}^{\pi}_{\Delta,{R}}=\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,\Spec({R})/{R}}
denotes the
\infty
-category of
R
-partition Lie algebras and

R\Gamma(X,T_{X/R}[1])

is the Kodaira–Spencer partition Lie algebra encoding infinitesimal deformations of the
R
-scheme
X
(cf.
Section

4.4
).

(3)

(Descent) Every étale map

Y\rightarrow X

induces a functor
\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,X/{R}}\rightarrow\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,Y/{R}}
lifting the pullback functor on the level of pro-coherent sheaves.
Moreover, there is a canonical equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,X/{R}}\xrightarrow{\ \simeq\ }\lim_{U\subseteq X\text{ affine open}}\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,U/{R}}.

Before proceeding to formal integration, let us highlight various special cases of our construction:

(a)

For
k
a field of characteristic
0
and
X=\Spec(k)
, the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,{X/k}}=\mathrm{LieAlg}^{\pi}_{\Delta,k}
is modelled by (shifted) differential graded Lie algebras over
k
.
For
X=\Spec(A)
an affine
k
-scheme,
\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,{X/k}}
arises from the category of differential graded Lie algebroids on
X
with the tame semi-model structure,
see
[
Nui19a
, Variant 3.12]
.

(b)

For
k
a field of characteristic
p
and
X=\Spec(k)
, we recover the
\infty
-category of partition Lie algebras introduced in
[
BM
]
. These can
be modelled by cosimplicial-simplicial
k
-vector spaces with additional operations parametrised by nested chains of partitions, see
[
BCN
, Definition 5.43]
. For
X=\Spec(F)
with
F/k
a
finite purely inseparable field extension,
\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,{X/k}}
recovers the
\infty
-category of
F/k
-partition Lie algebroids appearing in the purely inseparable Galois correspondence
[
BW20
]
.
For
R=A
complete local Noetherian with residue field
k
and
X=\Spec(k)
, the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,{X/A}}
is equivalent to the mixed partition Lie algebras appearing in
[
BM
, Definition 6.22]
.

(c)

For
R
a coherent ring and
X=\Spec(R)
, the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,{X/R}}=\mathrm{LieAlg}^{\pi}_{\Delta,R}
appears in
[
BCN
, Section 3]
. It admits an explicit model in terms of simplicial-cosimplicial objects, given in
[
BCN
, Theorem 5.42]
.

Formal moduli stacks

Given
X
and
R
as above, we will now introduce
formal moduli stacks

X\rightarrow\widehat{S}

under
X
. Informally, these can be thought of
in at least two different ways:

(1)

as decompositions of
X
into of formal leaves
\mathcal{F}^{\wedge}_{x}
parametrised by the points
x
of
\widehat{S}
;

(2)

as infinitesimal deformation functors of
X
-indexed families of algebro-geometric objects.

To formalise the notion of a formal moduli stack, we will use
animated rings
.

Notation 1.3
.

We write
\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}=\mathscr{P}_{\Sigma}(\mathrm{Poly})
for the
\infty
-category of animated rings, which is obtained by freely adding sifted colimits to the category of finitely generated polynomial rings. Note that
\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}
is also sometimes denoted by
\mathrm{Ani}
,
\mathrm{DAlg}_{\geq 0},

\CAlg^{\Delta}
, or
\mathrm{SCR}
in the literature. The last notation emphasises that animated rings can be modelled by simplicial commutative rings.

Formal moduli stacks will belong to the
\infty
-category of
R
-prestacks

\mathrm{PrStk}_{R}

i.e. the
\infty
-category of (accessible) functors from animated
R
-algebras to spaces. Every derived
R
-scheme
X
gives a prestack
R\mapsto\Map_{\mathrm{Sch}^{\der}}(\Spec(R),X)
, which we
denote by the same name.

Definition 1.4

(Formal moduli stacks)
.

A
formal moduli stack

\mathcal{F}
under an
R
-prestack
X
is a map of
R
-prestacks

X\longrightarrow\widehat{S}

satisfying the following conditions:

(1)

\widehat{S}
has deformation theory, which means that it preserves limits of Postnikov towers and pullbacks along nilpotent extensions of animated
R
-algebras, see
Definition

B.6
;

(2)

X\rightarrow\widehat{S}
is locally almost of finite presentation, see
Definition

B.3
;

(3)

X\rightarrow\widehat{S}
restricts to an equivalence on all reduced affine schemes.

Write
\mathrm{ModuliStk}_{X/R}\subset(\mathrm{PrStk}_{R})_{X/}
for the full subcategory of formal moduli stacks under
X
.

Remark 1.5

(Formal leaves of formal moduli stacks)
.

Given an
R
-point
x
in
X
and a formal moduli stack

X\rightarrow\widehat{S}
, the formal leaf
\mathcal{F}^{\wedge}_{x}
passing through
x
is the formal moduli problem
sending an augmented local Artinian animated
R
-algebra
A
to the space of dotted lifts

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spec(R)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spec(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{S}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{x}}

Remark 1.6

(Deformation functors and formal moduli stacks)
.

Let
B
be a coherent eventually coconnective animated
R
-algebra. Formal deformation functors of (suitably nice)
B
-indexed families of algebro-geometric objects over
R
are then parametrised by functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\colon\mathrm{Art}_{{R}/B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{S}}

from the
\infty
-category

\mathrm{Art}_{{R}/B}\subset\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{{R}/B}

of Artinian extensions of
B
(cf.
Definition

4.1
) to the
\infty
-category
\mathscr{S}
of spaces
satisfying

(1)

F(B)\simeq\ast
;

(2)

F
preserves pullbacks along nilpotent extensions.

In
Section

5
, we show that the
\infty
-category of such functors is equivalent to the
\infty
-category of formal moduli stacks under
X=\Spec(B)
; this statement is essentially contained in
[
GR17b
, 5.1.2]
.
Indeed, if
X\to\widehat{S}
is a formal moduli stack, then the corresponding functor
\mathrm{Art}_{R/B}\to\mathscr{S}
sends an Artinian extension
A\to B
over
R
to the space of dotted lifts in the following diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spec(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spec({R}).}

The main theorem

Given a formal moduli stack
X\rightarrow\widehat{S},

we can consider the sheaf of ‘derived tangent vectors’
to the formal leaves
\mathcal{F}_{x}^{\wedge}
.

To make this idea precise,
we use that the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X}
of pro-coherent sheaves can be identified with the
\infty
-category of functors

\mathrm{Coh}_{X,\geq 0}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}

preserving terminal objects and pullbacks along
\pi_{0}
-surjections. Here
\mathrm{Coh}_{X,\geq 0}
denotes the
\infty
-category of connective coherent sheaves on
X
.

The pro-coherent tangent sheaf

T_{X/\widehat{S}}

then
corresponds to the functor sending
I\in\mathrm{Coh}_{X,\geq 0}
to the space of dotted arrows in the following square:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{sqz}_{X}(I)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{S}.}

Here
\mathrm{sqz}_{X}(I)
denotes the derived scheme
(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}\oplus I)
, where
\mathcal{O}_{X}\oplus I
is the trivial square-zero extension of the structure sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{X}
by
I
. There is a natural map

T_{X/\widehat{S}}\rightarrow T_{X/R},

which captures the derived tangents to the formal leaves. We then prove:

Theorem 1.7

(Main theorem)
.

Let
X
be a locally coherent qcqs derived
{R}
-scheme over a coherent animated ring
R
.
The shifted tangent fibre functor

\mathrm{ModuliStk}_{X/R}\longrightarrow(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X})_{T_{X/R}[1]}

(X\rightarrow Y)\ \ \mapsto\ \ (T_{X/Y}[1]\rightarrow T_{X/R}[1])

lifts to an equivalence of
\infty
-categories

T_{X/-}[1]:\mathrm{ModuliStk}_{X/R}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,X/{R}}.

Hence every partition Lie algebroid on
X
integrates uniquely to a formal moduli
stack under
X
.

This equivalence is functorial with respect to almost finitely presented maps.

A key ingredient in the proof of
Theorem

1.7
is a Koszul equivalence for animated rings. Before stating it, we introduce some notation.

Definition 1.8
.

An animated
R
-algebra
A
over
B
is said to be
complete almost finitely augmented
if

(1)

the induced map
\pi_{0}(A)\rightarrow\pi_{0}(B)
is surjective on
\pi_{0}
with kernel
I
;

(2)

the animated ring
A
is derived
I
-complete;

(3)

the
A
-module
B
is almost perfect as an
A
-module.

Write
\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{aft},\wedge}_{R/B}\subset\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{R/B}
for the full subcategory spanned by those animated
R
-algebras which are complete almost finitely augmented over
B
.

Example 1.9
.

For
R=B
a complete local Noetherian animated ring, we can identify
\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{aft},\wedge}_{R/B}
with the
\infty
-category of augmented animated
R
-algebras which are complete, local, and Noetherian.

In
Section

3
, we lift the assignment
A\mapsto(L_{B/A}^{\vee}[1]\rightarrow L_{B/R}^{\vee}[1])
to a functor

\mathfrak{D}:\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{R/B}\rightarrow\Lie^{\pi}_{\Delta,X/R}

for
X=\Spec(B)
and establish the following result:

Theorem 1.10
.

The functor
\mathfrak{D}
restricts to an equivalence between the full subcategory

\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{aft},\wedge}_{R/B}\subset\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{R/B}

of complete almost finitely augmented objects and the full subcategory

\Lie^{\pi,\dap,\weirdleq 0}_{\Delta,X/R}\subset\Lie^{\pi}_{\Delta,X/R}

of partition Lie algebroids
\mathfrak{g}\rightarrow L_{B/R}^{\vee}[1]
with
\mathfrak{g}
dually almost perfect of Tor-amplitude
\leq 0
.

Theorem

1.7
and
Theorem

1.10
generalise various earlier results:

(a)

Let
k
be a field of characteristic
0
. For
R=k
and
X=\Spec(k)
a point, our results specialise to Lurie–Pridham’s equivalence between formal moduli problems and differential graded Lie algebras
[
DAG X
,
Pri10
]
, which is in turn extends earlier work work by Deligne
[
Del
]
, Drinfel’d
[
Dri
]
, Hinich
[
Hin01
]
, Manetti
[
Man09
]
, and others.

Now assume
A
is a Noetherian and eventually coconnective animated
k
-algebra. For
R=k
and
X=\Spec(A)
, our result is contained in
[
Nui19b
]
, whereas for
R=A
and
X=\Spec(A)
, it was proven by Hennion
[
Hen18
]
.

In characteristic
0
, the monadicity of the tangent fibre functor is also proven in the work of Gaitsgory–Rozenblyum
[
GR17b
, Chapter 8]
. Note, however, that their proof
crucially relies on the fact that looped Lie algebras in characteristic
0
are abelian — this fails away from characteristic
0
due to the presence of power operations.

(b)

Let
k
be a field of characteristic
p
. For
X=\Spec(k)
and
R=k
or, more generally,
R=A
a complete local Noetherian ring with residue field
k
, our results are contained in
[
BM
]
. For
X=\Spec(L)
with
L
a finite purely inseparable field extension over
R=k
, our Koszul statement
Theorem

1.10
recovers
[
BW20
, Theorem 3.15]
.

1.2.
Acknowledgements

We are grateful to Dan Abramovich, Jiaqi Fu, Sofía Marlasca Aparicio, Bertrand Toën, and Nikola Tomić for various discussions related to the content of this paper.

L.B. was supported as a Royal Society University Research Fellow at Oxford University through grant URF
\backslash
R1
\backslash
211075 and by the Centre national de la recherche scientifique (CNRS) at Orsay.
K.M. was supported by NSF grants DMS-2152235 and DMS-21002010. J.N. was supported by the CNRS, under the programme PEPS JCJC, and the ANR project ANR-24-CE40-5367-01 (“LieDG”).

2.
Affine Koszul duality

We write
\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{der}}_{{R}}
for the
\infty
-category of derived commutative rings and

\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{{R}}
for the
\infty
-category of animated
{R}
-algebras; a summary of their theory is given in
Appendix

A
.
Note that animated rings are the same as connective derived rings:

`\text{animated }=\text{ derived }+\text{ connective'}.

The goal of this section will be to provide some results about derived rings that are derived
I
-adically complete for ideals
I
that are of “sufficiently finite type”; most importantly, we prove a comonadicity result (
Theorem

2.25
) generalising results from
[
BM
]
.

We will start by recalling a derived analogue of endowing a ring with the
I
-adic filtration with respect to an ideal
I
. Instead of working directly with ideals
I\subseteq A
, it is more convenient to work with the corresponding closed immersion
A\to A/I=B
. We will refer to objects
A\to A/I=B
as
arrows
and use the notation
(A\rightarrow B)
.

2.1.
Derived filtered and graded rings

To describe the adic filtration associated to an arrow
(A\rightarrow B)
, we first recall how one can obtain the
\infty
-category of derived filtered rings as the
\infty
-category of
\mathbb{L}\mathrm{Sym}
-algebras in the
\infty
-category of filtered modules.

Notation 2.1
.

We will consider the following
\infty
-categories:

(a)

The
\infty
-category
\Mod_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\Delta^{1}}:=\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\Mod_{\mathbb{Z}})
of maps of
\mathbb{Z}
-modules
(X\to Y)
.

(b)

The
\infty
-category
\mathrm{FilMod}_{\mathbb{Z}}=\mathrm{Fun}\big((\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0},\leq)^{\mathrm{op}},\Mod_{\mathbb{Z}}\big)
of non-negatively filtered
\mathbb{Z}
-modules, i.e. diagrams in
\Mod_{\mathbb{Z}}
of the form

\dots\to F^{1}V\to F^{0}V=V.

We will denote objects of
\mathrm{FilMod}_{\mathbb{Z}}
as
F^{\star}V
, and often denote the underlying object
F^{0}V
of the filtration simply as
V
.

(c)

The
\infty
-category
\mathrm{GrMod}_{\mathbb{Z}}=\mathrm{Fun}((\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0},=),\Mod_{\mathbb{Z}})
of non-negatively graded
\mathbb{Z}
-modules. We use the notation
V^{\bullet}
for objects of
\mathrm{GrMod}_{\mathbb{Z}}
to emphasise the grading.

We will be interested in the following three functors relating these
\infty
-categories:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{GrMod}_{\mathbb{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{FilMod}_{\mathbb{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Mod^{\Delta^{1}}_{\mathbb{Z}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Gr}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\aug}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ker}

Here
\mathrm{Gr}^{\bullet}(V)
is the associated graded of a filtered module
F^{\star}V
, i.e.
\mathrm{Gr}^{i}(V)=F^{i}V/F^{i+1}V
. The functor
\aug
sends a filtered module
F^{\star}V
to the natural augmentation
(V\to\mathrm{Gr}^{0}(V))
to its
0
-th graded piece. The functor
\ker
is its
left
adjoint and sends an arrow
(p\colon V\to V_{0})
to the filtered module
\dots\to 0\to 0\to\fib(p)\to V
.

We endow each of these
\infty
-categories with a
t
-structure making it a stably projectively generated
\infty
-category in the sense of
Definition

A.1
, as well as a closed symmetric monoidal structure:

(a)

We endow
\Mod_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\Delta^{1}}
with the
surjective
t
-structure
, in which an object
X\rightarrow Y
is connective if
Y
and
\mathrm{fib}(X\to Y)
(and hence also
X
) are connective
\mathbb{Z}
-modules. In other words,
X\to Y
is connective if
X
and
Y
are connective and
\pi_{0}(X)\to\pi_{0}(Y)
is surjective. An object
X\to Y
is coconnective if
X
and
\mathrm{fib}(X\rightarrow Y)
are coconnective
\mathbb{Z}
-modules. The monoidal structure is the levelwise tensor product

(X\to Y)\otimes(X^{\prime}\to Y^{\prime})=(X\otimes X^{\prime}\to Y\otimes Y^{\prime}).

(b)

We endow
\mathrm{FilMod}_{\mathbb{Z}}
with the Day convolution product and the
t
-structure in which
F^{\star}V
is (co)connective if each
F^{i}V
is (co)connective.

(c)

Likewise,
\mathrm{GrMod}_{\mathbb{Z}}
is equipped with the Day convolution product and the
t
-structure in which
V^{\bullet}
is (co)connective if each
V^{\bullet}
is (co)connective.

The generators of
\Mod_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\Delta^{1}}
are the surjections
\mathbb{Z}^{\oplus m+n}\to\mathbb{Z}^{\oplus n}
and the generators of
\mathrm{FilMod}_{\mathbb{Z}}
and
\mathrm{GrMod}_{\mathbb{Z}}
are the finitely generated free abelian groups, where each generator is of a certain (filtration) weight. Each of these
\infty
-categories is then a derived algebraic context in the sense of
Definition

A.8
(see
[
Rak20
, Section 4.2]
) and hence comes with a notion of derived commutative algebra: these are algebras over the monad obtained by right-left extending the (strict) symmetric algebra monad on the categories of surjections of flat abelian groups and of flat filtered/graded abelian groups.

Definition 2.2
.

We define the
\infty
-categories of
animated
(resp.
derived
)
filtered rings
,
animated
(resp.
derived
)
graded rings
and
animated
(resp.
derived
)
surjections
to be the
\infty
-categories

\displaystyle\mathrm{FilCAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}(\mathrm{FilMod}_{\mathbb{Z},\geq 0})

\displaystyle\mathrm{FilCAlg}^{\der}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{der}}(\mathrm{FilMod}_{\mathbb{Z}})

\displaystyle\mathrm{GrCAlg}^{\an}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}(\mathrm{GrMod}_{\mathbb{Z},\geq 0})

\displaystyle\mathrm{GrCAlg}^{\der}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{der}}(\mathrm{GrMod}_{\mathbb{Z}})

\displaystyle\mathrm{CAlg}^{\an,\Delta^{1}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}(\Mod_{\mathbb{Z},\geq 0}^{\Delta^{1}})

\displaystyle\mathrm{CAlg}^{\der,\Delta^{1}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{der}}(\Mod_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\Delta^{1}})

of animated and derived commutative algebras in the sense of
Definition

A.9
.

Remark 2.3
.

An animated surjection is simply a map of animated rings
A\to B
which is surjective on
\pi_{0}
; these have been introduced by Z. Mao in
[
Mao21
]
, where they are called animated pairs.

Remark 2.4
.

By Remark
A.13
, every derived filtered or graded ring has an underlying
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebra with respect to Day convolution, and modules are modules over this underlying
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebra.

Taking associated graded objects and augmentations commutes with the
\mathbb{L}\mathrm{Sym}
-monads, so that we obtain functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{GrCAlg}^{\der}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{FilCAlg}^{\der}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{CAlg}^{\der,\Delta^{1}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Gr}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\aug}

Both functors preserve limits, colimits and connective objects, and the functor
\aug
sends a filtered algebra
F^{\star}A
to the augmentation arrow
F^{0}A\to\mathrm{Gr}^{0}(A)
.

Definition 2.5
.

The
adic filtration
functor
\mathrm{adic}\colon\mathrm{CAlg}^{\der,\Delta^{1}}\to\mathrm{FilCAlg}^{\der}
is the left adjoint to
\aug
.

Since the augmentation functor is left
t
-exact, taking adic filtrations preserves connective objects. We therefore obtain a diagram of the form

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{GrCAlg}^{\an}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{FilCAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{CAlg}^{\an,\Delta^{1}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Gr}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\aug}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{adic}}

where all functors are left adjoints. Note that taking the associated graded does not preserve limits at the connective level.

We give a familiar description of the adic filtration on the level of
0
-th homotopy groups.

Proposition 2.6
.

Let
(A\to B)
be an animated surjection, and
I=\ker(\pi_{0}A\rightarrow\pi_{0}B)
. Then
\pi_{0}\big(\mathrm{adic}(A\to B)\big)
is given by the commutative algebra in
\mathrm{Fun}((\mathbb{Z},\geq),\Mod_{{R}}^{\heartsuit})

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sym}^{3}_{\pi_{0}(A)}(I)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sym}^{2}_{\pi_{0}(A)}(I)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{0}(A).}

In the situation where
I
is flat as a
\pi_{0}(A)
-module, we have that
\mathrm{Sym}^{n}_{\pi_{0}(A)}(I)\simeq I^{n}
. Each map is then an inclusion and
\pi_{0}\big(\mathrm{adic}(A\to B)\big)
is given by the classical
I
-adic filtration on
\pi_{0}(A)
.

Proof.

We can assume that both
A
and
B
are discrete. The filtered algebra
\pi_{0}\mathrm{adic}(A\to B)
has the universal property that given any discrete filtered algebra
F^{\star}R^{\prime}
, any map
(A\to B)\rightarrow(R^{\prime}\to\mathrm{Gr}^{0}(R^{\prime}))
extends uniquely to a filtered map
\pi_{0}\mathrm{adic}(A\to B)\rightarrow F^{\star}R^{\prime}
. Note that the category of discrete filtered algebras is equivalent to the category of commutative algebras in
\mathrm{FilMod}_{{R}}^{\heartsuit}=\mathrm{Fun}((\mathbb{Z},\geq),\Mod_{{R}}^{\heartsuit})
; in other words,
F^{n+1}R^{\prime}\to F^{n}R^{\prime}
need not be injective.

The data of a map of surjections
(A\to B)\rightarrow(R^{\prime}\to\mathrm{Gr}^{0}(R^{\prime}))
is the same as the data of a map
(A,I)\rightarrow(R^{\prime},F^{1}R^{\prime})
of algebras equipped with an ideal. Let
f_{1}\colon I\rightarrow F^{1}R^{\prime}
be induced map on ideals. This extends uniquely to a map of discrete filtered algebra
\mathrm{Sym}_{A}^{\star}I\to F^{\star}R^{\prime}
, given in weight
n
by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sym}_{A}^{n}(f)\colon\mathrm{Sym}^{n}_{A}(I)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F^{n}R;\qquad f^{\otimes n}(x_{1}\otimes\dots\otimes x_{n})=f_{1}(x_{1})\dots f_{1}(x_{n}),}

where the product is taken inside
F^{\star}R
.
∎

Finally, recall that a filtered module is
complete
if
\lim F^{n}V=0
and that a derived filtered commutative ring is complete if it is complete as a filtered module. Equivalently, a filtered module is complete if it is local with respect to all maps inducing equivalences on the associated graded. Since taking symmetric algebras preserves such graded equivalences, it follows that the completion of a derived filtered ring is again a derived filtered ring. In good situations, one can relate the completeness of a filtered derived algebra
F^{\star}A
to completeness of the underlying algebra
F^{0}A
with respect to its augmentation ideal:

Lemma 2.7
.

Let
A
be an animated filtered ring and let
I
denote the kernel of the augmentation
\pi_{0}(F^{0}A)\to\pi_{0}(\mathrm{Gr}^{0}(A))
. Suppose that
I
is a finitely generated ideal of
\pi_{0}(F^{0}A)
. Then the following hold:

(1)

If
M
is a complete filtered
A
-module, then the underlying
F^{0}A
-module
F^{0}M
is
I
-complete
[
SAG
, Definition 7.3.1.1]
.

(2)

Suppose that
A=\mathrm{adic}(F^{0}A\to\mathrm{Gr}^{0}(A))
is the adic filtration of an animated surjection, and let
A^{\wedge}
be its completion. If
F^{0}A
is
I
-complete and
\mathrm{Gr}^{0}(A)\otimes_{F^{0}A}F^{0}A^{\wedge}\to\mathrm{Gr}^{0}(A)
is an equivalence, then
A\simeq A^{\wedge}
.

The proof of
Lemma

2.7
makes use of the following version of the “derived Nakayama lemma”:

Lemma 2.8
.

Let
A\to B
be a map of connective
\mathbb{E}_{2}
-rings inducing a surjection on
\pi_{0}
and suppose that
I=\ker(\pi_{0}(A)\to\pi_{0}(B))
is a finitely generated ideal. If
M
is a connective
I
-complete
A
-module such that
B\otimes_{A}M\simeq 0
, then
M\simeq 0
.

Proof.

Suppose that there exists a smallest integer
n
such that
\pi_{n}(M)\neq 0
. Then
\pi_{n}(M)
is an
I
-complete
\pi_{0}(A)
-module
[
SAG
, Theorem 7.3.4.1]
such that
\pi_{n}(M)\otimes_{\pi_{0}(A)}\pi_{0}(B)\simeq 0
. But
[
DG02
, Proposition 6.5]
would then imply that
\pi_{n}(M)=0
.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

2.7
.

If
M
is a complete filtered module, then
F^{0}M\simeq\lim F^{0}M/F^{n}M
is a limit of
I
-complete
F^{0}A
-modules: since each
x\in I
is of weight
\geq 1
,
x^{n}\colon F^{0}M/F^{n}M\to F^{0}M/F^{n}M
is null.

For (2), the map
A\to A^{\wedge}
induces an equivalence on the associated graded, so that it suffices to verify that
F^{0}A\to F^{0}A^{\wedge}
is an equivalence. To see this, let us start by observing that since
A
arises as an adic filtration,
Proposition

2.6
implies that

\pi_{0}(F^{0}A^{\wedge})\cong\lim_{n}\mathrm{coker}\big(\pi_{0}(F^{n}A)\to\pi_{0}(F^{0}A)\big)\cong\pi_{0}(F^{0}A)^{\wedge}_{I}

agrees with the (classical)
I
-adic completion of
\pi_{0}(F^{0}A)
. Since
\pi_{0}(F^{0}A)
is
I
-complete, the map
\pi_{0}(F^{0}A)\to\pi_{0}(F^{0}A^{\wedge})
is surjective. Consequently, the kernel
I^{\prime}=\ker\big(\pi_{0}(F^{0}A^{\wedge})\to\pi_{0}(\mathrm{Gr}^{0}(A))\big)
is the image of
I
; in particular, it is finitely generated. It follows from part (1) that
F^{0}A^{\wedge}
is
I^{\prime}
-complete and hence
I
-complete as an
A
-module.
Lemma

2.8
then implies that
F^{0}A\to F^{0}A^{\wedge}
is an equivalence if and only if
\mathrm{Gr}^{0}(A)\to\mathrm{Gr}^{0}(A)\otimes_{F^{0}A}F^{0}A^{\wedge}
, is an equivalence. This map is a section of the natural map
\mathrm{Gr}^{0}(A)\otimes_{F^{0}A}F^{0}A^{\wedge}\to\mathrm{Gr}^{0}(A)
.
∎

2.2.
Cotangent fibre

The usual cotangent complex formalism for derived rings extends in an evident way to the graded/filtered/arrow setting; we refer to Appendix
A.1
for a general discussion. We will be mainly interested in the relative cotangent complexes of augmentation maps:

Definition 2.9

(Cotangent fibre)
.

For every
A
in
\mathrm{GrCAlg}^{\der}
or
\mathrm{FilCAlg}^{\der}
, we define the
cotangent fibre
to be the desuspended relative cotangent complex

\cot(A)=L_{\mathrm{Gr}^{0}(A)/A}[-1].

Here we view
\mathrm{Gr}^{0}(A)
as a derived graded/filtered algebra concentrated in weight
0
. Note that
\cot(A)
is concentrated in weights
\geq 1
, i.e.
\mathrm{Gr}^{0}\big(\cot(A)\big)=0
.

Likewise, for every derived arrow
(A\to B)
we define the
cotangent fibre
to be the desuspended relative cotangent complex

\cot(A\to B)=L_{B/A}[-1].

One can also identify this with the relative cotangent complex of the map of arrows
(A\to B)\to(B\to B)
, which is given by the
(B\to B)
-module
\big(\cot(A\to B),0\big)
.

Note that the cotangent fibre is connective for each animated graded/filtered ring and each animated surjection.

Remark 2.10
.

For any filtered derived ring
A
and any derived arrow
(B^{\prime}\to B)
, there are natural equivalences

\displaystyle\mathrm{Gr}(\cot(A))

\displaystyle\simeq\cot(\mathrm{Gr}(A))

\displaystyle\aug(\cot(A))

\displaystyle\simeq\cot(\aug(A))

\displaystyle F^{\star\leq 1}\cot(B^{\prime}\to B)

\displaystyle\simeq\cot(\mathrm{adic}(B^{\prime}\to B))

where
F^{\star\leq 1}\cot(B^{\prime}\to B)
denotes the
B
-module
\cot(B^{\prime}\to B)
put in filtration weight
1
. The first two equivalences follow from the fact that the functors
\mathrm{Gr}
and
\aug
preserve cotangent complexes (since they commute with taking derived symmetric algebras) and the last equivalence follows by adjunction from the fact that
\aug
preserves square zero extensions.

Lemma 2.11

(
[
BM
, Remark 4.25]
)
.

Let
f\colon A\to B
be a map between derived graded rings. Then the following are equivalent:

(1)

f
is an equivalence.

(2)

A^{0}\to B^{0}
is an equivalence and
\cot(A)\to\cot(B)
is an equivalence of graded modules.

Similarly, a map between complete animated filtered rings
f\colon A\to B
is an equivalence if and only if
\mathrm{Gr}^{0}(A)\to\mathrm{Gr}^{0}(B)
is an equivalence and
\cot(A)\to\cot(B)
induces an equivalence on the associated graded.

Proof.

The complete filtered case follows from the graded case since equivalences between complete derived animated rings are detected on the associated graded and because the associated graded commutes with taking cotangent complexes. For the graded case, suppose that
f
satisfies condition (2) and that there exists a lowest weight
n\geq 1
such that
\cofib(A^{n}\to B^{n})
is not zero. Let
A^{\geq n}
and
B^{\geq n}
denote the weight
\geq n
parts of
A
and
B
, both of which are graded
A
-modules, and consider the natural map

B^{\prime}=A\otimes_{\mathbb{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{A}(A^{\geq n})}\mathbb{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{A}(B^{\geq n})\to B

induced by
f
and the inclusion
B^{\geq n}\to B
. Using that
\mathbb{L}\mathrm{Sym}^{\geq 2}_{A}(B^{\geq n})
and
\mathbb{L}\mathrm{Sym}^{\geq 2}_{A}(B^{\geq n})
are concentrated in weights
\geq 2n
(hence in weights
\geq n+1
), one sees that
B^{\prime}\to B
is an equivalence in weights
\leq n
. It follows that
L_{B/B^{\prime}}
is zero in weights
\leq n
; this can be seen for instance by using the bar resolution to resolve
B
by free
B^{\prime}
-algebras, each of which agrees with
B^{\prime}
in weight
\leq n
. Consequently, in weight
n
we find that
L_{B/A}^{n}\simeq L_{B^{\prime}/A}^{n}\simeq\cofib(A^{n}\to B^{n})
is non-zero. By part (1) of
Lemma

A.21
, this implies that
B^{0}\otimes_{B}L_{B/A}\simeq\cofib(\cot(A)\to\cot(B))
is non-zero, contradicting the assumption that
\cot(A)\simeq\cot(B)
.
∎

Lemma 2.12

(
[
BM
, Proposition 4.26]
)
.

For any derived arrow
(A\to B)
, there is a natural equivalence of derived graded rings

\mathbb{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{B}(\cot(A\to B)(1))\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Gr}(\mathrm{adic}(A\to B))

where
\cot(A\to B)(1)
denotes the cotangent fibre concentrated in weight
1
.

Proof.

For any graded algebra
B^{\bullet}
, the map
\mathbb{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{B^{0}}(B^{1})\to B^{\bullet}
induces an equivalence in degrees
\leq 1
and hence induces a natural equivalence of
B^{0}
-modules
B^{1}\to\cot(B)^{1}
. Since

\cot\big(\mathrm{Gr}(\mathrm{adic}(A\to B))\big)\simeq\cot(A\to B)(1)

is concentrated in weight
1
, we obtain an equivalence
\cot(A\to B)\simeq\mathrm{Gr}(\mathrm{adic}(A\to B))^{1}
of
B
-modules. This induces a map of derived graded rings as in the lemma, which is an equivalence by
Lemma

2.11
.
∎

2.3.
Almost finite type conditions

We will use the cotangent fibre to study finiteness properties of animated (graded or filtered) rings. To this end, let us recall from
Definition

A.18
that a map of animated (graded or filtered) rings
A\to B
is
almost finitely presented
if
B
is an almost compact object in the
\infty
-category of animated (graded or filtered)
A
-algebras.

Lemma 2.13

(
[
BM
, Proposition 5.15]
)
.

The following are equivalent for an animated graded ring
A
:

(1)

\cot(A)
is an almost perfect graded
A^{0}
-module.

(2)

the map of graded algebras
A\to A^{0}
is almost finitely presented.

(3)

the map of graded algebras
A^{0}\to A
is almost finitely presented.

Proof.

It is clear that (3)
\Rightarrow
(2)
\Rightarrow
(1), so assume that
\cot(A)
is almost perfect. We can then define a sequence of animated graded algebras
A^{0}=A_{(0)}\to A_{(1)}\to\dots
with colimit
A
, where

A_{(n)}=A_{(n-1)}\otimes_{\mathbb{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{A^{0}}(A_{(n-1)}^{n})}\mathbb{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{A^{0}}(A^{n})

and
A_{(0)}=A^{0}
. The natural map
A_{(n)}\to A
is an equivalence in weight
\leq n
, so that the colimit is indeed
A
. Furthermore, an inductive argument shows that
\cot(A_{(n)})
is precisely the weight
\leq n
part of
\cot(A)
. Using this, there are equivalences of
A^{0}
-modules concentrated in weight
n

(A/A_{(n-1)})^{n}\simeq L_{A/A_{(n-1)}}^{n}\simeq(A^{0}\otimes_{A}L_{A/A_{(n-1)}})^{n}\simeq\cot(A)^{n}.

Here the first two equivalences use that
A_{(n-1)}\to A
is an equivalence in weight
\leq n-1
. The last equivalence uses the cofibre sequence
\cot(A_{(n-1)})\to\cot(A)\to A^{0}\otimes_{A}L_{A/A_{(n-1)}}
and the fact that
\cot(A_{(n-1)})
coincides with the weight
\leq n-1
part of
\cot(A)
.
By assumption, each
\cot(A)^{n}
is an almost perfect
A^{0}
-module that becomes increasingly connective as
n
tends to
\infty
. It follows that each
A_{(n-1)}\to A_{(n)}
is almost finitely presented, with cofibre becoming increasingly connective as
n
tends to
\infty
. This implies that the colimit
A
is almost finitely presented as well.
∎

Proposition 2.14
.

Let
A\to B
be a map of animated filtered rings inducing a surjection on
\pi_{0}
. Then the following are equivalent:

(1)

B
is an almost finitely presented filtered
A
-algebra.

(2)

L_{B/A}
is an almost perfect filtered
B
-module and the map of filtered algebras
\pi_{0}(A)\to\pi_{0}(B)
is of finite presentation.

(3)

B
is almost perfect as a filtered
A
-module.

The same assertions apply to animated rings and animated graded rings.

Proof.

This is a special case of
Proposition

A.19
.
∎

Definition 2.15
.

We will say that:

(1)

an animated graded algebra
A
is
almost finitely augmented
if it satisfies the equivalent conditions of
Lemma

2.13
.

(2)

an animated filtered algebra
A
is
complete almost finitely augmented
if it is complete and the (filtered) map
A\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr}^{0}(A)
satisfies the equivalent conditions of
Proposition

2.14
.

(3)

an animated surjection
(A\to B)
is
complete almost finitely augmented
if
\mathrm{adic}(A\to B)
is complete almost finitely augmented.

Lemma 2.16
.

Let
A
be a complete filtered animated ring and let
M
be a connective filtered
A
-module. Then
M
is an almost perfect
A
-module if and only if it is complete and
\mathrm{Gr}(M)
is an almost perfect graded
\mathrm{Gr}(A)
-module.

Proof.

This is a special case of
Lemma

A.22
.
∎

We now verify that this agrees with the notions introduced in
[
BM
]
(in the pointed setting):

Lemma 2.17

(
[
BM
, Proposition 5.28]
)
.

Let
A
be a complete animated filtered ring. Then
A
is complete almost finitely augmented if and only if
\mathrm{Gr}(A)
is an almost finitely augmented graded ring.

Proof.

By
Proposition

2.14
,
A
is almost finitely augmented if and only
\mathrm{Gr}^{0}(A)
is almost perfect as a filtered
A
-module. By
Lemma

2.16
, this equivalent to
\mathrm{Gr}^{0}(A)
being an almost perfect graded
\mathrm{Gr}(A)
-module, which in turn is equivalent to
\mathrm{Gr}(A)
being almost finitely augmented by
Proposition

2.14
.

∎

Proposition 2.18
.

Let
(A\to B)
be an animated surjection and let
I=\ker(\pi_{0}(A)\rightarrow\pi_{0}(B))
. Then the following are equivalent:

(1)

The map
(A\to B)
is complete almost finitely augmented.

(2)

The map
(A\to B)
is almost finitely presented and
A
is
I
-complete.

(3)

B
is almost perfect as an
A
-module and
A
is
I
-complete.

Proof.

Write
A_{\mathrm{ad}}=\mathrm{adic}(A\to B)
. Assertions (2) and (3) are equivalent by
Proposition

2.14
. For (1)
\Rightarrow
(2), suppose that the adic filtration
A_{\mathrm{ad}}
is complete almost finitely augmented and note that
A\to B
is equivalent to
F^{0}A_{\mathrm{ad}}\to\mathrm{Gr}^{0}(A_{\mathrm{ad}})
. To see that this map is almost finitely presented, note that
F^{0}\colon\mathrm{FilCAlg}^{\der}_{{R}}\to\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{der}}_{{R}}
preserves almost finitely presented maps. Indeed, its right adjoint, sending a derived algebra
A^{\prime}
to the filtered algebra
\dots\to A^{\prime}\to A^{\prime}\to A^{\prime}
, preserves filtered colimits and
n
-coconnective objects.

Since
A_{\mathrm{ad}}\to\mathrm{Gr}^{0}(A_{\mathrm{ad}})
is almost finitely presented, we conclude that
F^{0}A_{\mathrm{ad}}\to\mathrm{Gr}^{0}(A_{\mathrm{ad}})
is almost finitely presented as well. Finally,
A
is
I
-complete by
Lemma

2.7
.

Let us now assume that condition (2) holds and show that
A_{\mathrm{ad}}
is complete almost finitely augmented. The functor
\mathrm{adic}
preserves almost finitely presented objects, since its right adjoint preserves filtered colimits and sends
n
-coconnective objects to
(n+1)
-coconnective objects. Since the map of animated surjections
(A\to B)\to(B\to B)
is almost finitely presented, we conclude that
A_{\mathrm{ad}}\to\mathrm{Gr}^{0}(A_{\mathrm{ad}})
is almost finitely presented. It follows that
A^{\wedge}_{\mathrm{ad}}
is complete, connective (by the Milnor sequence) and
\mathrm{Gr}(A^{\wedge}_{\mathrm{ad}})\simeq\mathrm{Gr}(A_{\mathrm{ad}})
is almost finitely augmented.
Lemma

2.17
therefore implies that
A^{\wedge}_{\mathrm{ad}}
is almost finitely augmented.

It therefore remains to show that
A_{\mathrm{ad}}\to A^{\wedge}_{\mathrm{ad}}
is an equivalence. By
Lemma

2.7
and the fact that
A_{\mathrm{ad}}
is
I
-complete, it suffices to verify that

f\colon\mathrm{Gr}^{0}(A)\otimes_{A}F^{0}A^{\wedge}_{\mathrm{ad}}\to\mathrm{Gr}^{0}(A)

is an equivalence. This map is an isomorphism on
\pi_{0}
because
\pi_{0}(F^{0}A_{\mathrm{ad}}^{\wedge})\cong\pi_{0}(A)^{\wedge}_{I}
and
\pi_{0}(\mathrm{Gr}^{0}(A))\cong\pi_{0}(A)/I
. It then suffices to verify that its cotangent complex vanishes. Unravelling the definitions, the cotangent complex of
f
is the underlying module of the filtered module
\cofib(\cot(A_{\mathrm{ad}})\to\cot(A^{\wedge}_{\mathrm{ad}}))
, which is indeed null: its associated graded is null and
\cot(A_{\mathrm{ad}})
and
\cot(A^{\wedge}_{\mathrm{ad}})
are both complete, by Remark
2.10
and
Proposition

2.18
(and since almost perfect
\mathrm{Gr}^{0}(A)
-modules are complete).
∎

Corollary 2.19

(
[
BM
, Proposition 5.23]
)
.

Let
A
be an animated filtered ring that is complete almost finitely augmented. Then the induced pair
F^{0}A\to\mathrm{Gr}^{0}A
is complete almost finitely presented.

Proof.

Since the functor
\aug
preserves free algebras, it sends almost finitely presented maps of filtered animated rings to almost finitely presented maps of pairs. In particular,
F^{0}A\to\mathrm{Gr}^{0}A
is almost finitely presented.
Lemma

2.7
implies that
A
is complete with respect to the kernel of
\pi_{0}(F^{0}A)\to\pi_{0}\mathrm{Gr}^{0}(A)
, and we conclude by
Proposition

2.18
.
∎

Lemma 2.20
.

Let
B
be an animated (filtered, graded) ring, let
M
be an almost perfect connective (filtered, graded)
B
-module and let
f\colon B_{\alpha}=B\oplus_{\alpha}M\to B
be a square zero extension of
B
by
M
classified by a map
\alpha\colon L_{B}[-1]\to M
. Then the following assertions hold:

(1)

Let
\mathscr{K}
be the thick subcategory of (filtered, graded)
B_{\alpha}
-modules generated by the
B_{\alpha}
-modules underlying an almost perfect
B
-module. Then
\mathscr{K}=\mathrm{APerf}_{B_{\alpha}}
.

(2)

f
is almost of finite presentation.

Proof.

Assuming (1), we see that
B
is almost perfect as a
B_{\alpha}
-module, so that (2) follows from
Proposition

2.14
. To see that
\mathrm{APerf}_{B_{\alpha}}\subseteq\mathscr{K}
, notice that every almost perfect
B_{\alpha}
-module
N
fits into a cofibre sequence where the outer terms arise from almost perfect
B
-modules

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M\otimes_{B}(B\otimes_{B_{\alpha}}N)\simeq M\otimes_{B_{\alpha}}N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B\otimes_{B_{\alpha}}N.}

To see that
\mathscr{K}\subseteq\mathrm{APerf}_{B_{\alpha}}
, observe that any object
N\in\mathscr{K}
is eventually connective and that
\pi_{k}(N)
is a finitely generated module over
\pi_{0}(B_{\alpha})
if
N
is
k
-connective. Indeed, these properties hold for almost perfect
B
-modules and are stable under (co)fibres and retracts. Consequently, every
k
-connective
N\in\mathscr{K}
fits into a cofibre sequence
B_{\alpha}[k]^{\oplus n}\to N\to N^{\prime}
where
N^{\prime}
is
(k+1)
-connective. Notice that
B_{\alpha}
is contained in
\mathscr{K}
, since it fits into a cofibre sequence
M\to B_{\alpha}\to B
where both
M
and
B
are almost perfect
B
-modules. It follows that
N^{\prime}
defines a
(k+1)
-connective object in
\mathscr{K}
.

Given a module
N\in\mathscr{K}
and
n\geq 0
, we can therefore apply this construction iteratively to obtain a cofibre sequence
K\to N\to N/K
where
K
is a perfect
B_{\alpha}
-module and
N/K
is
n
-connective. This implies that
N
is an almost perfect
B_{\alpha}
-module.
∎

Corollary 2.21
.

Let
B
be an animated ring,
M\in\mathrm{APerf}(B)_{\geq 0}
and
B\oplus_{\alpha}M
a square zero extension of
B
by
M
classified by a map
\alpha\colon L_{B}[-1]\to M
. Then the arrow
(B\oplus_{\alpha}M\to B)
is complete almost finitely augmented.

Proof.

Let
I
be the kernel of
\pi_{0}(B\oplus_{\alpha}M)\to\pi_{0}(B)
. This is a finitely generated ideal, with generators arising from the generators of
\pi_{0}(M)
.
By
Proposition

2.18
, it suffices to prove that
B\oplus_{\alpha}M\to B
is almost of finite presentation and that
B\oplus_{\alpha}M
is
I
-complete. The first condition follows from
Lemma

2.20
and the second condition is immediate since
I^{2}=0
.
∎

Corollary 2.22
.

Let
(A\to B)
be an animated arrow which is complete almost finitely presented. For any almost perfect connective
A
-module
M
and any square zero extension of
A
by
M
, the arrow
(A\oplus_{\alpha}M\to B)
is again complete almost finitely presented.

Proof.

We apply
Proposition

2.18
. As the composite
A\oplus_{\alpha}M\to A\to B
of two maps that are almost finitely presented,
A\oplus_{\alpha}M\to B
is almost finitely presented. Let
I
be the kernel of
\pi_{0}(A\oplus_{\alpha}M)\to\pi_{0}(B)
. This is generated by finitely many generators
x_{i}
of
\pi_{0}(M)
, which square to zero, and finitely many elements
y_{i}
that lift the generators of
\ker(\pi_{0}(A)\to\pi_{0}(B))
. Then
A\oplus_{\alpha}M
is evidently
(x_{i})
-complete, and it is
(y_{i})
-complete since it is an extension of
A
by
M
, both of which are
(y_{i})
-complete. We conclude that
A\oplus_{\alpha}M
is
I
-complete.
∎

2.4.
Comonadicity of the cotangent fibre

Throughout this section, we fix a base animated ring
{R}
. For each animated surjection
(A\to B)
of
{R}
-algebras, the cotangent fibre
\cot(A\to B)=L_{B/A}[-1]
is a connective
B
-module that comes equipped with a natural map

L_{B/{R}}[-1]\to\cot(A\to B).

The goal of this section will be to show that when
(A\to B)
is complete almost finitely augmented, its structure can be completely encoded in terms of its cotangent fibre.

Notation 2.23
.

Let
B
be an animated
{R}
-algebra. We will write

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\wedge\mathrm{aft}}_{{R}/B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{{R}/B}}

for the full subcategory of connective derived
{R}
-algebras
A
endowed with a map
A\to B
that exhibits
A
as complete almost finitely augmented (
Definition

2.15
).

Definition 2.24
.

Let
B
be a derived
{R}
-algebra. We define a
derivation

(M,\alpha)
on
B
to be the datum of a
B
-module
M
, together with a map
\alpha\colon L_{B/{R}}[-1]\to M
. Write

\mathrm{Der}_{B/{R}}=(\Mod_{B})_{L_{B/{R}}[-1]/}

for the
\infty
-category of derivations
(M,\alpha)
on
B
. If
B
is connective, let
\mathrm{Der}_{B/{R},\geq 0}^{\mathrm{aperf}}\subseteq\mathrm{Der}_{B/{R}}
denote the full subcategory spanned by the derivations
(M,\alpha)
, where
M
is an almost perfect connective.

Our goal will then be the following:

Theorem 2.25
.

Let
B
be an animated
{R}
-algebra. Then the the cotangent fibre defines the left adjoint in a comonadic adjunction

(1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cot\colon\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\wedge\mathrm{aft}}_{{R}/B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Der}_{B/{R},\geq 0}^{\mathrm{aperf}}\colon\mathrm{sqz}.}

Let us first construct the desired adjoint pair. In fact, for later purposes we will construct a version of this adjunction that also encodes naturality in the animated
{R}
-algebra
B
.

Construction 2.26
.

Let
\Mod
be the
\infty
-category of pairs
(B,M)
consisting of a derived
{R}
-algebra and a
B
-module (cf. Construction
A.17
). We then have a commuting diagram as follows:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{der},\Delta^{1}}_{{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Mod\times_{\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{der}}}\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{der}}_{{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{der}}_{{R}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{ev}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{triv}}

Here the two downwards pointing functors send a map of derived rings
A\to B
, resp. a derived ring with a module
(B,M)
, to
B
; both functors are cocartesian and cartesian fibrations. The pair
(L,\triv)
is the relative adjunction given by the cotangent complex and trivial square zero extension functors

L(A\to B)=\big(B,L_{A/{R}}\otimes_{A}B\big)\qquad\qquad\triv(B,M)=(B\oplus M\to B).

We abuse notation and also write
L\colon\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{der}}_{{R}}\to\Mod
for the functor sending
A\mapsto(A,L_{A/{R}})
. We then define the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{Der}_{/{R}}
of
derivations
to be the fibre product

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Der}_{/{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}([1],\Mod)\times_{\mathrm{Fun}([1],\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{der}})}\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{der}}_{{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{der}}_{{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Mod\times_{\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{der}}}\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{der}}_{{R}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{ev}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L[-1]}

An object of
\mathrm{Der}_{/{R}}
is given by a tuple
\big(B,M,\alpha\big)
consisting of a derived ring
B
and a derivation on
B
, i.e. a
B
-module
M
and a map
\alpha\colon L_{B/{R}}[-1]\to M
. By adjunction, one can also identify this with a tuple
(B,M,\alpha)
of a derived ring, a module and a map of derived pairs
\alpha\colon(B,B)\to\big(B\oplus M[1],B)
which is the identity on the codomains.

Remark 2.27
.

The projection
\mathrm{Der}_{/{R}}\to\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{der}}_{{R}};\ (B,M,\alpha)\longmapsto B
is a cartesian fibration by construction, classifying the functor

B\longmapsto\mathrm{Der}_{B/{R}}.

Each map
f\colon B\to B^{\prime}
induces a functor
f_{*}\colon\mathrm{Der}_{B^{\prime}/{R}}\to\mathrm{Der}_{B/{R}}
by restriction of scalars, sending a derivation
(N,\beta)
to
f_{*}(N,\beta)=(f_{*}N,\beta^{\prime})
with

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta^{\prime}\colon L_{B/{R}}[-1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}L_{B^{\prime}/{R}}[-1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}N}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}\beta}

This admits a left adjoint, sending a derivation
(M,\alpha)
to the derivation computed as the pushout of the diagram of
B^{\prime}
-modules

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{B^{\prime}/{R}}[-1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}L_{B/{R}}[-1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}M.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}\alpha}

Consequently, the projection
\mathrm{Der}_{/{R}}\to\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{der}}_{{R}}
is a cocartesian fibration as well.

Remark 2.28
.

Let
f\colon B\to B^{\prime}
be a map of derived rings with
L_{B^{\prime}/B}\simeq 0
. Then the induced adjoint pair from Remark
2.27
has a simpler description: the left adjoint sends
(M,\alpha)
to the derivation
(f^{*}M,f^{*}\alpha)
, with
f^{*}\alpha\colon L_{B^{\prime}/{R}}[-1]\simeq f^{*}L_{B/{R}}[-1]\to f^{*}M
. In other words, the adjoint pair is simply given by the functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}\colon\mathrm{Der}_{B/{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Der}_{B^{\prime}/{R}}\colon f_{*}}

taking base change and direct image at the level of modules.

Definition 2.29
.

We will write

(2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{der},\Delta^{1}}_{{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Der}_{/{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{der}}_{{R}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{ev}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{sqz}}

for the relative adjunction defined as follows:

(1)

The right adjoint
\mathrm{sqz}
sends a derivation
\big(B,M,\alpha\big)
to the square zero extension
B\oplus_{\alpha}M\to B
classified by
\alpha
. More precisely, this is given by the fibre product in the
\infty
-category of maps of derived
{R}
-algebras

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[B\oplus_{\alpha}M\to B]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[B\to B]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[B\to B]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[B\oplus M[1]\to B]}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}

(2)

The left adjoint
\cot
is given by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[A\to B]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(B,\ L_{B/{R}}[-1]\to L_{B/A}[-1]\big).}

Proof of
Theorem

2.25
.

The proof is essentially the same as in
[
BM
]
, although one cannot apply the axiomatic argument from loc. cit. (nor the modification in Section 6) stricto sensu. To start, notice that Corollary
2.21
implies that for each animated
{R}
-algebra
B
, the adjoint pair (
2
) restricts to an adjoint pair
\cot\colon\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\wedge\mathrm{aft}}_{{R}/B}\leftrightarrows\mathrm{Der}_{B/{R},\geq 0}^{\mathrm{aperf}}\colon\mathrm{sqz}
. We have to verify that this adjoint pair is comonadic. To see that
\cot
is conservative, let
(A\to B)\to(A^{\prime}\to B)
be a map in
\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\wedge\mathrm{aft}}_{{R}/B}
that induces an equivalence on cotangent fibres. The induced map
\mathrm{adic}(A\to B)\to\mathrm{adic}(A^{\prime}\to B)
then induces an equivalence on the associated graded by
Lemma

2.12
. Since the adic filtrations are complete by definition, this implies that
A\to A^{\prime}
is an equivalence.

Next, let
A^{\bullet}
be a cosimplicial diagram in
\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\wedge\mathrm{aft}}_{{R}/B}
such that
\cot(A^{\bullet})
admits a splitting in
\mathrm{Der}_{B/{R},\geq 0}^{\mathrm{aperf}}
. Let
\Tot(A^{\bullet})
be the totalisation of
A^{\bullet}
, computed in the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{der}}_{{R}/B}
of (not necessarily connective) derived
{R}
-algebras augmented over
B
. We need to show that
\Tot(A^{\bullet})
is complete almost finitely presented and that the natural map
\cot(\Tot(A^{\bullet}))\to\Tot(\cot(A^{\bullet}))
is an equivalence.

To see this, let
A^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{ad}}=\mathrm{adic}(A^{\bullet}\to B)
denote the associated diagram of complete almost finitely presented derived filtered algebras. Recall that there are functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{GrCAlg}^{\der}_{{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{FilCAlg}^{\der}_{{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{CAlg}^{\der,\Delta^{1}}_{{R}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\aug}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Gr}}

preserving both limits (computed in unbounded
{R}
-modules) and colimits, and commuting with the functors taking cotangent fibres. In particular,
\aug(\Tot(A^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{ad}}))\simeq\Tot(\aug(A^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{ad}}))\simeq\Tot(A^{\bullet})
and there is a commuting square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\aug\big(\cot(\Tot(A^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{ad}}))\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\aug\big(\Tot(\cot(A^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{ad}}))\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cot(\Tot(A^{\bullet}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Tot(\cot(A^{\bullet})).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

It therefore suffices to verify that:

(a)

the derived filtered ring
\Tot(A^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{ad}})
is complete almost finitely augmented (hence connective).

(b)

the filtered cotangent fibre preserves this totalisation (in unbounded filtered modules).

For (a), note that
\Tot(A^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{ad}})
is complete as a limit of complete algebras. Its associated graded is

\mathrm{Gr}(\Tot(A^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{ad}}))\simeq\Tot\big(\mathrm{Gr}(A^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{ad}})\big)\simeq\Tot\big[\mathbb{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{B}\big(\cot(A^{\bullet}\to B)\big)\big]\simeq\mathbb{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{B}\big(\Tot[\cot(A^{\bullet}\to B)]\big).

Here the second equivalence is
Lemma

2.12
and the last equivalence uses that the augmented cosimplicial diagram
\Tot[\cot(A^{\bullet}\to B)]\to\cot(A^{\bullet}\to B)
admits a splitting, so that
\mathbb{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{B}
(or any other functor) preserves this totalisation. Since
\Tot[\cot(A^{\bullet}\to B)]
is almost perfect by assumption, we conclude that
\mathrm{Gr}\big(\Tot(A^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{ad}})\big)
is an almost finitely augmented graded algebra (in particular connective). It follows that the complete derived filtered algebra
\Tot(A^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{ad}})
is connective as well, and
Lemma

2.17
implies that it is complete almost finitely augmented.

For (b), note that there is a commuting square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Gr}\big(\cot(\Tot(A^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{ad}}))\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Gr}\big(\Tot(\cot(A^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{ad}}))\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cot\big(\Tot[\mathrm{Gr}(A^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{ad}})]\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Tot\big[\cot(\mathrm{Gr}(A^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{ad}}))\big]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

since taking the associated graded commutes with totalisations (in the unbounded setting) and with cotangent complexes. Since the cosimplicial diagram
\mathrm{Gr}(A^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{ad}})\simeq\mathbb{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{B}\big(\cot(A^{\bullet}\to B)\big)
has a
split
totalisation (preserved by any functor), the bottom map is an equivalence. Consequently, the map

(3)

\cot(\Tot(A^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{ad}}))\to\Tot(\cot(A^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{ad}}))

induces an equivalence on the associated graded. The domain is an almost perfect connective filtered module: this follows from
Proposition

2.14
, since
\Tot(A^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{ad}})
is complete almost finitely presented by part (a). In particular,
\cot(\Tot(A^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{ad}}))
is a complete filtered module. The codomain of the map (
3
) is a limit of complete (in fact, almost perfect connective) filtered
B
-modules and is therefore complete itself. We conclude that (
3
) is an equivalence, as desired.
∎

In particular,
Theorem

2.25
shows that for every animated surjection
(A\to B)
which is complete almost finitely augmented, there exists a cosimplicial diagram of square zero extensions of
B
whose totalisation is equivalent to
A
: this is simply the comonadic cobar resolution of
A
. For later purposes, we record the following variant of this construction:

Corollary 2.30
.

Let
A\to B\in\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\wedge,\mathrm{aft}}_{{R}/B}
and let
M\in\mathrm{APerf}_{B,\geq 0}
. For any map
\alpha\colon A\to B\oplus M
of animated
{R}
-algebras augmented over
B
, there exists a natural coaugmented cosimplicial diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{\bullet}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B\oplus M}

in
(\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\wedge,\mathrm{aft}}_{{R}/B})_{/B\oplus M}
with the following properties:

(1)

For each
i
, the map
A^{i}\to B\oplus M
is equivalent to
\mathrm{sqz}(M^{i},\alpha^{i})\to\mathrm{sqz}(M,0)
for a map
(M^{i},\alpha^{i})\to(M,0)
in
\mathrm{Der}_{B/{R},\geq 0}^{\mathrm{aperf}}
.

(2)

The augmented cosimplicial diagram
\cot(A)\to\cot(A^{\bullet})
in
\mathrm{Der}_{B/{R},\geq 0}^{\mathrm{aperf}}
is split.

(3)

A\simeq\Tot(A^{\bullet})
.

Proof.

Suppose that
U\colon\mathscr{D}\leftrightarrows\mathscr{C}\colon R
is a comonadic adjunction. For every object
c\in\mathscr{C}
, this induces an adjoint pair
U\colon\mathscr{D}_{/R(c)}\leftrightarrows\mathscr{C}_{/c}\colon C
on over-categories, which is comonadic as well. It follows that every object
\alpha\colon d\to R(c)
in
\mathscr{D}_{/R(c)}
admits a comonadic cobar resolution of the form

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(RU)^{\bullet}(d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R(c).}

Here each
\alpha^{n}\colon(RU)^{n}(d)\to R(c)
is the image under
R
of the map
U(RU)^{n-1}(d)\to UR(c)\to c
, where the first map is the image of
\alpha^{n-1}
under the forgetful functor
U
. This has the property that the augmented cosimplicial object
U(d)\to U(RU)^{\bullet}(d)
in
\mathscr{C}_{/c}
is split and (hence) that
d\simeq\Tot((RU)^{\bullet}(d))
. We now apply this to the situation where
(U,R)=(\cot,\mathrm{sqz})
is the comonadic adjunction (
1
),
c
is the derivation
(M,0)\in\mathrm{Der}_{B/{R},\geq 0}^{\mathrm{aperf}}
and
d\to R(c)
is the map
\alpha\colon A\to B\oplus M
.
∎

3.
Partition Lie algebroids

Recall that a
Lie algebroid
over a smooth scheme
Z
over a field
k
is a quasi-coherent sheaf
\mathfrak{g}\in\mathrm{QC}^{\heartsuit}_{Z}
equipped with an
\mathcal{O}_{Z}
-linear anchor map
\rho\colon\mathfrak{g}\to T_{Z/{R}}
to the tangent sheaf and a
k
-linear Lie bracket
[-,-]\colon\mathfrak{g}\otimes_{k}\mathfrak{g}\to\mathfrak{g}
satisfying the Leibniz rule. The goal of this section will be to introduce a derived (and shifted) variant of such Lie algebroids over a qcqs locally coherent derived
{R}
-scheme
X
, which we will refer to as
partition Lie algebroids
. More precisely, we will define a partition Lie algebroid to be given by a map
\rho\colon\mathfrak{g}\to T_{X/{R}}[1]
in the
\infty
-category

\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X}=\text{Ind}(\mathrm{Coh}_{X}^{\mathrm{op}})

of pro-coherent sheaves on
X
, together with the structure of an algebra over a certain monad

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lie^{\pi}_{\Delta,X/{R}}\colon\big(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X}\big)_{/T_{X/{R}}[1]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X}\big)_{/T_{X/{R}}[1]}.}

Here
T_{X/{R}}=L_{X/{R}}^{\vee}
denotes the pro-coherent dual of the cotangent sheaf of
X
(Example
B.27
). One can therefore think of
\rho\colon\mathfrak{g}\to T_{X/{R}}[1]
as the
anchor map
underlying the partition Lie algebroid.

Our construction of the monad
\Lie^{\pi}_{\Delta,X/{R}}
will be based on two ingredients:

(1)

Let
\mathrm{Der}_{X/{R},\geq 0}^{\mathrm{aperf}}
denote the
\infty
-category of derivations
\alpha\colon L_{X/{R}}[-1]\to M
with values in a connective, almost perfect quasi-coherent sheaf on
X
. We then consider the comonad

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coLie^{\pi}_{\Delta,X/{R}}\colon\mathrm{Der}_{X/{R},\geq 0}^{\mathrm{aperf}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Der}_{X/{R},\geq 0}^{\mathrm{aperf}};\quad(M,\alpha)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cot\big(\mathcal{O}_{X}\oplus_{\alpha}M\big)}

sending each derivation
\alpha\colon L_{X/{R}}[-1]\to M
to the cotangent fibre of the square zero extension
\mathcal{O}_{X}\oplus_{\alpha}M
.

(2)

Taking pro-coherent duals determines a fully faithful functor (
Lemma

A.34
)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\vee}\colon\mathrm{Der}_{X/{R},\geq 0}^{\mathrm{aperf},\mathrm{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X}\big)_{/T_{X/{R}}[1]}.}

Its essential image consists of maps
F\to T_{X/{R}}[1]
where
F
is dually almost perfect (
Definition

A.33
) and of non-positive tor-amplitude (
Definition

A.35
).

Our main aim will then be to prove the following:

Theorem 3.1
.

Let
X
be a locally coherent qcqs derived
{R}
-scheme. There exists a unique extension

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Der}_{X/{R},\geq 0}^{\mathrm{aperf},\mathrm{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Der}_{X/{R},\geq 0}^{\mathrm{aperf},\mathrm{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X}\big)_{/T_{X/{R}}[1]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X}\big)_{/T_{X/{R}}[1]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\coLie^{\pi}_{\Delta,X/{R}})^{\mathrm{op}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\vee}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\vee}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lie^{\pi}_{\Delta,X/{R}}}

to a monad
\Lie^{\pi}_{\Delta,X/{R}}
that preserves sifted colimits.

Definition 3.2
.

We will refer to the monad
\Lie^{\pi}_{\Delta,X/{R}}
of
Theorem

3.1
as the
partition Lie algebroid monad
and define the
\infty
-category of
partition Lie algebroids

\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,X/{R}}=\Alg_{\Lie^{\pi}_{\Delta,X/{R}}}\Big(\big(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X}\big)_{/T_{X/{R}}[1]}\Big)

to be its
\infty
-category of algebras.

We will prove the affine case of
Theorem

3.1
in Section
3.1
. In Section
3.2
we will study the naturality of the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}}
in the coherent animated
{R}
-algebra
B
. More precisely, we will prove the following in
Proposition

3.25
:

(a)

For each map
f\colon B\to B^{\prime}
of that is almost finitely presented, there exists a functor

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\sharp}\colon\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,B^{\prime}/{R}}.}

(b)

When
f
is étale, the functor
f^{\sharp}=f^{*}
is given by the usual inverse image at the level of the underlying pro-coherent module, and admits a right adjoint.

We will then use this in Section
3.3
to prove
Theorem

3.1
by a descent argument.

3.1.
Partition Lie algebroids on affine schemes

Let
B
be an animated
{R}
-algebra and recall the adjoint pair from
Definition

2.29

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cot\colon\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{der}}_{{R}/B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Der}_{B/{R}}\penalty\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\mskip 2.0mu\mathrm{sqz}.}

Here
\mathrm{Der}_{B/{R}}
is the
\infty
-category of derivations
(M,\alpha)=(M,\alpha\colon L_{B/{R}}[-1]\to M)
on
B
(
Definition

2.24
).

Notation 3.3
.

Let us write
\coLie^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}}:=\cot\circ\mathrm{sqz}
for the composite comonad

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coLie^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}}=\cot\circ\mathrm{sqz}\colon\mathrm{Der}_{B/{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Der}_{B/{R}}.}

Remark 3.4
.

Given a derivation
(M,\alpha)
, one can endow
\coLie^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}}(M,\alpha)
with a natural decreasing filtration, as follows. Consider
L_{B/{R}}[-1]
as a filtered module in weight
0
(i.e.
F^{1}=0
) and consider
M
as a filtered module in weight
1
, i.e.,
F^{2}M=0
and
F^{1}M=M
. The derivation
\alpha
then lifts to a derivation
\alpha\colon L_{B/{R}}[-1]\to F^{\star}M
and we obtain a filtration

F^{\star}\coLie^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}}(M,\alpha)=\cot(B\oplus_{\alpha}F^{\star}M).

Note that
L_{B/{R}}[-1]\to F^{\star}M
is null on the associated graded, so that the associated graded of this filtration is equivalent to
\coLie^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}}(M,0)
.

We will use the following notation throughout this paper:

Definition 3.5
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be an
\infty
-category such that the inclusion
\tau_{\leq n}\mathscr{C}\subseteq\mathscr{C}
of its
n
-truncated objects admits a left adjoint
\tau_{\leq n}
for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
. We will say that a tower

\dots\to X_{1}\to X

is
almost eventually constant
if for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists an
m
such that

\tau_{\leq n}X_{i+1}\to\tau_{\leq n}X_{i}

is an equivalence for all
i\geq m
.

Lemma 3.6
.

The comonad
\coLie^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}}\colon\mathrm{Der}_{B/{R}}\to\mathrm{Der}_{B/{R}}
has the following properties:

(1)

It preserves derivations
(M,\alpha)
where
M
is an almost perfect connective
B
-module.

(2)

It preserves sifted colimits.

(3)

For each finite cosimplicial diagram
(M^{\bullet},\alpha^{\bullet})
in
\mathrm{Der}_{B/{R}}
such that
\Tot(M^{\bullet})
and all
M^{t}
are in
\mathrm{APerf}_{B,\geq 0}
, there is a natural equivalence

\coLie^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}}\big(\Tot(M^{\bullet},\alpha^{\bullet})\big)\simeq\Tot\big(\coLie^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}}(M^{\bullet},\alpha^{\bullet})\big).

(4)

For any tower
\dots\to(M_{2},\alpha_{2})\to(M_{1},\alpha_{1})
in
\mathrm{Der}_{B/{R}}
such that

\dots\to M_{2}\to M_{1}
is eventually constant (i.e. the connectivity of
\fib(M_{n+1}\to M_{n})
tends to
\infty
), there is an equivalence

\coLie^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}}(\lim_{n}(M_{n},\alpha_{n}))\simeq\lim_{n}\big(\coLie^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}}(M_{n},\alpha_{n})\big).

Proof.

Property (1) follows from
Lemma

2.20
and (2) follows from the fact that
\cot
and
\mathrm{sqz}
both preserve sifted colimits. For (3), we use the filtration from Remark
3.4
. Since limits in
\mathrm{Der}_{B/{R}}
are computed on the underlying
B
-modules, it then suffices to show that the natural map

\mu\colon\cot\big(\Tot(B\oplus_{\alpha^{\bullet}}F^{\star}M^{\bullet})\big)\to\Tot\big(\cot(B\oplus_{\alpha^{\bullet}}F^{\star}M^{\bullet})\big)

is an equivalence of filtered modules.
Proposition

2.14
and
Lemma

2.20
imply that
\cot\big(B\oplus_{\alpha}F^{\star}M\big)
is an almost perfect filtered
B
-module whenever
M
is almost perfect. In particular, the domain and codomain of
\mu
are complete, and it remains to verify that the induced map on the associated graded is an equivalence. As the maps
\alpha^{\bullet}
are null on the associated graded, it will be enough to show that the functor

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\colon\Mod_{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{GrMod}_{B}^{\geq 1};\quad M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cot\big(B\oplus M(1)\big)}

preserves finite totalisations, where
\mathrm{GrMod}_{B}^{\geq 1}
is the
\infty
-category of
strictly
positively graded
B
-modules. Using the bar resolution, the functor
F
can be obtained via colimits and compositions from the functors
\mathbb{L}\mathrm{Sym}^{n}_{B}\colon\mathrm{GrMod}_{B}^{\geq 1}\to\mathrm{GrMod}_{B}^{\geq 1}
. These are
n
-excisive and hence preserve finite totalisations
[
BM
, Proposition 3.37]
. Since the functors preserving finite totalisations are stable under composition and colimits, the result follows.

For (4), note that if
\fib(M_{n+1}\to M_{n})
is
i
-connective, then
B\oplus_{\alpha_{n+1}}M_{n+1}\to B\oplus_{\alpha_{n}}M_{n}
has
i
-connective fibres. Consequently, its relative cotangent complex is
(i+1)
-connective
[
SAG
, Corollary 25.3.6.4]
. Taking cotangent fibres, one finds that
\coLie^{\pi}_{\Delta}(M_{n+1})\to\coLie^{\pi}_{\Delta}(M_{n})
has
i
-connective fibres. The tower therefore stabilises after
k
-truncation for any
k
, from which the result follows.
∎

Let us now suppose that
B
is a coherent animated
{R}
-algebra and consider the dual picture, using linear duality in the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{B}=\mathrm{Ind}(\mathrm{Coh}_{B}^{\mathrm{op}})
of pro-coherent
B
-modules. We refer to Appendix
B
for the theory of pro-coherent sheaves and just recall that there are left adjoint functors (cf. Observation
A.30
and
Definition

A.31
)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\upiota\colon\Mod_{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\vee}\colon\Mod_{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{QC}^{\vee,\mathrm{op}}_{B}.}

The first functor is fully faithful on eventually connective
B
-modules and the second is fully faithful on almost perfect
B
-modules. We will write
\mathrm{dAPerf}_{B}\subseteq\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{B}
for the full subcategory of
dually almost perfect
pro-coherent
B
-modules (
Definition

A.33
). Explicitly, one can identify
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{B}
with the
\infty
-category of exact functors
F\colon\mathrm{Coh}_{B}\to\mathrm{Sp}
. The pro-coherent module
\upiota(M)
then sends
K\mapsto K\otimes_{B}M
and the pro-coherent dual
M^{\vee}
sends
K\mapsto\mathrm{Hom}_{B}(M,K)
.

Notation 3.7
.

Given a coherent animated
{R}
-algebra
B\in\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{{R}}
, we will write
T_{B/{R}}=L_{B/{R}}^{\vee}
for the pro-coherent dual of the cotangent complex and refer to it as the (pro-coherent)
tangent complex
.

By
Lemma

A.34
, pro-coherent duality determines an equivalence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\vee}\colon\mathrm{Der}_{B/{R}}^{\mathrm{aperf},\mathrm{op}}=\big((\mathrm{APerf}_{B})_{L_{B/{R}}[-1]/}\big)^{\mathrm{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(\mathrm{dAPerf}_{B}\big)_{/T_{B/{R}}[1]}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

Using this, we construct the partition Lie algebroid monad in the affine setting:

Proposition 3.8
.

Let
B
be a coherent animated
{R}
-algebra. Then there exists a unique extension

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Der}_{B/{R},\geq 0}^{\mathrm{aperf},\mathrm{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Der}_{B/{R},\geq 0}^{\mathrm{aperf},\mathrm{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{B}\big)_{/T_{B/{R}}[1]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{B}\big)_{/T_{B/{R}}[1]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\coLie^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}})^{\mathrm{op}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\vee}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\vee}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lie^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}}}

to a monad
\Lie^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}}
that preserves sifted colimits.

Definition 3.9
.

Following
Definition

3.2
, we will write
\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}}
for the
\infty
-category of partition Lie algebroids over
B
, that is, algebras over the monad
\Lie^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}}
.

Before turning to the proof of
Proposition

3.8
, we record some first consequences of the above definition.

Example 3.10
.

If
B={R}
is a coherent animated ring, then the monad
\Lie^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/B}
coincides with the partition Lie algebra monad from
[
BM
,
BCN
]
, so that
\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/B}\simeq\mathrm{LieAlg}^{\pi}_{\Delta,B}
is equivalent to the
\infty
-category of partition Lie algebras over
B
. In particular, the free partition Lie algebra on a dually almost perfect
B
-module can be computed explicitly in terms of the partition complex, using the derived functor of
\Sigma_{r}
-invariants (see
[
BCN
, Section 3.6]
)

\Lie^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/B}(M)\simeq\sum_{r}\big(\widetilde{C}^{*}(\Sigma|\Pi_{r}|^{\diamond},B)\otimes_{B}M^{\otimes r}\big)^{\Sigma_{r}}.

Remark 3.11
.

For a coherent animated
{R}
-algebra
B
, the free partition Lie algebroid
\Lie^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}}(M,\alpha)
can be viewed as a deformation of the free
B
-linear partition Lie algebra on
M
. More precisely,
\Lie^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}}(M,\alpha)
