admits a natural exhaustive increasing filtration whose associated graded is equivalent to the free partition Lie algebra
\Lie^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}}(M,0)\simeq\Lie^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/B}(M)
. Indeed, Remark
3.4
and
Lemma

A.22
provide such a filtration when
M
is the pro-coherent dual of a connective almost perfect
B
-module; one then extends by sifted colimits. The resulting filtration is analogous to the filtration on free Lie algebroids constructed in
[
Kap07
]
.

Proposition 3.12
.

Let
B
be a coherent animated
{R}
-algebra. Then the following hold:

(1)

There is an equivalence
(\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}})_{/0}\simeq\mathrm{LieAlg}^{\pi}_{\Delta,B}
.

(2)

Taking the fibre of the anchor map defines a sifted colimit preserving right adjoint functor
\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}}\to\mathrm{LieAlg}^{\pi}_{\Delta,B}
.

It follows that one can identify
\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}}
with the
\infty
-category of algebras over a monad on
\mathrm{LieAlg}^{\pi}_{\Delta,B}
whose underlying endofunctor sends
\mathfrak{g}
to
\mathfrak{g}\times T_{B/{R}}
.

Proof.

For (1), note that the forgetful functor
(\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}})_{/0}\to\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{B}
exhibits
(\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}})_{/0}
as the
\infty
-category of algebras over a sifted colimit preserving monad on
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{B}
. Unravelling the definitions, this monad coincides with
\Lie^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/B}
, so that the result follows from Example
3.10
. Part (2) follows from part (1) and the fact that taking pullbacks along the map of partition Lie algebroids
0\to T_{B/{R}}[1]
preserves limits and sifted colimits.
∎

Finally, we record the following reformulation of
Theorem

2.25
, using pro-coherent duality:

Corollary 3.13
.

Let
B
be a coherent animated
{R}
-algebra. Then there is a fully faithful functor

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{D}\colon\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\wedge\mathrm{aft}}_{{R}/B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Coalg}_{\mathrm{coLie}^{\pi}_{\Delta}}(\mathrm{Der}_{B/{R},\geq 0}^{\mathrm{aperf}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}})^{\mathrm{op}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\vee}}

whose essential image consists of the partition Lie algebroids whose underlying pro-coherent
B
-module is dually almost perfect of non-positive tor-amplitude.

Let us now turn to the proof of
Proposition

3.8
. We will show that the
\infty
-category
(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{B})_{/T_{B/{R}}[1]}
is generated under sifted colimits by the full subcategory of
M\to T_{B/{R}}[1]
where
M
is dually almost perfect of nonpositive tor-amplitude (
Definition

A.35
). To make this more precise, recall the process of adding colimits to
\infty
-categories from
[
HTT
, Section 5.3.6]
:

Definition 3.14
.

Let
\mathscr{K}
be a class of small
\infty
-categories. An
\infty
-category
\mathscr{V}
is said to be
\mathscr{K}
-complete if it admits all colimits of diagrams indexed by
\infty
-categories in
\mathscr{K}
.

Suppose that
\mathscr{C}
is a small
\infty
-category equipped with a set of colimiting cocones
\mathscr{R}=\{K_{\alpha}^{\rhd}\to\mathscr{C}\}
where each
K_{\alpha}\in\mathscr{K}
. We will say that a functor
j\colon\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{V}
exhibits
\mathscr{V}
as the
\mathscr{K}
-completion of
\mathscr{C}
relative to
\mathscr{R}
if it is the initial
\mathscr{K}
-complete
\infty
-category with a functor from
\mathscr{C}
that preserves the colimit diagrams in
\mathscr{R}
.

By
[
HTT
, Proposition 5.3.6.2]
, the
\mathscr{K}
-completion of
\mathscr{C}
relative to
\mathscr{R}
always exists and the functor
j\colon\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{V}
is fully faithful.

Proposition 3.15
.

Let
\mathscr{K}
be a class of small
\infty
-categories, each of which is contractible. Suppose that
j\colon\mathscr{C}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{V}
exhibits
\mathscr{V}
as the
\mathscr{K}
-completion of
\mathscr{C}
relative to a set of colimit diagrams
\mathscr{R}=\{K_{\alpha}^{\rhd}\to\mathscr{C}\}
. For any object
X\in\mathscr{V}
, the induced fully faithful functor

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j\colon\mathscr{C}_{/X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{V}_{/X}}

exhibits
\mathscr{V}_{/X}
as the
\mathscr{K}
-completion of
\mathscr{C}_{/X}
relative to the set
\mathscr{R}_{/X}
of colimit diagrams
K_{\alpha}^{\rhd}\to\mathscr{C}_{/X}
whose image in
\mathscr{C}
is contained in
\mathscr{R}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathscr{P}_{\mathscr{R}}(\mathscr{C})\subseteq\mathscr{P}(\mathscr{C})
be the
\infty
-category of presheaves on
\mathscr{C}
that send the colimit diagrams in
\mathscr{R}
to limits. By the proof of
[
HTT
, Proposition 5.3.6.2]
, we can then identify
\mathscr{V}\subseteq\mathscr{P}_{\mathscr{R}}(\mathscr{C})
with the smallest full subcategory that contains the representable presheaves and is closed under
\mathscr{K}
-indexed colimits. Using this, we can identify
X
with a presheaf
X\colon\mathscr{C}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathscr{S}
and
\mathscr{C}_{/X}
is the full subcategory of representable presheaves over
X
.

Now recall that the inclusion
\mathscr{C}_{/X}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{P}(\mathscr{C})_{/X}
induces a natural equivalence
\mathscr{P}(\mathscr{C}_{/X})\to\mathscr{P}(\mathscr{C})_{/X}

[
HTT
, Corollary 5.1.6.12]
(the proof in loc. cit. also applies to non-representable
X
). This equivalence restricts to an equivalence
\mathscr{P}_{\mathscr{R}}(\mathscr{C})_{/X}\simeq\mathscr{P}_{\mathscr{R}_{/X}}(\mathscr{C}_{/X})
. Since the forgetful functor
\mathscr{P}_{\mathscr{R}_{/X}}(\mathscr{C}_{/X})\to\mathscr{P}_{\mathscr{R}}(\mathscr{C})
detects colimits of
\mathscr{K}
-indexed diagrams (which are all contractible), it follows that
\mathscr{V}_{/X}\subseteq\mathscr{P}_{\mathscr{R}_{/X}}(\mathscr{C}_{/X})
is the smallest full subcategory that contains the representable presheaves and is closed under
\mathscr{K}
-indexed colimits. The result then follows from
[
HTT
, Proposition 5.3.6.2]
.
∎

Proof of
Proposition

3.8
.

Let
\mathrm{dAPerf}_{B}^{\weirdleq 0}\subseteq\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{B}
denote the full subcategory of dually almost perfect objects of non-positive tor-amplitude (
Definition

A.35
). This
\infty
-category is equivalent to
\mathrm{APerf}_{B,\geq 0}^{\mathrm{op}}
by pro-coherent duality.
It now follows from
[
BCN
, Proposition 4.20]
that
\mathrm{dAPerf}_{B}^{\weirdleq 0}\to\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{B}
exhibits
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{B}
as the sifted colimit completion of
\mathrm{dAPerf}_{B}^{\weirdleq 0}
relative to the set
\mathscr{R}_{B}
of colimit diagrams in
\mathrm{dAPerf}_{B}^{\weirdleq 0}
that are dual to the following diagrams in
\mathrm{APerf}_{B,\geq 0}
:

(a)

Augmented cosimplicial diagrams
M^{\bullet}\colon\Delta_{+}\to\mathrm{APerf}_{B,\geq 0}
such that
M^{-1}\simeq\Tot(M^{\bullet})
(computed in
\Mod_{B}
).

(b)

Limits
M_{\infty}\to\dots\to M_{2}\to M_{1}
of towers in
\mathrm{APerf}_{B,\geq 0}
that are almost eventually constant (see
Definition

3.5
).

By
Proposition

3.15
,
(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{B})_{/T_{B/{R}}[1]}
is the sifted colimit completion of
(\mathrm{dAPerf}_{B}^{\weirdleq 0})_{/T_{B/{R}}[1]}
relative to
(\mathscr{R}_{B})_{/T_{B/{R}}[1]}
. This implies that restriction along
j
determines an equivalence between:

(1)

the (monoidal)
\infty
-category of sifted colimit preserving endofunctors of
(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{B})_{/T_{B/{R}}[1]}
that preserve the full subcategory
(\mathrm{dAPerf}_{B}^{\weirdleq 0})_{/T_{B/{R}}[1]}
.

(2)

the (monoidal)
\infty
-category of endofunctors of
(\mathrm{dAPerf}_{B}^{\weirdleq 0})_{/T_{B/{R}}[1]}
that preserve all sifted colimits of the form (a) and (b) above.

Using that
(\mathrm{dAPerf}_{B}^{\weirdleq 0})_{/T_{B/{R}}[1]}\simeq\mathrm{Der}^{\mathrm{aperf},\mathrm{op}}_{B/R,\geq 0}
by pro-coherent duality (
Lemma

A.34
), the result then follows from
Lemma

3.6
.
∎

3.2.
Base change

We will now study the functoriality of the
\infty
-categories
\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}}
of partition Lie algebroids with respect to the animated
{R}
-algebra
B
. We will do this by directly construct the cartesian fibration classifying
B\longmapsto\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}}
. This is somewhat technical, and we refer to
Proposition

3.25
for the final result.

We start by studying the functoriality of the
\infty
-category
(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{B})_{/T_{B/{R}}[1]}
in the coherent animated
{R}
-algebra
B
. To do this, we will organise these
\infty
-categories into a cartesian fibration over the opposite of a certain subcategory of animated
{R}
-algebras.

Notation 3.16
.

Let
\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{coft}}_{{R}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{{R}}
denote the subcategory whose objects are coherent animated
{R}
-algebras and whose morphisms are maps
A\to B
that are almost finitely presented.
More generally, for any functor
\mathscr{M}\to\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{{R}}
or
\mathscr{N}\to\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{op}}_{{R}}
, we denote

\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{coft}}=\mathscr{M}\times_{\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{{R}}}\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{coft}}_{{R}}\hskip 40.0pt\mathscr{N}^{\mathrm{coft}}=\mathscr{N}\times_{\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{op}}_{{R}}}\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{coft},\mathrm{op}}_{{R}}.

Recall the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{Der}_{/{R}}
of derivations
(A,M,\alpha)=(A,\alpha\colon L_{A/{R}}[-1]\to M)
from Construction
2.26
, and let
\mathrm{Der}_{/{R},\geq 0}^{\mathrm{aperf}}\subseteq\mathrm{Der}_{/{R}}
denote the full subcategories of derivations
(A,M,\alpha)
where
M
is a connective almost perfect
A
-module.

Lemma 3.17
.

The projection
\pi\colon\mathrm{Der}_{/{R},\geq 0}^{\mathrm{aperf},\mathrm{coft}}\to\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{coft}}_{{R}}
is a cocartesian fibration.

Proof.

Let
f\colon A\to B
be a map of animated
{R}
-algebras. Recall from Remark
2.27
that a map
(A,M,\alpha)\to(B,N,\beta)
covering
f
is a cocartesian arrow in
\mathrm{Der}_{/{R}}
if the induced square of
B
-modules

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}L_{A/{R}}[-1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{B/{R}}[-1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}(\alpha)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta}

is cocartesian. If
f
is almost finitely presented, then the cofibres of the horizontal maps are almost perfect connective
B
-modules. Consequently,
N
will be an almost perfect
B
-module if
M
is an almost perfect connective
A
-module. We conclude that
\mathrm{Der}_{/{R},\geq 0}^{\mathrm{aperf},\mathrm{coft}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Der}_{/{R}}
is stable under taking cocartesian lifts of maps in
\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{coft}}_{{R}}
, so that
\pi
is indeed a cocartesian fibration.
∎

Construction 3.18
.

Let
\mathrm{Der}_{/{R},\geq 0}^{\mathrm{aperf},\mathrm{coft},\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{coft},\mathrm{op}}_{{R}}
be the opposite of the cocartesian fibration from
Lemma

3.17
. This classifies a functor
\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{coft}}_{{R}}\to\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
sending
A\mapsto\mathrm{Der}^{\mathrm{aperf},\mathrm{op}}_{A/{R},\geq 0}
and sending each map
f\colon A\to B
to the functor

(4)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(L_{A/{R}}[-1]\to M\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Big(L_{B/{R}}[-1]\to L_{B/{R}}[-1]\oplus_{f^{*}L_{A/{R}}[-1]}f^{*}M\Big).}

Write
\mathscr{R}_{A}
for the collection of colimit diagrams in
\mathrm{Der}^{\mathrm{aperf},\mathrm{op}}_{A/{R},\geq 0}
opposite to pullback squares of derivations along
\pi_{0}
-surjections, as well as limits of towers of derivations that are almost eventually constant (cf.
Definition

3.5
).

By
Proposition

3.15
and
Lemma

A.42
, the fully faithful functor

(5)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\vee}\colon\mathrm{Der}^{\mathrm{aperf},\mathrm{op}}_{A/{R},\geq 0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}\big)_{/T_{A/{R}}[1]}}

exhibits
(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A})_{/T_{A/{R}}[1]}
as the completion of
\mathrm{Der}^{\mathrm{aperf},\mathrm{op}}_{A/{R},\geq 0}
under colimits relative to the set of colimit diagrams
\mathscr{R}_{A}
from the proof of
Proposition

3.8
(see
Definition

3.14
).

Each base change functor
\mathrm{Der}^{\mathrm{aperf},\mathrm{op}}_{A/{R},\geq 0}\to\mathrm{Der}^{\mathrm{aperf},\mathrm{op}}_{B/{R},\geq 0}
sends colimit diagrams in
\mathscr{R}_{A}
to colimit diagrams in
\mathscr{R}_{B}
. The universal property of
(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A})_{/T_{A/{R}}[1]}
therefore implies that the maps (
5
) assemble into a natural transformation between two diagrams of
\infty
-categories. Explicitly, this sends
f\colon A\to B
to a commuting square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Der}^{\mathrm{aperf},\mathrm{op}}_{A/{R},\geq 0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}\big)_{/T_{A/{R}}[1]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Der}^{\mathrm{aperf},\mathrm{op}}_{B/{R},\geq 0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}\big)_{/T_{B/{R}}[1]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\vee}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\vee}}

where the right vertical functor
f^{\sharp}
is the unique colimit preserving extension of the left vertical functor, given by

f^{\sharp}\big(F\to T_{A/{R}}[1]\big)=f^{*}F\times_{(f^{*}L_{A/{R}})^{\vee}[1]}T_{A/{R}}[1].

Note that this indeed preserves colimits and is dual to (
4
).

Definition 3.19
.

We will write
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{/T[1]}\to\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{coft},\mathrm{op}}_{{R}}
for the cartesian fibration classified by the functor
A\longmapsto(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A})_{/T_{A/{R}}[1]}
from Construction
3.18
, and

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Der}_{/{R},\geq 0}^{\mathrm{aperf},\mathrm{coft},\mathrm{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{/T[1]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{coft},\mathrm{op}}_{{R}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\vee}}

for the unstraightening of the natural transformation from Construction
3.18
.

Our next goal will be to construct a version of the partition Lie algebroid monad in families. To this end, recall from
Definition

2.29
that taking cotangent fibres and square zero extensions defines a relative adjunction

(6)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{der},\Delta^{1}}_{{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Der}_{/{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{der}}_{{R}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{ev}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{sqz}}

We will write
\coLie^{\pi}_{\Delta}=\cot\circ\mathrm{sqz}
for the composite comonad relative to the base
\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{der}}_{{R}}
. On the fibre over each animated
{R}
-algebra
B
, this restricts to the comonad
\coLie^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}}
from Notation
3.3
.

Lemma 3.20
.

The adjoint pair (
6
) enjoys the following properties:

(1)

Let
f\colon(A^{\prime}\to A)\to(B^{\prime}\to B)
be a map of animated surjections such that
A\otimes_{A^{\prime}}B^{\prime}\simeq B
and
L_{B/A}\simeq 0
. Then
\cot(A^{\prime}\to A)\to\cot(B^{\prime}\to B)
is a cocartesian arrow in
\mathrm{Der}_{/{R}}
.

(2)

Let
(A,M,\alpha)\to(B,N,\beta)
be a cocartesian arrow in
\mathrm{Der}_{/{R}}
such that
A,M,B,N
are all connective and
L_{B/A}\simeq 0
. Then the induced square of animated rings

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\oplus_{\alpha}M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B\oplus_{\beta}N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}

is cocartesian.

Proof.

For property (1), note that the left commutative square of animated
{R}
-algebras gives rise to the right square of
B
-modules:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}L_{A/{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}L_{A/A^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{B/{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{B/B^{\prime}}}

Since left square is a pushout of derived rings, the right vertical map
f^{*}L_{A/A^{\prime}}\to L_{B/B^{\prime}}
is an equivalence. The left vertical map is an equivalence as well, since
f\colon A\to B
was formally étale. It follows that the right square is a pushout square of
B
-modules, which means precisely that
\cot(A^{\prime}\to A)\to\cot(B^{\prime}\to B)
is a cocartesian arrow in
\mathrm{Der}_{/{R}}
by Remark
2.28
.

Property (2) follows from deformation theory. Indeed, consider the cartesian square of animated rings

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\oplus_{\alpha}M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\oplus M[1].}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}

and note that we need to show that the natural map
q_{2}^{*}(B\oplus_{\beta}N)\to B
is an equivalence of connective
A
-modules. By
[
SAG
, Theorem 16.2.0.2]
, the above square induces an equivalence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(q_{1}^{*},q_{2}^{*})}\colon\Mod_{A\oplus_{\alpha}M,\geq 0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Mod_{A,\geq 0}\times_{\Mod_{A\oplus M[1],\geq 0}}\Mod_{A,\geq 0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

with inverse sending
(E_{1},E_{2},0^{*}E_{1}\simeq\alpha^{*}E_{2})
to the fibre product
q_{1*}(E_{1})\times_{q_{0*}0^{*}(E_{1})}q_{2*}(E_{2})
.
Since
L_{B/A}\simeq 0
, the cocartesian morphism
(A,M,\alpha)\to(B,N,\beta)
induces an equivalence
N\simeq B\otimes_{A}M
(Remark
2.28
). Using this, one sees that the
A\oplus_{\alpha}M
-module
B\oplus_{\beta}N
corresponds under the above equivalence to the triple
(B,B,0^{*}(B)\simeq\alpha^{*}B)
, where the equivalence is induced by
\beta
. This implies that
q_{2}^{*}(B\oplus_{\beta}N)\to B
is an equivalence.
∎

Lemma 3.21
.

The comonad
\coLie^{\pi}_{\Delta}
restricts to a fibrewise comonad

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Der}_{/{R},\geq 0}^{\mathrm{aperf},\mathrm{coft}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Der}_{/{R},\geq 0}^{\mathrm{aperf},\mathrm{coft}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{coft}}_{{R}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{coLie}^{\pi}_{\Delta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}

where
\pi
sends a tuple
(A,M,\alpha)
to the underlying coherent animated
{R}
-algebra
A
. Furthermore, it preserves
\pi
-cocartesian maps covering étale maps.

Proof.

The comonad restricts by
Lemma

3.6
and preserves
\pi
-cocartesian morphisms covering étale maps by
Lemma

3.20
.
∎

We then have the following families version of
Proposition

3.8
:

Proposition 3.22
.

There exists a unique extension

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Der}_{/{R},\geq 0}^{\mathrm{aperf},\mathrm{coft},\mathrm{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Der}_{/{R},\geq 0}^{\mathrm{aperf},\mathrm{coft},\mathrm{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}\big)_{/T[1]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}\big)_{/T[1]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\coLie^{\pi}_{\Delta})^{\mathrm{op}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\vee}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\vee}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lie^{\pi}_{\Delta}}

to a monad
\Lie^{\pi}_{\Delta}
relative to
\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{coft},\mathrm{op}}_{{R}}
, that is, a monad in
\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty/\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{coft},\mathrm{op}}_{{R}}}
, that preserves sifted colimits fibrewise.

Remark 3.23
.

The relative monad
\Lie^{\pi}_{\Delta}
restricts to a monad
(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{B})_{/T_{B/{R}}[1]}\to(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{B})_{/T_{B/{R}}[1]}
on the fibre over a coherent animated
{R}
-algebra
B
. This monad preserves sifted colimits and extends the monad
(\coLie^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}})^{\mathrm{op}}
, so that it is naturally equivalent to the partition Lie algebroid monad
\Lie^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}}
of
Proposition

3.8
. Consequently,
Proposition

3.22
shows that the partition Lie algebroid monads
\Lie^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}}
depend oplax naturally on the coherent animated
{R}
-algebra
B
.

As the proof of
Proposition

3.22
is somewhat technical, we first describe the consequences of
Proposition

3.22
at the level of algebras.

Definition 3.24
.

We define the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,/{R}}
of
partition Lie algebroids
to be the
\infty
-category of algebras for the monad
\Lie^{\pi}_{\Delta}\colon\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{/T[1]}\to\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{/T[1]}
.

Since
\Lie^{\pi}_{\Delta}
covers the identity monad on
\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{coft},\mathrm{op}}_{{R}}
, we obtain a natural projection

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,/{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Alg_{\id}\big(\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{coft},\mathrm{op}}_{{R}}\big)\simeq\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{coft},\mathrm{op}}_{{R}}.}

For each coherent animated
{R}
-algebra
B
, the fibre over
B
is equivalent to the
\infty
-category of partition Lie algebroids over
B
from
Definition

3.9
.

Proposition 3.25
.

The following assertions hold:

(1)

The forgetful functor defines a map of cartesian fibrations preserving cartesian arrows

(7)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,/{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{/T[1]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{coft},\mathrm{op}}_{{R}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\forget}

In particular, every almost finitely presented map
f\colon A\to B
of animated
{R}
-algebras induces a commuting square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,A/{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}(A)_{/T_{A/{R}}[1]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{B})_{/T_{B/{R}}[1]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\forget}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\forget}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\sharp}}

where the bottom functor is given by
f^{\sharp}(\mathfrak{g})=f^{*}(\mathfrak{g})\times_{(f^{*}L_{A/{R}})^{\vee}[1]}T_{B/{R}}[1]
.

(2)

If
f\colon A\to B
is étale, then the square (
7
) is left adjointable, i.e.
f^{\sharp}
intertwines the free partition Lie algebroid functors. Consequently,
f^{\sharp}\colon\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,A/{R}}\to\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}}
preserves colimits and is given by
\mathfrak{g}\longmapsto f^{*}\mathfrak{g}
on the underlying pro-coherent modules.

(3)

The cartesian fibration
\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,/{R}}\to\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{coft},\mathrm{op}}
is classified by a functor
A\longmapsto\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,A/{R}}
satisfying étale descent.

Remark 3.26
.

Note that for a general finitely presented map
f\colon A\to B
, the functor
f^{\sharp}
preserves sifted colimits, but it need not preserve limits and it typically does not preserve all colimits.

Proof.

Assertion (1) is a formal property of categories of algebras over a fibrewise monad: if
p\colon\mathscr{Y}\to\mathscr{X}
is a cartesian fibration and
T\in\mathrm{End}_{/\mathscr{X}}(\mathscr{Y})
is a fibrewise monad on
\mathscr{Y}
, then the forgetful functor
\Alg_{T}(\mathscr{Y})\to\mathscr{Y}
is a map of cartesian fibrations over
\mathscr{X}
that preserves cartesian edges. To see this, let
\mathscr{O}
be the operad for algebras and modules and consider the map of
\mathscr{O}
-monoidal categories
\big(\mathrm{End}_{/\mathscr{X}}(\mathscr{Y}),\mathscr{Y}\big)\to\big(\ast,\mathscr{X}\big)
. Taking algebras, we then obtain

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Alg_{\mathscr{O}}\big(\mathrm{End}_{/\mathscr{X}}(\mathscr{Y}),\mathscr{Y}\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Alg_{\mathbb{E}_{1}}\big(\mathrm{End}_{/\mathscr{X}}(\mathscr{Y})\big)\times\mathscr{Y}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}.}

It follows from
[
HA
, Corollary 3.2.2.3]
that this is a map of cartesian fibrations over
\mathscr{X}
preserving cartesian arrows. Taking the fibre over a fixed monad
T\in\Alg_{\mathbb{E}_{1}}\big(\mathrm{End}_{/\mathscr{X}}(\mathscr{Y})\big)
, one obtains that
\Alg_{T}(\mathscr{Y})\to\mathscr{X}
is a cartesian fibration as well (with cartesian arrows detected in
\mathscr{Y}
).

Assertion (2) follows from
Lemma

3.6
and (3) follows from (2) and étale descent for
A\longmapsto\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}(A)
.
∎

We now turn to the proof of
Proposition

3.22
. We will need the following technical category-theoretic result about completing families of
\infty
-categories under colimits:

Definition 3.27
.

Let
p\colon\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{B}
be a cartesian fibration over a small
\infty
-category and let
\mathscr{K}
be a class of small
\infty
-categories.

•

Let us say that a colimit diagram
K^{\rhd}\to\mathscr{C}_{b}
in a fibre is
conserved
if it is preserved by the base change functor
f^{*}\colon\mathscr{C}_{b}\to\mathscr{C}_{b^{\prime}}
for each
b^{\prime}\to b
in
\mathscr{B}
.

•

We will say that
p
is
\mathscr{K}
-complete
if each fibre
\mathscr{E}_{b}
is
\mathscr{K}
-complete and if all of these colimits are conserved.

•

By a
collection of conserved colimit diagrams
we will mean a collection
\mathscr{R}=\cup_{b\in\mathscr{B}}\mathscr{R}_{b}
, where each
\mathscr{R}_{b}=\big\{f_{\alpha}\colon K_{\alpha}^{\rhd}\to\mathscr{C}_{b}\big\}
is a set of conserved colimit diagrams in the fibre
\mathscr{C}_{b}
and each base change functor sends
\mathscr{R}_{b}
to
\mathscr{R}_{b^{\prime}}
.

•

Given another cartesian fibration
q\colon\mathscr{W}\to\mathscr{B}
, write
\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathscr{R}/\mathscr{B}}(\mathscr{C},\mathscr{W})
for the full subcategory of
\mathrm{Fun}_{/\mathscr{B}}(\mathscr{C},\mathscr{W})
on those functors
F\colon\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{W}
over
\mathscr{B}
such that each map on fibres
F_{b}\colon\mathscr{C}_{b}\to\mathscr{W}_{b}
preserves the colimits in
\mathscr{R}_{b}
. If
\mathscr{R}
is the collection of all colimits indexed by diagrams from
\mathscr{K}
, we will simply write
\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathscr{K}/\mathscr{B}}(\mathscr{C},\mathscr{W})
.

Proposition 3.28
.

Let
\mathscr{B}
be a small
\infty
-category and consider a fully faithful map of cartesian fibrations, preserving cartesian arrows

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{V}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{B}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{p}}

Let
\mathscr{K}
be a collection of small
\infty
-categories and let
\mathscr{R}
be a set of conserved colimit diagrams, indexed by
\infty
-categories in
\mathscr{K}
. Suppose that the following condition holds:

(1)

\hat{p}\colon\mathscr{V}\to\mathscr{B}
is
\mathscr{K}
-complete.

(2)

Each
j_{b}\colon\mathscr{C}_{b}\to\mathscr{V}_{b}
exhibits
\mathscr{V}_{b}
as the
\mathscr{K}
-completion of
\mathscr{C}_{b}
relative to
\mathscr{R}_{b}
.

Then for every
\mathscr{K}
-complete cartesian fibration
q\colon\mathscr{W}\to\mathscr{B}
, restriction along
j
induces an equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathscr{K}/\mathscr{B}}(\mathscr{V},\mathscr{W})\simeq\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathscr{R}/\mathscr{B}}(\mathscr{C},\mathscr{W}).

Proof.

We will need some details about the construction of the
\mathscr{K}
-completion
\mathscr{V}_{b}
of
\mathscr{C}_{b}
relative to
\mathscr{R}_{b}
from the proof of
[
HTT
, Proposition 5.3.6.2]
. Let
\mathscr{P}_{\mathscr{R}_{b}}(\mathscr{C}_{b})\subseteq\mathscr{P}(\mathscr{C}_{b})
be the
\infty
-category of presheaves on
\mathscr{C}_{b}
sending all colimits in
\mathscr{R}_{b}
to limits and recall that we can identify
\mathscr{V}_{b}\subseteq\mathscr{P}_{\mathscr{R}_{b}}(\mathscr{C}_{b})
with the smallest subcategory that contains the representable presheaves and is closed under
\mathscr{K}
-indexed colimits. Let
L_{b}\colon\mathscr{P}(\mathscr{C}_{b})\to\mathscr{P}_{\mathscr{R}_{b}}(\mathscr{C}_{b})
be the left adjoint to the inclusion and let
\mathscr{E}_{b}\subseteq\mathscr{P}(\mathscr{C}_{b})
be the inverse image
L_{b}^{-1}(\mathscr{V}_{b})
; note that
\mathscr{E}_{b}
is closed under
\mathscr{K}
-indexed colimits in
\mathscr{P}(\mathscr{C}_{b})
. The functor
j_{b}\colon\mathscr{C}_{b}\to\mathscr{V}_{b}
then factors as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{b}\colon\mathscr{C}_{b}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{E}_{b}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{V}_{b}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{b}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{b}}

where the first functor is the Yoneda embedding and the second is a localisation (at the
L_{b}
-local equivalences). All of this depends functorially on the pair
(\mathscr{C}_{b},\mathscr{R}_{b})
. Consequently, under unstraightening it gives rise to a diagram of cartesian fibrations and maps preserving cartesian arrows

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{V}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{p}}

where
\mathscr{E}
and
\mathscr{V}
are both
\mathscr{K}
-complete. By
[
Hin16
]
, the map
L
is a localisation, inverting the
L_{b}
-local equivalences in each fibre
\mathscr{E}_{b}
. Since each localisation
L_{b}
preserves
\mathscr{K}
-indexed colimits and admits a fully faithful right adjoint, it follows that restriction along
L
defines a fully faithful embedding

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathscr{K}/\mathscr{B}}(\mathscr{V},\mathscr{W})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathscr{K}/\mathscr{B}}(\mathscr{E},\mathscr{W}).}

Write
\mathrm{Fun}^{\prime}_{\mathscr{K}/\mathscr{B}}(\mathscr{E},\mathscr{W})
for the essential image; it consists of those functors
F\colon\mathscr{E}\to\mathscr{W}
over
\mathscr{B}
such that each
F_{b}\colon\mathscr{E}_{B}\to\mathscr{W}_{b}
preserves
\mathscr{K}
-indexed colimits and sends the
L_{b}
-local equivalences to equivalences in
\mathscr{W}_{b}
. We now claim that the following two assertions hold:

(a)

Let
F\in\mathrm{Fun}_{/\mathscr{B}}(\mathscr{E},\mathscr{W})
such that its restriction
F\big|\mathscr{C}
is contained in
\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathscr{R}/\mathscr{B}}(\mathscr{C},\mathscr{W})
. Then
F\in\mathrm{Fun}^{\prime}_{\mathscr{K}/\mathscr{B}}(\mathscr{E},\mathscr{W})
is the
q
-left Kan extension of
F\big|\mathscr{C}
along
h
if and only if
F
sends
L
-local equivalences and preserves
\mathscr{K}
-indexed colimits fibrewise.

(b)

If
F_{0}\in\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathscr{R}/\mathscr{B}}(\mathscr{C},\mathscr{W})
, then there exists a
q
-left Kan extension
F\in\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathscr{K}/\mathscr{B}}(\mathscr{E},\mathscr{W})
of
F_{0}
along
h
.

Given (a) and (b), the result follows: restriction along the localisation
L
yields an equivalence
\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathscr{K}/\mathscr{B}}(\mathscr{V},\mathscr{W})\simeq\mathrm{Fun}^{\prime}_{\mathscr{K}/\mathscr{B}}(\mathscr{E},\mathscr{W})
and restriction along
h
yields an equivalence
\mathrm{Fun}^{\prime}_{\mathscr{K}/\mathscr{B}}(\mathscr{E},\mathscr{W})\simeq\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathscr{R}/\mathscr{B}}(\mathscr{C},\mathscr{W})
with inverse given by
q
-left Kan extension.

It remains to verify claims (a) and (b). In the case where
\mathscr{B}=\ast
, these assertions are proven in the proof of
[
HTT
, Proposition 5.3.6.2]
; we will reduce to this case by a cofinality argument.
To this end, let us fix a presheaf
X\in\mathscr{E}_{b}\subseteq\mathscr{P}(\mathscr{C}_{b})
in the fibre over
b\in\mathscr{B}
. Then the functor

(8)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathscr{C}_{b})_{/X}:=\mathscr{C}_{b}\times_{\mathscr{E}_{b}}(\mathscr{E}_{b})_{/X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{C}\times_{\mathscr{E}}\mathscr{E}_{/X}=:\mathscr{C}_{/X}}

is cofinal. To see this, we have to show that for each
g\colon c\to X
in
\mathscr{C}_{/X}
covering a map
f\colon b^{\prime}\to b
, the
\infty
-category
\big((\mathscr{C}_{b})_{/X}\big)_{g/}
is contractible. Unravelling the definitions, the left fibration
\big((\mathscr{C}_{b})_{/X}\big)_{g/}\to(\mathscr{C}_{b})_{/X}
classifies the functor

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathscr{C}_{b})_{/X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{S};}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(c^{\prime}\to X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map_{\mathscr{E}_{b^{\prime}}}(c,f^{*}c^{\prime})\times_{\Map_{\mathscr{E}_{b^{\prime}}}(c,f^{*}X)}\{g\}.}

The colimit of this diagram is contractible: indeed,
f^{*}\colon\mathscr{E}_{b}\to\mathscr{E}_{b^{\prime}}
and
\Map_{\mathscr{E}_{b^{\prime}}}(c,-)\colon\mathscr{E}_{b^{\prime}}\to\mathscr{S}
both preserve colimits (recall that
\mathscr{E}^{\prime}_{b}\subseteq\mathscr{P}(\mathscr{C}^{\prime}_{b})
and that
f^{*}
is the colimit-preserving extension of
f^{*}\colon\mathscr{C}_{b}\to\mathscr{C}_{b}^{\prime}
by definition).

Using
[
HTT
, Corollary 4.3.1.16]
and the fact that (
8
) is cofinal, we find that:

(a’)

F\in\mathrm{Fun}_{/\mathscr{B}}(\mathscr{E},\mathscr{W})
is the
q
-left Kan of its restriction to
\mathscr{C}
if and only if each
F_{b}\colon\mathscr{E}_{b}\to\mathscr{W}_{b}
is the left Kan extension of its restriction to
\mathscr{C}_{b}
.

(b’)

F_{0}\in\mathrm{Fun}_{/\mathscr{B}}(\mathscr{C},\mathscr{W})
admits a
q
-left Kan extension along
h
if and only if each
F_{0b}\colon\mathscr{C}_{b}\to\mathscr{W}_{b}
admits a left Kan extension along
h_{b}
.

Using this, it suffices to verify assertions (a) and (b) fibrewise, where they hold by the proof of
[
HTT
, Proposition 5.3.6.2]
.
∎

Proof of
Proposition

3.22
.

This follows the same strategy as the proof of
Proposition

3.8
. By
Proposition

3.28
and the proof of
Proposition

3.8
(applied fibrewise), restriction defines an equivalence of monoidal
\infty
-categories between:

(1)

endofunctors of
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{/T[1]}
relative to
\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{coft},\mathrm{op}}_{{R}}
that preserve sifted colimits fibrewise and that preserve the full subcategory
\mathrm{Der}_{/{R},\geq 0}^{\mathrm{aperf},\mathrm{coft},\mathrm{op}}
.

(2)

endofunctors of
\mathrm{Der}_{/{R},\geq 0}^{\mathrm{aperf},\mathrm{coft},\mathrm{op}}
relative to
\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{coft},\mathrm{op}}_{{R}}
that preserve the sifted colimit diagrams (a) and (b) from the proof of
Proposition

3.8
fibrewise.

The result now follows from the fact that the monad
(\coLie^{\pi}_{\Delta})^{\mathrm{op}}
defines an algebra in the
\infty
-category (2), by
Lemma

3.6
.
∎

3.3.
Partition Lie algebroids on schemes

We will now deduce
Theorem

3.1
from
Proposition

3.25
by a descent argument. To establish unicity of the partition Lie algebroid monad
\Lie^{\pi}_{\Delta,X/{R}}
, we will need the following observation:

Lemma 3.29
.

Let
X
be a locally coherent qcqs scheme,
T\in\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X}
and write
\mathscr{C}_{T}\subseteq(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X})_{/T}
for the full subcategory of
M\to T
where
M
is dually almost perfect of non-positive tor-amplitude. If
\mathscr{D}
be an
\infty
-category with sifted colimits and
F\colon(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X})_{/T}\to\mathscr{D}
preserves sifted colimits, then
F
is left Kan extended from its restriction to
\mathscr{C}_{T}\subseteq(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X})_{/T}
.

Proof.

The proof of
Lemma

A.42
shows that the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X}\simeq\mathrm{Ind}(\mathrm{Coh}_{X}^{\mathrm{op}})
is equivalent to the
\infty
-category of functors
\big(\mathrm{APerf}_{X,\geq 0}\big)^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathscr{S}
that are reduced excisive and preserve limits of almost eventually constant towers (cf.
Definition

3.5
). Under this equivalence, the dually almost perfect objects of non-positive tor-amplitude simply correspond to the corepresentable functors. Consequently, for every
M\in\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X}
the canonical map
\colim_{N\in\mathscr{C}_{M}}N\to M
is an equivalence.

Now let
M\in(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X})_{/T}
and notice that there is an equivalence
(\mathscr{C}_{T})_{/M}\simeq\mathscr{C}_{M}
. To see that
F
is left Kan extended from
\mathscr{C}_{T}
, we need to show that

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\colim_{N\in(\mathscr{C}_{T})_{/M}}F(N)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\big(\colim_{N\in(\mathscr{C}_{T})_{/M}}N\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(M)}

are both equivalences. Now note that
(\mathscr{C}_{T})_{/M}\simeq\mathscr{C}_{M}
, so that the last map is an equivalence. Since
\mathscr{C}_{M}
is sifted (it has finite coproducts) and
F
preserves sifted colimits, the first map is an equivalence as well.
∎

Proof of
Theorem

3.1
.

It follows directly from
Lemma

3.29
that space of sifted colimit preserving extensions is either empty or contractible. It therefore remains to prove the existence of the monad
\Lie^{\pi}_{\Delta,X/{R}}
. Writing
\mathfrak{U}
for the poset of affine opens of
X
,
Proposition

3.25
provides a natural diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{U}^{\mathrm{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{RAd}}(\Delta^{1},\mathrm{Cat});\quad U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Big((\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{U})_{T_{U/{R}}[1]}\xrightarrow{\text{free}}\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,\mathcal{O}(U)/{R})}\Big)}

to the
\infty
-category of left adjoints and right adjointable squares between them
[
HA
, Definition 4.7.4.16]
. The limit of this diagram then yields an adjoint pair
[
HA
, Proposition 4.7.4.19]

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lie^{\pi}_{\Delta}\colon\lim\limits_{U\in\mathfrak{U}^{\mathrm{op}}}(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{U})_{/T_{U/{R}}[1]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lim\limits_{U\in\mathfrak{U}^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,\mathcal{O}(U)/{R}}\colon\forget}

where the left (right) adjoint is given by the limit of the diagram of free (forgetful) functor for all affine open subspaces. Consequently, both adjoints preserve sifted colimits. Since pro-coherent sheaves satisfy descent (Corollary
A.39
), one obtains a sifted colimit-preserving monad

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lie^{\pi}_{\Delta,X/{R}}\colon(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X})_{/T_{X/{R}}[1]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X})_{/T_{X/{R}}[1]}.}

By construction, the restriction of this monad to the full subcategory
\mathrm{Der}_{X/{R},\geq 0}^{\mathrm{aperf},\mathrm{op}}
is the limit of the monads

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(\cot\circ\mathrm{triv}(-^{\vee})\big)^{\vee}\colon\mathrm{Der}_{\mathcal{O}(U)/{R},\geq 0}^{\mathrm{aperf},\mathrm{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Der}_{\mathcal{O}(U)/{R},\geq 0}^{\mathrm{aperf},\mathrm{op}}}

for all affine opens
U\subseteq X
. By descent for almost perfect complexes, this monad naturally equivalent to
\big(\cot\circ\mathrm{triv}(-^{\vee})\big)^{\vee}
, as desired.
∎

Remark 3.30
.

The proof of
Theorem

3.1
shows that the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,X/{R}}
from
Definition

3.2
coincides with the definition as the limit of the categories
\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,U/{R}}
from
Definition

3.9
for all affine open subschemes.

4.
Infinitesimal deformations of families

In this section, we will show that the
\infty
-category of partition Lie algebroids over a coherent animated
{R}
-algebra
B
is equivalent to the
\infty
-category of deformation functors, or
formal moduli problems
in the terminology of Lurie
[
SAG
, Chapter 12]
, defined on a certain
\infty
-category of Artinian extensions of
B
. In Section
4.4
, we will use this to show that every partition Lie algebroid over
B
has an underlying
{R}
-linear partition Lie algebra.

4.1.
Artinian extensions

We begin by introducing the following variant of Artin local rings relative to
B
:

Definition 4.1

(Artinian extensions)
.

Let
B
be a coherent animated
{R}
-algebra. We will say that a map of animated
{R}
-algebras
A\to B
is an
Artinian extension
if it satisfies the following three conditions:

(1)

A\to B
is a nilpotent extension, that is,
\pi_{0}(A)\to\pi_{0}(B)
is surjective with nilpotent kernel.

(2)

A\to B
is almost of finite presentation.

(3)

the fibre of
A\to B
is eventually coconnective.

A map of animated
{R}
-algebras
A\to B
is said to be an
almost Artinian extension
if it satisfies conditions (1) and (2). We will write
\mathrm{Art}_{{R}/B}\subseteq\mathrm{AArt}_{{R}/B}\subseteq\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{{R}/B}
for the full subcategory on the Artinian extensions of
B
.

Remark 4.2
.

Let
A
be a discrete commutative ring and let
I\subseteq A
be a finitely presented nilpotent ideal such that
A/I
is a coherent ring. Let us recall the following facts:

(1)

A
is coherent
[
Gla89
, Theorem 4.1.1]
.

(2)

For every
k\geq 0
, the ideal
I^{k}
is finitely generated, and hence finitely presented over
A
.

(3)

If
M
is a finitely presented
A
-module, then
I^{k}M/I^{k+1}M
is a finitely presented
A
-module and hence a finitely presented
A/I
-module
[
Gla89
, Theorem 2.1.8, Theorem 2.2.1]
.

Proposition 4.3
.

Let
A^{\prime}\to A
be a map in
\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{{R}/B}
with
A\in\mathrm{Art}_{{R}/B}
. Then the following assertions are equivalent:

(1)

A^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Art}_{{R}/B}
and
A^{\prime}\to A
is a nilpotent extension.

(2)

There exists a finite chain of maps
A^{\prime}=A_{n}\to A_{n-1}\to\dots\to A_{0}=A
in
\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{{R}/B}
where each
A_{i}\to A_{i-1}
is a square zero extension by a connective coherent
B
-module, viewed as an
A_{i-1}
-module via the canonical map
A_{i-1}\to B
.

Proof.

If
A^{\prime}\to A
satisfies condition (2), it is clearly a nilpotent extension. We will prove by induction on
i
that the map
A_{i}\to B
exhibits
B
as an almost perfect
A_{i}
-module. This is evident for
i=0
. Assume that
B\in\mathrm{APerf}_{A_{i-1}}
, so that every almost perfect
B
-module is almost perfect as an
A_{i-1}
-module. It follows that
A_{i}\to A_{i-1}
is a square zero extension by an almost perfect
A_{i-1}
-module.
Lemma

2.20
now implies that every almost perfect
A_{i-1}
-module is almost perfect as an
A_{i}
-module, so that
B\in\mathrm{APerf}_{A_{i}}
as well.
Proposition

2.14
now implies that
A_{i}\to B
is almost finitely presented. Since
A_{i}\to B
is clearly a nilpotent extension with eventually coconnective fibre, we conclude that
A_{i}\to B
is an Artinian extension, so that (1) follows.

For the converse, we first suppose that
A^{\prime}\to A
is a nilpotent extension with
A^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Art}_{{R}/B}
such that
A\in\mathrm{APerf}_{A^{\prime}}
. Write

I=\ker(\pi_{0}(A^{\prime})\to\pi_{0}(B))\qquad\text{and}\qquad J=\ker(\pi_{0}(A^{\prime})\to\pi_{0}(A)).

Both
I
and
J
are finitely presented nilpotent ideals, since
A^{\prime}\to A
and
A^{\prime}\to B
are almost finitely presented. In particular,
\pi_{0}(A^{\prime})
is a coherent ring by Remark
4.2
, and likewise for
\pi_{0}(A)
.

We now fix
m\geq 0
such that
I^{m}=0
and
t\geq 1
such that
\cofib(A^{\prime}\to A)
is
t
-coconnective; such
t
exists since
A^{\prime}\to B
and
A\to B
both have eventually coconnective fibres.
We will construct a sequence of animated
A^{\prime}
-algebras
A^{\prime}=A_{(t+1)}\to\dots\to A_{(3)}\to A_{(2)}\to A_{(1)}\to A_{(0)}=A
with the following properties:

(a)

For each
i
,
\cofib(A\to A_{(i)})
is a
i
-connective and
t
-coconnective almost perfect
A^{\prime}
-module.

(b)

Each
A_{(i+1)}\to A_{(i)}
decomposes into
m
square zero extensions, each by the
i
-fold suspension of a finitely presented discrete
\pi_{0}(B)
-module.

Since finitely presented discrete
\pi_{0}(B)
-modules are coherent as
B
-modules (
Definition

A.41
), this provides the desired decomposition.

To begin, we define
A_{(1)}=\pi_{0}(A^{\prime})\times_{\pi_{0}(A)}A
and note that
A_{(1)}\to A_{(0)}=A
is the base change of the sequence of square zero extensions

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{0}(A^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{0}(A^{\prime})/I^{m-1}J}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{0}(A^{\prime})/IJ}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{0}(A^{\prime})/J=\pi_{0}(A)}

by the finitely presented discrete
\pi_{0}(B)
-modules
I^{k}J/I^{k+1}J
(by Remark
4.2
). Note that
\pi_{0}(A^{\prime})\cong\pi_{0}(A_{(1)})
and that
\cofib(A^{\prime}\to A_{(1)})
is
1
-connective and
t
-coconnective, as desired.

For
i\geq 1
, suppose that we have constructed
A_{(i)}
and let
C=\cofib(A^{\prime}\to A_{(i)})\in\mathrm{APerf}_{A^{\prime}}
. Since
C
is
i
-connective and
\pi_{0}(A^{\prime})\cong\pi_{0}(A_{(i)})
,
L_{A_{(i)}/A^{\prime}}
is
i
-connective and
\pi_{i}(L_{A_{(i)}/A^{\prime}})\cong\pi_{i}(C)
is a finitely presented
\pi_{0}(A^{\prime})
-module. We define
A_{(i+1)}
to be the square zero extension of
A_{(i)}
classified by the map
L_{A_{(i)}/A^{\prime}}\to\pi_{i}(C)[i]
. Then
\cofib(A^{\prime}\to A_{(i+1)})\simeq\tau_{\geq i+1}C
is
(i+1)
-connective and
t
-coconnective. The finitely presented
\pi_{0}(A^{\prime})
-module
\pi_{i}(C)
fits into a sequence of extensions

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{i}(C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{i}(C)/I^{m-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{i}(C)/I^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{i}(C)\otimes_{\pi_{0}(A^{\prime})}\pi_{0}(B)}

by finitely presented discrete
\pi_{0}(B)
-modules. This implies that
A_{(i+1)}\to A_{(i)}
has property (b).

It now remains to verify by induction that
A_{(i+1)}
is indeed an almost perfect
A^{\prime}
-module for all
i\geq-1
. For
i=-1
, this holds by hypothesis and for
i\geq 0
we constructed
A_{(i+1)}
as an iterated extension of
A_{(i)}
by coherent
B
-modules, all of which are in
\mathrm{APerf}_{A}
by inductive hypothesis.

Finally, let us show that if
A^{\prime}\to A
is a map in
\mathrm{Art}_{{R}/B}
, then
A\in\mathrm{APerf}_{A^{\prime}}
. To this end, note that
A\to B
exhibits
B
as an almost perfect
A
-module because
A\to B
is an Artinian extension. The previous part of the proof therefore shows that
A
is an iterated square zero extension of
B
by coherent
B
-modules. Because
A^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Art}_{{R}/B}
, these are all almost perfect as
A^{\prime}
-modules, so that
A\in\mathrm{APerf}_{A^{\prime}}
as well.
∎

Corollary 4.4
.

The subcategory
\mathrm{Art}_{{R}/B}\subset\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{{R}/B}
is the smallest full subcategory satisfying the following two conditions:

(1)

It contains the terminal object
B\to B
.

(2)

Given
M\in\mathrm{Coh}_{B,\geq 0}
and a pullback square exhibiting
A_{\eta}
as the square zero extension of
A
by
M

(9)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{\eta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B\oplus M[1]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta}

we have that
A_{\eta}\in\mathrm{Art}_{{R}/B}
as soon as
A\in\mathrm{Art}_{{R}/B}
.

Furthermore, for each diagram
A_{1}\to A_{0}\leftarrow A_{2}
in
\mathrm{Art}_{{R}/B}
where one arrow is a nilpotent extension, the pullback is again contained in
\mathrm{Art}_{{R}/B}
.

Proof.

The first assertion follows from
Proposition

4.3
applied to
A=B
: this shows that
A^{\prime}\to B
is an Artinian extension if and only if it decomposes into a finite sequence of square zero extensions by connective coherent
B
-modules. The second assertion follows from
Proposition

4.3
by decomposing the nilpotent extension into square zero extensions by coherent
B
-modules and using the first assertion.

∎

Corollary 4.5
.

Let
B
be a coherent animated ring and let
A\to B
be an Artinian extension. Then
A
is coherent as well.

Proof.

By
Proposition

4.3
, it suffices to treat the case where
A
is a square zero extension of
B
by a coherent
B
-module. In this case,
Lemma

2.20
implies that the truncation of an almost perfect
A
-module remains almost perfect.
∎

Corollary 4.6
.

Let
B
be a coherent animated ring and let
A\to A^{\prime}
be a map of Artinian extensions of
B
. Then
A^{\prime}
is almost finitely presented as an animated
A
-algebra.

Proof.

By Proposition
A.19
, it suffices to verify that
A^{\prime}
is an almost perfect
A
-module. Since
A^{\prime}
arises from
B
by square zero extensions by coherent
B
-modules, it suffices to verify that
B
is almost perfect as an
A
-module, which holds by assumption (and Proposition
A.19
).
∎

The
\infty
-category of almost Artinian extensions
\mathrm{AArt}_{{R}/B}
is essentially obtained from the
\infty
-category of Artinian extensions by adding limits of relative Postnikov towers.

Lemma 4.7
.

Let
\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{sur}}_{{R}/B}\subseteq\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{{R}/B}
denote the full subcategory of animated surjections
A\to B
. For each
(A\to B)\in\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{sur}}_{{R}/B}
, there exists an initial map
(A\to B)\to(\tau_{\leq n}(A/B)\to B)
to an animated surjection with
n
-coconnective fibre.

We will refer to
\tau_{\leq n}(A/B)
as the
fibrewise
n
-truncation
of
A
over
B
.

Proof.

Consider the full subcategory
\mathscr{C}\subset\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{{R}})
spanned by the animated surjections, and recall that
\mathscr{C}
has compact projective generators of the form
R[x_{1},\dots,x_{n+m}]\to R[x_{1},\dots,x_{n}]
. Using this, one sees that an object
(A\to B)
of
\mathscr{C}
is
n
-truncated if and only if
A
and
\fib(A\to B)
are
n
-truncated (which implies that
B
is
(n+1)
-truncated). Furthermore, each animated surjection
(A\to B)
admits an
n
-truncation
\tau_{\leq n}(A\to B)
whose domain and fibre are
\tau_{\leq n}A
and
\tau_{\leq n}\fib(A\to B)
; write
\tau^{\prime}_{\leq n}B
for the codomain, which need not be the
n
-truncation of
B
. Given an animated surjection
A\to B
, we then define

\tau_{\leq n}(A/B)=\tau_{\leq n}A\times_{\tau^{\prime}_{\leq n}B}B.

This determines a functor
\tau_{\leq n}\colon\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{sur}}_{{R}/B}\to\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{sur}}_{{R}/B}
equipped with a natural transformation
\id\to\tau_{\leq n}
. By construction, the map
\fib(A\to B)\to\fib(\tau_{\leq n}(A/B)\to B)
exhibits its target as the
n
-truncation of the domain. In particular, this implies that
\tau_{\leq n}
is a localisation and that the
\tau_{\leq n}
-local objects are the animated surjections with
n
-coconnective fibre.
∎

Lemma 4.8
.

Let
A\to B
be an animated surjection. Then the following are equivalent:

(1)

A\to B
is an almost Artinian extension.

(2)

For each
n\geq 0
, the fibrewise
n
-truncation
\tau_{\leq n}(A/B)\to B
is an Artinian extension.

Consequently,
\mathrm{AArt}_{B/{R}}\subseteq\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{{R}/B}
is closed under square zero extensions by almost perfect connective
B
-modules.

Proof.

Note that
\pi_{i}(A)\cong\pi_{i}(\tau_{\leq n}(A/B))
for all
i\leq n
.
Assuming (2), it therefore follows that
A\to B
is a nilpotent extension and that
A
is a coherent animated ring. Since each
\pi_{i}(B)
is a finitely presented discrete module over
\pi_{0}(A)\cong\pi_{0}(\tau_{\leq 0}(A/B))
, we conclude that
B
is an almost perfect
A
-module, so that
A\to B
is almost finitely presented.

Assuming (1), the argument from
Proposition

4.3
shows that there exists a tower
\dots\to A_{n}\to A_{n-1}\to\dots\to B
of Artinian extensions such that
\fib(A\to A_{n})
becomes increasingly connective. In particular, choosing
n
large enough we see that
\pi_{0}(A)\cong\pi_{0}(A_{n})
is a coherent ring and that each
\pi_{i}(A)\cong\pi_{i}(A_{n})
is a finitely presented
\pi_{0}(A)
-module. It follows that
A
is coherent, so that each truncation
\tau_{\leq n}\fib(A\to B)
is an almost perfect
A
-module. Using this and
Proposition

2.14
, one sees that each
A\to\tau_{\leq n}(A/B)
is almost finitely presented. It follows that
\tau_{\leq n}(A/B)\to B
is almost finitely presented, and it is clearly a nilpotent extension with
n
-coconnective fibre.
∎

Definition 4.9
.

Let
\mathrm{Art}_{{R}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{AArt}_{{R}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{{R}})
denote the subcategories whose:

(1)

objects are (almost) Artinian extensions
A\to B
where
B
is coherent.

(2)

morphisms
(\tilde{f},f)\colon(A\to B)\to(A^{\prime}\to B^{\prime})
are commuting squares in which
f
is étale.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

Proposition 4.10
.

Let
\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{coh},\mathrm{et}}_{{R}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{{R}}
denote the subcategory of coherent animated
{R}
-algebras and étale maps between them. Then the codomain projections define cocartesian fibrations

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Art}_{{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{coh},\mathrm{et}}_{{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{AArt}_{{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{coh},\mathrm{et}}_{{R}}.}

Furthermore, for each étale map
f\colon B\to B^{\prime}
, the induced functor
f^{*}\colon\mathrm{AArt}_{{R}/B}\to\mathrm{AArt}_{{R}/B^{\prime}}
preserves pullbacks along nilpotent extensions, as well as limits of fibrewise Postnikov towers.

Proof.

A commuting square as in
Definition

4.9
defines a locally cocartesian arrow in
\mathrm{AArt}_{{R}}
if
A\to A^{\prime}
is étale. Indeed, any map of Artinian extensions
(A\to B)\to(A^{\prime\prime}\to B^{\prime})
covering the map
B\to B^{\prime}
factors uniquely over
(A^{\prime}\to B^{\prime})
because the étale map
A\to A^{\prime}
has the unique lifting property against the nilpotent extension
A^{\prime\prime}\to B^{\prime}
. It is clear that these types of locally cocartesian arrows are stable under composition.

It therefore remains to verify that for every Artinian extension
(A\to B)
and every étale map
f\colon B\to B^{\prime}
, there exists a lift
(\tilde{f},f)\colon(A\to B)\to(A^{\prime}\to B^{\prime})
such that
\tilde{f}\colon A\to A^{\prime}
is étale. For every animated ring
B
, the functor
B^{\prime}\mapsto\pi_{0}(B)\otimes_{B}B^{\prime}
defines an equivalence between the categories of étale
B
-algebras and étale
\pi_{0}(B)
-algebras. In turn, the functor
B^{\prime\prime}\mapsto B^{\prime\prime}\otimes_{\pi_{0}(B)}\pi_{0}(B)/I
determines an equivalence between the (ordinary) categories of étale
\pi_{0}(B)
-algebras and
\pi_{0}(B)/I
-algebras whenever
I
is a nilpotent ideal
[
Sta19
, Tag 039R]
. Using this, one sees that there is a unique lift of
f
to an étale map
\tilde{f}\colon A\to A^{\prime}
.

To see that the resulting map
A^{\prime}\to B^{\prime}
is (almost) Artinian, note that it is almost of finite presentation since both
A^{\prime}
and
B^{\prime}
are almost of finite presentation over
A
. Furthermore,
\ker(\pi_{0}(A^{\prime})\to\pi_{0}(B^{\prime}))\cong\pi_{0}(A^{\prime})\otimes_{\pi_{0}(A)}\ker(\pi_{0}(A)\to\pi_{0}(A^{\prime}))
is the base change of a nilpotent ideal along a flat map, and hence a nilpotent ideal itself. Likewise,
\fib(A^{\prime}\to B^{\prime})\simeq A^{\prime}\otimes_{A}\fib(A\to B)
is eventually coconnective if
\fib(A\to B)
is eventually coconnective, as
A\to A^{\prime}
is flat.

Finally, consider the change of fibre functor
f^{*}\colon\mathrm{AArt}_{B/{R}}\to\mathrm{AArt}_{B^{\prime}/{R}}
. For any map
A_{1}\to A_{2}
of (almost) Artinian extensions of
B
, we have that
A_{i}\to f^{*}(A_{i})
is the unique étale map covering
f
. This implies that
f^{*}(A_{2})\simeq f^{*}(A_{1})\otimes_{A_{1}}A_{2}
. Using this, one readily verifies that
f^{*}
preserves pullbacks along nilpotent extensions. Furthermore, the fact that
A\to f^{*}(A)
is flat implies that
f^{*}(A)\otimes_{A}\tau_{\leq n}(A/B)\simeq\tau_{\leq n}(f^{*}(A)/B^{\prime})
, so that
f^{*}
preserves limits of fibrewise Postnikov towers as well.
∎

4.2.
Formal moduli problems

We recall the notion of a formal moduli problem
[
SAG
, Chapter 12]
:

Definition 4.11
.

A functor
X\colon\mathrm{Art}_{{R}/B}\to\mathscr{S}
is a
formal moduli problem
if it satisfies the following two conditions:

(1)

X(B)\simeq\ast
.

(2)

For any pullback square in
\mathrm{Art}_{{R}/B}
of the form

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{01}}

where
A_{0}\to A_{01}
is a nilpotent extension,
X(A)\to X(A_{0})\times_{X(A_{01})}X(A_{1})
is an equivalence.

We will write
\mathrm{Moduli}_{B/{R}}
for the
\infty
-category of formal moduli problems.

Remark 4.12
.

Using
Proposition

4.3
, one sees that
X\colon\mathrm{Art}_{{R}/B}\to\mathscr{S}
is a formal moduli problem if and only if
X(B)\simeq\ast
and
X
preserves each pullback square (
9
) describing a square zero extension of
A\in\mathrm{Art}_{{R}/B}
by a coherent connective
B
-module.

Example 4.13
.

Let
B^{\prime}\to B
be a map of derived
R
-algebras. We will write
\mathrm{Spf}(B^{\prime})
for the corresponding
corepresentable formal moduli problem

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Spf}(B^{\prime})\colon\mathrm{Art}_{{R}/B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{S};\quad A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map_{\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{der}}_{{R}/B}}(B^{\prime},A).}

This defines the right adjoint in an adjoint pair

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}\colon\mathrm{Moduli}_{B/{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{der},\mathrm{op}}_{{R}/B}\penalty\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\mskip 2.0mu\mathrm{Spf}}

where
\mathcal{O}(X)
can be viewed as the global sections of the formal moduli problem
X
.

Lemma 4.14
.

Restriction along
i\colon\mathrm{Art}_{{R}/B}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{AArt}_{{R}/B}
defines an equivalence between
\mathrm{Moduli}_{B/{R}}
and the
\infty
-category of functors
X\colon\mathrm{AArt}_{{R}/B}\to\mathscr{S}
satisfying the following conditions:

(1)

X(B)\simeq\ast
.

(2)

X
preserves pullbacks along nilpotent extensions in
\mathrm{AArt}_{{R}/B}
.

(3)

X
preserves limits of fibrewise Postnikov towers.

Proof.

Lemma

4.8
implies that for any functor
Y\colon\mathrm{Art}_{{R}/B}\to\mathscr{S}
, its right Kan extension along
i
is given by
i_{*}Y(A)=\lim_{n}Y(\tau_{\leq n}(A/B))
. The result follows readily from this.
∎

Recall that every formal moduli problem has a tangent complex:

Construction 4.15
.

Let
\mathrm{Der}^{\mathrm{coh}}_{B/{R},\geq 0}
denote the
\infty
-category of derivations
\alpha\colon L_{B/{R}}[-1]\to M
where
M
is a connective coherent
B
-module.

Given a formal moduli problem
X\colon\mathrm{Art}_{{R}/B}\to\mathscr{S}
, write

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{B/X}[1]\colon\mathrm{Der}^{\mathrm{coh}}_{B/{R},\geq 0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{S};\quad(M,\alpha)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(B\oplus_{\alpha}M)}

for the restriction of
X
to the square zero extensions of
B
. Because
X
is a formal moduli problem, this functor preserves pullbacks of
\pi_{0}
-surjections. Consequently,
Proposition

3.15
and
Lemma

A.42
(or Example
4.25
below) imply that
T_{B/X}[1]
is classified by an object of
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{B/T_{B/{R}}[1]}
that we will refer to as the
tangent fibre
of the formal moduli problem
X
, and denote by
T_{B/X}[1]
as well.

Example 4.16
.

Let
B^{\prime}\to B
be a map of derived
R
-algebras and let
\mathrm{Spf}(B^{\prime})
be the associated corepresentable formal moduli problem (Example
4.13
). The tangent fibre of
\mathrm{Spf}(B^{\prime})
is then given by the functor
\mathrm{Der}^{\mathrm{coh}}_{B/{R},\geq 0}\to\mathscr{S}
corepresented by the cotangent complex
L_{B/{R}}[-1]\to L_{B/B^{\prime}}[-1]
. Consequently,
T_{B/\mathrm{Spf}(B^{\prime})}\simeq L^{\vee}_{B/B^{\prime}}[1]
is the pro-coherent dual of the cotangent fibre.

Finally, we record the functoriality of the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{Moduli}_{B/{R}}
with respect to étale maps:

Construction 4.17
.

Write
\mathscr{A}\to\mathscr{B}
for the cocartesian fibration
\mathrm{Art}_{{R}}\to\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{coh},\mathrm{et}}_{{R}}
from
Proposition

4.10
and consider the relative functor
\infty
-category
\mathrm{Fun}_{/\mathscr{B}}(\mathscr{A},\mathscr{S})\to\mathscr{B}
. Recall that this is the
\infty
-category over
\mathscr{B}
defined by the universal property that functors
\mathscr{D}\to\mathrm{Fun}_{/\mathscr{B}}(\mathscr{A},\mathscr{S})
are naturally equivalent to pairs consisting of a functor
\mathscr{D}\to\mathscr{B}
and a functor
\mathscr{A}\times_{\mathscr{B}}\mathscr{D}\to\mathscr{S}
. Then projection
\mathrm{Fun}_{/\mathscr{B}}(\mathscr{A},\mathscr{S})\to\mathscr{B}
is a cartesian and cocartesian fibration, classifying the functor sending
B\mapsto\mathrm{Fun}(\mathrm{Art}_{{R}/B},\mathscr{S})
and a map
f\colon B\to B^{\prime}
to restriction and left Kan extension along
\mathrm{Art}_{{R}/B}\to\mathrm{Art}_{{R}/B^{\prime}}

[
GHN17
, Proposition 7.3]
. We write

\mathrm{Moduli}_{/{R}}\subseteq\mathrm{Fun}_{/\mathscr{B}}(\mathscr{A},\mathscr{S})

for the full subcategory spanned by the tuples
(B,X\colon\mathrm{Art}_{{R}/B}\to\mathscr{S})
where
X
is a formal moduli problem. Since each
\mathrm{Art}_{{R}/B}\to\mathrm{Art}_{{R}/B^{\prime}}
preserves pullbacks along nilpotent extensions, the projection
\mathrm{Moduli}_{/{R}}\to\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{coh},\mathrm{et}}_{{R}}
is a cartesian and cocartesian fibration.

4.3.
Deformation theory

Let
B
be a coherent animated
{R}
-algebra. Our goal will be to show that taking the tangent complex of a formal moduli problem (Construction
4.15
) refines to an equivalence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{B/-}[1]\colon\mathrm{Moduli}_{B/{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

In fact, we will construct a version of this equivalence that is functorial in
B
with respect to étale maps. To this end, we introduce the following notation:

Notation 4.18
.

Consider the functor
\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{coh},\mathrm{et}}_{{R}}\to\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
sending
B\mapsto\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}}
and sending each étale map
f\colon B\to B^{\prime}
to
f^{*}\colon\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}}\to\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,B^{\prime}/{R}}
. This classifies a cartesian fibration and a cocartesian fibration that we will denote by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p\colon\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi,\mathrm{et}}_{\Delta/{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{coh},\mathrm{et},\mathrm{op}}_{{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{\vee}\colon\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\Delta/{R}}_{\pi}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{coh},\mathrm{et}}_{{R}}.}

More precisely,
p
is the restriction of the cartesian fibration from
Proposition

3.25
to the subcategory of étale maps, and we define
p^{\vee}
to be the cocartesian fibration classified by the same diagram of
\infty
-categories. Part (2) of
Proposition

3.25
implies that
p^{\vee}
is a cartesian fibration as well.

Theorem 4.19
.

There is an equivalence of
\infty
-categories

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\Delta/{R}}_{\pi,\mathrm{et}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Moduli}_{/{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{coh},\mathrm{et}}_{{R}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

Furthermore, the induced equivalence between fibres over a coherent animated
{R}
-algebra
B
fits into a commuting diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Moduli}_{B/{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{B/T_{B/{R}}[1]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}\big(\mathrm{Der}^{\mathrm{coh}}_{B/{R},\geq 0},\mathscr{S}\big).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\forget}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{B/-}[1]}

We will first construct the functor
\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\Delta/{R}}_{\pi,\mathrm{et}}\to\mathrm{Moduli}_{/{R}}
and then use the language of deformation theories from
[
SAG
, Chapter 12]
to prove that it is an equivalence.

Construction 4.20
.

If an animated surjection
A\to B
is an (almost) Artinian extension, then it is in particular complete almost finitely augmented (
Definition

2.15
). Using Corollary
3.13
, we thus obtain a composite functor

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{D}\colon\mathrm{Art}_{{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Coalg}_{\mathrm{coLie}^{\pi}_{\Delta}}(\mathrm{Der}_{/{R},\geq 0}^{\mathrm{aperf}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,/{R}})^{\mathrm{op}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\vee}}

sending each Artinian extension
A\to B
to the partition Lie algebroid
\mathfrak{D}(A)=\cot(A)^{\vee}
over
B
. Since the maps in
\mathrm{Art}_{{R}}
all cover étale maps
B\to B^{\prime}
, this assembles into a functor

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Art}_{{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{LieAlgd}_{\Delta/{R}}^{\pi,\mathrm{et}})^{\mathrm{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{coh},\mathrm{et}}_{{R}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{D}}

The functor
\mathfrak{D}
preserves cocartesian arrows: indeed, for any cocartesian arrow
(A\to B)\to(A^{\prime}\to B^{\prime})
in
\mathrm{Art}_{{R}}
covering an étale map
f\colon B\to B^{\prime}
, we have that
B^{\prime}\simeq A^{\prime}\otimes_{A}B
so that
\mathfrak{D}(A^{\prime})\simeq f^{*}\mathfrak{D}(A)
.
The functor
\mathfrak{D}
induces a fully faithful functor on fibres by Corollary
3.13
and is hence fully faithful itself, since it preserves cocartesian arrows.

Proposition 4.21
.

Let
B
be a coherent animated ring and let
A\to B\oplus M[1]
be a map in
\mathrm{Art}_{{R}/B}
classifying a square zero extension
A_{\eta}\to A
by a coherent connective
B
-module. Then induced map of partition Lie algebroids over
B

(10)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{D}(A)\amalg_{\mathfrak{D}(B\oplus M[1])}\mathfrak{D}(B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{D}(A_{\eta})}

is an equivalence.

Lemma 4.22
.

Let
B
be a coherent animated ring and let
F^{\star}M\in\mathrm{FilMod}_{B}
be an almost perfect filtered
B
-module. Then the diagram of pro-coherent duals

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(F^{0}M/F^{1}M)^{\vee}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(F^{0}M/F^{2}M)^{\vee}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(F^{0}M)^{\vee}}

is a colimit diagram in
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{B}
.

Proof.

Recall that the pro-coherent dual of a
B
-module
M
can be described by the left exact functor
\mathrm{Coh}_{B}\to\mathscr{S}
corepresented by
M
. It therefore suffices to verify that for any coherent
B
-module
N
, the map
\colim\Map_{B}(F^{0}M/F^{n}M,N)\to\Map_{B}(F^{0}M,N)
is an equivalence of spaces. Suppose that
N
is
k
-coconnective. Because
F^{\star}M
is almost perfect, there exists an
m
such that
F^{n}M
is
k
-connective for all
n\geq m
. It follows that
\Map_{B}(F^{0}M/F^{n}M,N)\to\Map_{B}(F^{0}M,N)
is an equivalence for all
n\geq m
.
∎

Proof of
Proposition

4.21
.

Let us say that an object
A\in\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{der},\wedge,\mathrm{aft}}_{{R}/B}
is
adequate
if for every
N\in\mathrm{APerf}_{B,\geq 0}
, the natural map

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{D}(A)\sqcup\mathfrak{D}(B\oplus N)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{D}(A\oplus N)}

is an equivalence, where
B\oplus N
and
A\oplus N=A\times_{B}(B\oplus N)
denote the trivial square zero extensions by
N
. To prove the proposition, it will suffice to verify the following assertions:

(1)

Every square zero extension
B\oplus_{\alpha}M
of
B
by
M\in\mathrm{APerf}_{B,\geq 0}
is adequate.

(2)

Suppose that
A
is adequate and consider a pullback square (
9
) classifying a square zero extension
A_{\eta}\to A
by
M\in\mathrm{APerf}_{B,\geq 0}
. Then the following hold:

(2a)

the map (
10
) is an equivalence.

(2b)

A_{\eta}
is adequate.

Indeed, Corollary
4.4
then shows by induction that every
A\in\mathrm{Art}_{{R}/B}
is adequate, so that
\mathfrak{D}
sends every pullback square (
9
) to a pushout of partition Lie algebroids.

Recall that for any square zero extension
B\oplus_{\alpha}M
classified by
\alpha\colon L_{B/{R}}[-1]\to M
, the partition Lie algebroid
\mathfrak{D}(B\oplus_{\alpha}M)
is the
free
partition Lie algebroid on
\alpha^{\vee}\colon M^{\vee}\to T_{B/{R}}[1]
. Consequently,
\mathfrak{D}((B\oplus_{\alpha}M)\oplus N)
is the free partition Lie algebroid on
(\alpha^{\vee},0)\colon M^{\vee}\oplus N\to T_{B/{R}}[1]
. Assertion (1) then follows from the fact that taking free Lie algebroids is a left adjoint.

We will simultaneously verify assertions (2a) and (2b). To this end, suppose that
A
is adequate and let
A_{\eta}\to A
be a square zero extension of
A
classified by
\alpha\colon A\to B\oplus M[1]
. Let
A\to A^{\bullet}\to B\oplus M[1]
be the cosimplicial resolution provided by Corollary
2.30
. Given
N\in\mathrm{APerf}_{B,\geq 0}
, we then obtain a diagram of complete almost finitely augmented algebras over
B

(11)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{\eta}\oplus N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{\bullet}_{\eta}\oplus N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B\oplus N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{\bullet}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B\oplus M[1].}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{id},0)}

where the top row is obtained from the bottom row by base change. This gives rise to a diagram of partition Lie algebroids of the form

(12)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big|\mathfrak{D}(A^{\bullet})\amalg_{\mathfrak{D}(B\oplus M[1])}\mathfrak{D}(B\oplus N)\big|}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{D}(A)\amalg_{\mathfrak{D}(B\oplus M[1])}\mathfrak{D}(B\oplus N)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big|\mathfrak{D}(A_{\eta}^{\bullet}\oplus N)\big|}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{D}(A_{\eta}\oplus N).}

It suffices to verify that the right vertical map is an equivalence: if this is the case, then taking
N=0
yields assertion (2a) and assertion (2b) then follows from the equivalences

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(\mathfrak{D}(A)\amalg_{\mathfrak{D}(B\oplus M[1])}\mathfrak{D}(B)\big)\amalg\mathfrak{D}(B\oplus N)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{D}(A)\amalg_{\mathfrak{D}(B\oplus M[1])}\mathfrak{D}(B\oplus N)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{D}(A_{\eta})\amalg\mathfrak{D}(B\oplus N)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{D}(A_{\eta}\oplus N).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

To see that the right vertical map in (
12
) is an equivalence, we will show that each of the other three maps is an equivalence.

Top horizontal map.
To see that the top horizontal map in (
12
) is an equivalence, it suffices to show that the map
|\mathfrak{D}(A^{\bullet})|\to\mathfrak{D}(A)
is an equivalence. Since geometric realisations of partition Lie algebroids are computed in
(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{B})_{/T_{B/{R}}[1]}
, it suffices to verify this at the level of pro-coherent modules. Now recall that the cosimplicial resolution
A\to A^{\bullet}
from Corollary
2.30
has the property that the augmented cosimplicial diagram
\cot(A)\to\cot(A^{\bullet})
in
\mathrm{Der}_{B/{R},\geq 0}^{\mathrm{aperf}}
is split. Taking pro-coherent duals, one obtains that the augmented simplicial diagram
\mathfrak{D}(A^{\bullet})\to\mathfrak{D}(A)
is split in
(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{B})_{/T_{B/{R}}[1]}
, so that
|\mathfrak{D}(A^{\bullet})|\simeq\mathfrak{D}(A)
.

Left vertical map.
It suffices to verify that each square of partition Lie algebroids

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{D}(B\oplus M[1])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{D}(B\oplus N)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{D}(A^{i})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{D}(A^{i}_{\eta}\oplus N)}

is a pushout. The cosimplicial resolution
A^{\bullet}
from Corollary
2.30
has the property that
A^{i}\simeq B\oplus_{\alpha}K
is a square zero extension of
B
and that the map
A^{i}\simeq B\oplus_{\alpha}K\to B\oplus M[1]
arises from a map
e\colon K\to M[1]
in
\mathrm{Der}_{B/{R},\geq 0}^{\mathrm{aperf}}
. It follows that the above square is the image of a pushout square in
(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{B})_{/T_{B/K}[1]}
under the free partition Lie algebroid functor, and hence a pushout square itself.

Bottom horizontal map.
It suffices to check that the augmented simplicial diagram
\mathfrak{D}(A^{\bullet}_{\eta}\oplus N)\to\mathfrak{D}(A_{\eta}\oplus N)
is a colimit diagram in
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{B}
. Recall that this is the pro-coherent dual of the augmented cosimplicial diagram of
B
-modules
\cot(A_{\eta}\oplus N)\to\cot(A_{\eta}^{\bullet}\oplus N)
. We will refine this to a diagram of filtered
B
-modules as follows. The diagram (
11
) of derived
{R}
-algebras over
B
lifts to a diagram of filtered
{R}
-algebras over
B
: we endow
B\oplus N
,
A
and
A^{\bullet}
with the filtration where
F^{i}=0
for all
i\geq 1
, and
B\oplus M[1]
with the filtration

F^{1}(B\oplus M[1])=M[1]\qquad\qquad F^{i}(B\oplus M[1])=0\qquad\text{for }i\geq 2.

Taking fibre products, we then obtain (finite) filtrations on
A_{\eta}\oplus N
and
A^{\bullet}_{\eta}\oplus N
.

The maps of animated filtered algebras
F^{\star}(A_{\eta}\oplus N)\to B
and
F^{\star}(A_{\eta}^{i}\oplus N)\to B
are almost of finite presentation. Indeed, each
A^{i}\to B
(concentrated in weight
0
) is almost of finite presentation and
A_{\eta}^{i}\oplus N\to A^{i}
is a square zero extension by an almost perfect filtered
A^{i}
-module, and thus almost of finite presentation by
Lemma

2.20
. By
Proposition

2.14
, we therefore obtain an augmented cosimplicial diagram of almost perfect filtered
B
-modules

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cot(F^{\star}(A_{\eta}\oplus N))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cot(F^{\star}(A_{\eta}^{\bullet}\oplus N)).}

At the level of
F^{0}
, this is given by
\cot(A_{\eta}\oplus N)\to\cot(A_{\eta}^{\bullet}\oplus N)
and at the level of the associated graded, it is given by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cot(A\oplus M(1)\oplus N)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cot(A^{\bullet}\oplus M(1)\oplus N).}

Here
A\oplus M(1)\oplus N
denotes the derived graded
{R}
-algebra given by the trivial square zero extension of
A
by
N
(in weight
0
) and
M
(in weight
1
).

Using
Lemma

4.22
, we therefore obtain a certain augmented simplicial diagram of pro-coherent
B
-modules equipped with an
increasing
filtration

(13)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{\star}\mathfrak{D}(A_{\eta}^{\bullet}\oplus N)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{\star}\mathfrak{D}(A_{\eta}\oplus N)}

At the level of the underlying objects (i.e. taking the colimit as
\star\to\infty
), this yields the augmented simplicial diagram
\mathfrak{D}(A^{\bullet}_{\eta}\oplus N)\to\mathfrak{D}(A_{\eta}\oplus N)
. To see that this is a colimit diagram, it now suffices to show that (
13
) is a colimit diagram at the level of the associated graded. The associated graded of (
13
) is equivalent to the augmented simplicial diagram (with a certain weight grading that will not play a role)

\mathfrak{D}(A^{\bullet}\oplus M\oplus N)\to\mathfrak{D}(A\oplus M\oplus N).

Since
A
and all
A^{i}
(which are of the form
B\oplus_{\alpha}I
) are adequate, this is equivalent to the map

\mathfrak{D}(A^{\bullet})\amalg\mathfrak{D}(B\oplus M\oplus N)\to\mathfrak{D}(A)\amalg\mathfrak{D}(B\oplus M\oplus N).

This is an equivalence because (as we already saw),
|\mathfrak{D}(A^{\bullet})|\simeq\mathfrak{D}(A)
.
∎

By
Proposition

4.21
, any partition Lie algebroid
\mathfrak{g}
over
B
determines a formal moduli problem

(14)

\Psi_{\mathfrak{g}}\colon\mathrm{Art}_{{R}/B}\to\mathscr{S};\quad\Psi_{\mathfrak{g}}(A)=\Map_{\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}}}(\mathfrak{D}(A),\mathfrak{g}).

Using this, we will now construct the putative equivalence of
Theorem

4.19
.

Construction 4.23
.

Consider the functor

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Art}_{{R}}\times_{\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{coh},\mathrm{et}}_{{R}}}\mathrm{LieAlgd}_{\pi,\mathrm{et}}^{\Delta/{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi,\mathrm{et}}_{\Delta/{R}})^{\mathrm{op}}\times_{\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{coh},\mathrm{et}}_{{R}}}\mathrm{LieAlgd}_{\pi,\mathrm{et}}^{\Delta/{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathfrak{D},\id)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map}

where the last functor is the fibrewise mapping space functor. Using Construction
4.17
, this is adjoint to a functor
\mathrm{LieAlgd}_{\pi,\mathrm{et}}^{\Delta/{R}}\to\mathrm{Fun}_{/\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{coh},\mathrm{et}}_{{R}}}(\mathrm{Art}_{{R}},\mathscr{S})
. Unravelling the definitions, this sends each
\mathfrak{g}\in\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}}
to the formal moduli problem
\Psi_{\mathfrak{g}}
from (
14
). We thus obtain a functor

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\Delta/{R}}_{\pi,\mathrm{et}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Moduli}_{/{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{coh},\mathrm{et}}_{{R}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi}

Because
\mathfrak{D}
preserved cocartesian fibrations, the functor
\Psi
is a map of cartesian fibrations that preserves cartesian arrows.

It now remains to verify that
\Psi
restricts to an equivalence on fibres. For this we will use Lurie’s formalism of deformation theories, slightly reformulated as follows:

Theorem 4.24

(cf.
[
SAG
, Theorem 12.3.3.5]
)
.

Let
\mathscr{B}
be a presentable
\infty
-category equipped with a set of adjunctions
L_{\alpha}\colon\mathrm{Sp}\leftrightarrows\mathscr{B}\penalty\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\mskip 2.0muR_{\alpha}
such that each
R_{\alpha}
preserves sifted colimits and the
R_{\alpha}
are jointly conservative. Let
\mathscr{B}_{0}\subseteq\mathscr{B}
be the smallest full subcategory of
\mathscr{B}
satisfying the following conditions:

(1)

It contains the initial object
0
.

(2)

If
K\in\mathscr{B}_{0}
, the for each pushout square in
\mathscr{B}
of the form

(15)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{\alpha}(\mathbb{S}[n])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K^{\prime}}

for some
\alpha
and
n<0
, the object
K^{\prime}
is contained in
\mathscr{B}_{0}
as well.

Then the restricted Yoneda embedding
h\colon\mathscr{B}\to\mathscr{P}(\mathscr{B}_{0});K\longmapsto\Map_{\mathscr{B}}(-,K)
is fully faithful, with essential image consisting of those presheaves
X
on
\mathscr{B}_{0}
such that
X(0)\simeq\ast
and such that (
15
) is sent to a pullback diagram of spaces.

Proof.

Consider the adjoint pair
\mathfrak{D}^{*}\colon\mathscr{B}\leftrightarrows\mathscr{P}(\mathscr{B}_{0})^{\mathrm{op}}\penalty\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\mskip 2.0mu\mathfrak{D}_{*}
where
\mathfrak{D}^{*}(K)=\Map_{\mathscr{B}}(K,-)
. The conditions imply that this is a deformation theory in the sense of
[
SAG
, Definition 12.3.3.2]
, and the result follows from
[
SAG
, Theorem 12.3.3.5]
.
∎

Example 4.25
.

Let
B
be a coherent animated
{R}
-algebra. For each
M\in\mathrm{Coh}_{B,\geq 0}
, consider

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{M}\colon\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{B/T_{B/{R}}[1]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sp};\quad(\rho\colon N\to T_{B/{R}}[1])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}(B)}(M^{\vee},\fib(\rho)).}

This is a right adjoint functor preserving sifted colimits since
M^{\vee}\in\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{B}
is compact. Since the collection of
M^{\vee}
with
M\in\mathrm{Coh}_{B,\geq 0}
generates
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{B}
, this satisfies the conditions of
Theorem

4.24
. The full subcategory
\mathscr{B}^{\mathrm{lin}}_{0}\subseteq\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{B/T_{B/{R}}[1]}
appearing in
Theorem

4.24
can then be identified as follows. Note that

L_{M}(\mathbb{S})\simeq\big(0\colon M^{\vee}\to T_{B/{R}}[1]\big).

For any
\rho\colon N^{\vee}\to T_{B/{R}}[1]
with
N\in\mathrm{Coh}_{B,\geq 0}
, we then have that:

(a)

For any
n<0
and
M^{\vee}[n]\to N^{\vee}
, the cofibre
\fib(N\to M[-n])^{\vee}
is dual to a coherent connective
B
-module.

(b)

it can be obtained as a pushout of a diagram
0\leftarrow L_{N}(\mathbb{S}[-1])\to 0
in
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{B/T_{B/{R}}[1]}
.

It follows that
\mathscr{B}^{\mathrm{lin}}_{0}
is the full subcategory of
\rho\colon N^{\vee}\to T_{B/{R}}[1]
where
N^{\vee}
is the pro-coherent dual of a coherent connective
B
-module. Using
Lemma

2.16
, we can identify
\mathscr{B}^{\mathrm{lin},\mathrm{op}}_{0}\simeq\mathrm{Der}^{\mathrm{coh}}_{B/{R},\geq 0}
with the full subcategory of derivations
\alpha\colon L_{B/{R}}[-1]\to M
for which
M\in\mathrm{Coh}_{B,\geq 0}
. Consequently, there is a fully faithful functor

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{B/T_{B/{R}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}\big(\mathrm{Der}^{\mathrm{coh}}_{B/{R},\geq 0},\mathscr{S}\big)}

whose essential image consists of functors
X
such that
X(0)\simeq\ast
and preserving pullbacks along all maps
0\to(M[1],\alpha)
in
\mathrm{Der}^{\mathrm{coh}}_{B/{R},\geq 0}
with
M\in\mathrm{Coh}_{B,\geq 0}
.

Proof of
Theorem

4.19
.

We will mimic the strategy from Example
4.25
. For each
M\in\mathrm{Coh}_{B,\geq 0}
, consider the functor
