{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{M}\colon\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sp};\quad\big(\rho\colon\mathfrak{g}\to T_{B/{R}}[1]\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{B}}(M^{\vee},\fib(\rho)).}

Proposition

3.8
implies that the forgetful functor
\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}}\to\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{B/T_{B/{R}}[1]}
is a right adjoint that preserves sifted colimits and detects equivalences. Using this and Example
4.25
, it follows that the set of functors
R_{M}
satisfies the conditions of
Theorem

4.24
.

To identify the full subcategory
\mathscr{B}_{0}\subseteq\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}}
, note that
L_{M}(\mathbb{S})\simeq\Lie^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}}(0\colon M^{\vee}\to T_{B/{R}})
is the free partition Lie algebroid generated by the zero map from
M^{\vee}
. By
Proposition

3.8
, this means that there are natural equivalences

L_{M}(\mathbb{S})\simeq\Lie^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}}(0\colon M^{\vee}\to T_{B/{R}})\simeq\mathfrak{D}(B\oplus M)

for all
M\in\mathrm{Coh}_{B,\geq 0}
. It follows that
\mathscr{B}_{0}\subseteq\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}}
is the smallest full subcategory containing the initial partition Lie algebroid
0\simeq\mathfrak{D}
which is closed under pushouts along the maps
\mathfrak{D}(B\oplus M[1])\to\mathfrak{D}(B)
for all
M\in\mathrm{Coh}_{B,\geq 0}
. Corollary
4.4
and
Proposition

4.21
now imply that there is a commuting diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Der}^{\mathrm{coh}}_{B/{R},\geq 0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{B}_{0}^{\mathrm{lin},\mathrm{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{B/T_{B/{R}}[1]}\big)^{\mathrm{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Art}_{{R}/B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{B}^{\mathrm{op}}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}})^{\mathrm{op}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{sqz}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\vee}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lie^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{D}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

where equivalence
\mathfrak{D}
sends each pullback square (
9
) to the opposite of a pushout square of the form (
15
). Using this,
Theorem

4.24
then implies that
\Psi\colon\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\Delta/{R}}_{\pi,\mathrm{et}}\to\mathrm{Moduli}_{/{R}}
restricts to an equivalence
\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}}\simeq\mathrm{Moduli}_{B/{R}}
on fibres with the desired properties.
∎

Remark 4.26
.

Combining the equivalence of
Theorem

4.19
with the adjoint pair from Example
4.13
, we obtain an adjoint pair

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{*}\colon\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{der},\mathrm{op}}_{{R}/B}\penalty\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\mskip 2.0mu\mathfrak{D}}

where
\mathfrak{D}(B^{\prime})=T_{B/\mathrm{Spf}(B^{\prime})}
with the partition Lie algebroid structure arising from
Theorem

4.19
. Example
4.16
implies that the underlying object of
\mathfrak{D}(B^{\prime})
in
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{B/T_{B/{R}}[1]}
is naturally equivalent to
L_{B/B^{\prime}}^{\vee}[1]\to L_{B/{R}}^{\vee}[1]=T_{B/{R}}[1]
.

The right adjoint
C^{*}
can be thought of as a version of the
Chevalley–Eilenberg complex
for partition Lie algebroids. Explicitly,
C^{*}(\mathfrak{g})
is the derived algebra of functions on the formal moduli problem classified by
\mathfrak{g}
. One can now check that the adjoint pair
(C^{*},\mathfrak{D})
also defines a deformation theory in the sense of
[
SAG
, Definition 12.3.3.2]
.

Remark 4.27
.

Suppose that
X
is a locally coherent qcqs derived scheme. By Remark
3.30
, the
\infty
-category of partition Lie algebroids on
X
can be identified with the
\infty
-category of dotted sections

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Open}^{\mathrm{aff}}(X)^{\mathrm{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\Delta/{R}}_{\pi,\mathrm{et}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Moduli}_{/{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{coh},\mathrm{et}}_{{R}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{g}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{\vee}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

sending each map to a cocartesian arrow. Using
Theorem

4.19
and unravelling Construction
4.17
, one sees that the
\infty
-category of partition Lie algebroids on
X
is equivalent to the
\infty
-category of functors
F\colon\mathrm{Art}_{{R}}\times_{\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{coh},\mathrm{et}}_{{R}}}\mathrm{Open}^{\mathrm{aff}}(X)^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathscr{S}
satisfying the following two properties:

(1)

For each affine open
U\subseteq X
,
F
restricts to a formal moduli problem
F_{U}\colon\mathrm{Art}_{{R}/\mathcal{O}(U)}\to\mathscr{S}
.

(2)

For each inclusion of affine opens
V\subseteq U\subseteq X
,
F_{V}
is the initial formal moduli problem equipped with a map from
F_{U}
to its restriction along
\mathrm{Art}_{{R}/\mathcal{O}(U)}\to\mathrm{Art}_{{R}/\mathcal{O}(V)}
.

4.4.
Underlying partition Lie algebra

Suppose that
{R}
is coherent and that
B
is a coherent animated
{R}
-algebra. We will use
Theorem

4.19
to show that the underlying pro-coherent
{R}
-module of a partition Lie algebroid
\mathfrak{g}
over
B
can be endowed with a (
{R}
-linear) partition Lie algebra structure. To this end, we begin by recalling the following properties of the
\infty
-categories
\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{A}
of animated
A
-algebras.

Proposition 4.28
.

Given a cartesian square of animated rings on the left in which
f\colon A_{0}\to A_{01}
induces a surjection on
\pi_{0}
, the right square of
\infty
-categories is cartesian as well:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{01}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{A_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{A_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{A_{12}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{*}}

Proof.

Write
h\colon A\to A_{01}
for the composite map and consider the following diagram of
\infty
-categories:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{A_{0}}\times_{\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{A_{01}}}\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{A_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Mod_{A,\geq 0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Mod_{A_{0},\geq 0}\times_{\Mod_{A_{01},\geq 0}}\Mod_{A_{1},\geq 0}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(g^{\prime*},f^{\prime*})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\forget}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\forget}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(g^{\prime*},f^{\prime*})}

Here the rows are both adjoint pairs: the left adjoint
(g^{\prime*},f^{\prime*})
sends an animated
A
-algebra (resp.
A
-module)
B
to the matching triple of objects
(A_{0}\otimes_{A}B,\ A_{01}\otimes_{A}B,\ A_{1}\otimes_{A}B)
and the right adjoint sends a matching triple of animated algebras (resp. modules)
(B_{0},B_{01},B_{1})
to the fibre product
g^{\prime}_{*}B_{0}\times_{h_{*}B_{01}}f^{\prime}_{*}B_{1}
of
A
-algebras (resp.
A
-modules). In particular, the vertical forgetful functors commute both with the left adjoints and with the right adjoints. Since the forgetful functor detects equivalences, the unit and counit of the top adjunction are an equivalence if the unit and counit of the bottom adjunction are equivalences. This follows from
[
SAG
, Theorem 16.2.0.2]
, so that the top adjunction is an equivalence.
∎

Definition 4.29
.

Suppose that
{R}
is coherent and let
B\in\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{{R}}
be a
{R}
-algebra. We will write
\mathrm{Def}_{B/{R}}
for the full subcategory of
\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{{R}})_{/{R}\to B}
spanned by those commuting squares

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{R}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}

that are cocartesian and in which
A\to{R}
is an Artinian extension. We will typically write
A\to B_{A}
for an object in
\mathrm{Def}_{B/{R}}
, omitting the data of the cocartesian square.

There is a canonical functor
\mathrm{Def}_{B/{R}}\to\mathrm{Art}_{{R}/{R}}
sending each such square to
A\to{R}
. We then have the following corollary of
Proposition

4.28
:

Lemma 4.30
.

Suppose that
{R}
is coherent and let
B
be an animated
{R}
-algebra. Then the functor
\mathrm{Def}_{B/{R}}\to\mathrm{Art}_{{R}/{R}}
is a left fibration, classified by a formal moduli problem
\mathrm{def}_{B}\colon\mathrm{Art}_{{R}/{R}}\to\mathscr{S}
.

Proof.

The functor
\mathrm{Def}_{B/{R}}\to\mathrm{Art}_{{R}/{R}}
is a cocartesian fibration, classified by
\mathrm{def}_{B}\colon\mathrm{Art}_{{R}/{R}}\to\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
sending
A\mapsto\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{A}\times_{\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{{R}}}\{B\}
.
Proposition

4.28
implies that
\mathrm{def}_{B}
is a formal moduli problem. In particular,
\mathrm{def}_{B}({R}\oplus M)\simeq\Omega\mathrm{def}_{B}({R}\oplus M[1])
is a space for every coherent
{R}
-module
M
. By Artinian induction, this implies that
\mathrm{def}_{B}(A)
is a space for all
A
, so that the result follows.
∎

Notation 4.31
.

Write
\Gamma(T_{B/{R}}[1])\in\mathrm{LieAlg}^{\pi}_{\Delta,{R}}
for the partition Lie algebra classified by the formal moduli problem
\mathrm{def}_{B}
.

One can identify the complex underlying
\Gamma(T_{B/{R}}[1])
as follows:

Construction 4.32
.

Given a map of coherent animated rings
f\colon{R}\to B
, consider the functor
f^{*}\colon\mathrm{Coh}_{{R},\geq 0}\to\Mod_{B}
and define

\mathrm{Def}_{B/{R}}^{\mathrm{sqz}}=\mathrm{Coh}_{{R},\geq 0}\times_{\Mod_{B}}(\Mod_{B})_{L_{B/{R}}[-1]/}

to be the
\infty
-category of tuples
(M,\alpha)
where
M\in\mathrm{Coh}_{{R},\geq 0}
and
\alpha\colon L_{B/{R}}[-1]\to f^{*}M
is a derivation. This fits into a commuting square of
\infty
-categories

(16)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Def}_{B/{R}}^{\mathrm{sqz}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Def}_{B/{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Coh}_{{R},\geq 0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Art}_{{R}/{R}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{sqz}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\triv}

in which the vertical functors are left fibrations. The bottom horizontal functor sends
M\in\mathrm{Coh}_{{R},\geq 0}
to the trivial square zero extension
{R}\oplus M
and the top horizontal map sends
(M,\alpha)
to
({R}\oplus M\to B\oplus_{\alpha}f^{*}M)
. This indeed defines a deformation of
B
over
{R}\oplus M
: it is obtained as the pullback of the diagram of deformations

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({R}\to B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({R}\oplus M[1]\to B\oplus f^{*}M[1])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({R}\to B)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

where the left map arises from the derivation
\alpha\colon B\to B\oplus f^{*}M[1]
and the right map from the zero derivation. By the proof of
Proposition

4.28
, the fibre product then defines a deformation of
B
as well.

Lemma 4.33
.

Let
f\colon{R}\to B
be a map of coherent animated rings. Then there is an equivalence of pro-coherent
{R}
-modules
\Gamma(T_{B/{R}}[1])\simeq f_{*}T_{B/{R}}[1]
.

Proof.

By construction, the left fibration
\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{sqz}}_{B/{R}}\to\mathrm{Coh}_{{R},\geq 0}
is classified by the functor
\mathrm{Coh}_{{R},\geq 0}\to\mathscr{S}
sending
M
to the space of
B
-linear maps
\Map_{B}(L_{B/{R}}[-1],f^{*}M)
. By
Lemma

A.42
, this functor is classified by a pro-coherent
{R}
-module; unravelling the definitions using Example
A.32
and
Definition

A.37
, one sees that this pro-coherent
{R}
-module is precisely
f_{*}T_{B/{R}}[1]
.

It now suffices to verify that this pro-coherent
{R}
-module is equivalent to the module underlying the partition Lie algebra
\Gamma(T_{B/{R}}[1])
. In terms of formal moduli problems, this is equivalent to the square (
16
) being cartesian. Since the base change

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P=\mathrm{Def}_{B/{R}}\times_{\mathrm{Art}_{{R}/{R}}}\mathrm{Coh}_{{R},\geq 0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Coh}_{{R},\geq 0}}

is a left fibration classified by a reduced excisive functor
\mathrm{Coh}_{{R},\geq 0}\to\mathscr{S}
, it suffices to verify the following: for each
M\in\mathrm{Coh}_{{R},\geq 0}
, the induced map on fibres
\Map_{B}(L_{B/{R}}[-1],f^{*}M)\to P_{M}
induces an equivalence on loop spaces at the canonical (zero) basepoint. This map of loop spaces can be identified with the canonical map

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map_{B}(L_{B/{R}},f^{*}M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map_{\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{{R}\oplus M/B}}\big(B\oplus f^{*}M,B\oplus f^{*}M\big)\simeq\Map_{\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{{R}/B}}(B,B\oplus f^{*}M)}

which is an equivalence by definition of the cotangent complex.
∎

Remark 4.34
.

Suppose that
{R}
is coherent and let
\mathfrak{g}\in\mathrm{LieAlg}^{\pi}_{\Delta,{R}}
be a partition Lie algebra. Write
X\colon\mathrm{Art}_{{R}/{R}}\to\mathscr{S}
for the formal moduli problem classified by
\mathfrak{g}
and
\int X\to\mathrm{Art}_{{R}/{R}}
for its unstraightening. We then have fully faithful inclusions

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{LieAlg}^{\pi}_{\Delta,{R}})_{/\mathfrak{g}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{Moduli}_{{R}/{R}})_{/X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}(\mathrm{Art}_{{R}/{R}},\mathscr{S})_{/X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}(\int X,\mathscr{S}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

The last equivalence follows from the fact that, under unstraightening, both
\infty
-categories are equivalent to the
\infty
left fibrations over
\int X
. Unravelling the definitions, one sees that the essential image of the above inclusion consists of those functors
\int X\to\mathscr{S}
that preserve the terminal object, as well as every pullback square in
\int X
whose image in
\mathrm{Art}_{{R}/{R}}
is as in
Definition

4.11
.

Construction 4.35
.

Let
B
be a coherent
{R}
-algebra. Given an object
A\to B_{A}
in
\mathrm{Def}_{B/{R}}
, the map
B_{A}\to B
is an almost Artinian extension. Indeed, it is the base change of the almost finitely presented map
A\to{R}
, hence almost finitely presented, and the kernel of
\pi_{0}(B_{A})\to\pi_{0}(B)
is generated by the images of the (nilpotent) generators of the kernel of
\pi_{0}(A)\to\pi_{0}({R})
. We thus obtain a functor
t\colon\mathrm{Def}_{B/{R}}\to\mathrm{AArt}_{{R}/B}
given by
t(A\to B_{A})=B_{A}
. Now suppose that
A_{0}\to A_{01}
is a nilpotent extension and consider a pullback square in
\mathrm{Def}_{B/{R}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A\to B_{A})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A_{0}\to B_{A_{0}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A_{1}\to B_{A_{1}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A_{01}\to B_{A_{01}}).}

Then
B_{A_{0}}\to B_{A_{01}}
is a nilpotent extension as well, and the proof of
Proposition

4.28
shows that
B_{A}\simeq B_{A_{0}}\times_{B_{A_{01}}}B_{A_{1}}
. It follows that restriction along
t
preserves formal moduli problems. Using Remark
4.34
, we therefore obtain a functor

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma\colon\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Moduli}_{B/{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(\mathrm{Moduli}_{{R}/{R}}\big)_{/\mathrm{def}_{B}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(\mathrm{LieAlg}^{\pi}_{\Delta,{R}}\big)_{/\Gamma(T_{B/{R}}[1])}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

Proposition 4.36
.

Let
f\colon{R}\to B
be a map of coherent animated rings. Then there is a commuting diagram of right adjoint functors

(17)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,B/{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{LieAlg}^{\pi}_{\Delta,{R}})_{/\Gamma(T_{B/{R}}[1])}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{B})_{/T_{B/{R}}[1]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{R})_{/\Gamma(T_{B/{R}}[1])}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\forget}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}}

In particular,
\Gamma(T_{B/{R}}[1])\simeq f_{*}(T_{B/{R}}[1])
as pro-coherent
{R}
-modules.

Proof.

Consider the commuting square of
\infty
-categories

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Def}_{B/{R}}^{\mathrm{sqz}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Der}_{B/{R},\geq 0}^{\mathrm{aperf}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Def}_{B/{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{AArt}_{{R}/B}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}

where the top functor
t^{\prime}
sends
(M,\alpha\colon L_{B/{R}}[-1]\to f^{*}M)
to
(f^{*}M,\alpha)
. By restriction, this induces a commuting square of
\infty
-categories of formal moduli problems and right adjoint functors between them. Using the equivalences from Example
4.25
,
Theorem

4.19
and Remark
4.34
, this square of
\infty
-categories of formal moduli problems is equivalent to a commuting square of the form (
17
). Indeed, the functor
\Gamma
is equivalent to restriction along
t
by definition, and one readily verifies that restriction along the functor
t^{\prime}
sending
(M,\alpha)
to
(f^{*}M,\alpha)
corresponds to
f_{*}
at the level of pro-coherent modules.
∎

5.
Formally integrating partition Lie algebroids

Let
X
be a locally coherent qcqs derived scheme over a coherent ground ring
{R}
.
In this section, we will formally integrate partition Lie algebroids

\mathfrak{g}\rightarrow T_{X/{R}}[1]

on locally coherent qcqs derived schemes
X
over animated rings
{R}
.
The result of our integration procedure will be
formal moduli stacks

X\rightarrow Y

under
X
, where
Y
can be thought of as a formal leaf space.
More precisely, we define:

Definition 5.1

(Formal moduli stacks)
.

A
formal moduli stack
under
X
is a map of
{R}
-prestacks

X\longrightarrow Y

satisfying the following conditions:

(1)

Y
has deformation theory, which means that it preserves limits of Postnikov towers and pullbacks along nilpotent extensions of
{R}
-algebras, see
Definition

B.6
;

(2)

X\rightarrow Y
is locally almost of finite presentation (‘laft’), see
Definition

B.3
;

(3)

X\rightarrow Y
is a nil-isomorphism. Equivalently, it restricts to an equivalence on all reduced affine schemes, see Remark
B.13
.

Let
\mathrm{ModuliStk}_{X/{R}}\subset(\mathrm{PrStk}_{R})_{X/}
be the full subcategory spanned by all formal moduli
stacks under
X
.

Remark 5.2
.

Note that
Definition

5.1
also makes sense for
X
a general prestack.

In
Section

5.1
below, we will link formal moduli stacks
under
affine
schemes to formal moduli problems in the sense of Definition
4.11
, and hence to partition Lie algebroids. In
Section

5.2
, we will then prove our main integration equivalence in
Theorem

5.26
.

5.1.
Formal moduli problems and formal moduli stacks

Throughout this section, let
X=\Spec(B)
be a coherent affine derived scheme over
{R}
. We will show:

Theorem 5.3
.

Let
X=\Spec(B)
be a coherent affine derived
{R}
-scheme. Then there is an equivalence between the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{ModuliStk}_{X/{R}}
of formal moduli stacks under
X
and the
\infty
-category of formal moduli problems

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\colon\mathrm{Art}_{B/{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{S}}

in the sense of Definition
4.11
.
If
X\to Y
is a formal moduli stack, then the corresponding formal moduli problem
\mathrm{Art}_{B/{R}}\to\mathscr{S}
sends
A\to B
to the space of diagonal lifts

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spec(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spec({R}).}

The proof closely follows (a simple version of) the argument in
[
GR17a
]
and proceeds in two steps.

Notation 5.4
.

Throughout, we write

X_{\mathrm{prored}}=\Spec(B_{\mathrm{prored}}),\qquad\qquad X_{\inf}:=(X/{R})_{\inf}

for the pro-reduction (
Definition

B.8
) and infinitesimal stack of the affine derived scheme
X
. We will write
\sqcup
for the pushout of (pro-)affine derived schemes (i.e. the fibre product of (ind-)rings) and consider the following categories:

(1)

Write
\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{laft-nil},+}_{X/{R}}
for the
\infty
-category of affine
{R}
-schemes equipped with a nilpotent embedding
X\to S
that exhibits
X
as an almost finitely presented
S
-scheme, such that
\mathcal{O}(S)\to\mathcal{O}(X)=B
has eventually coconnective fibre. Equivalently, we simply have

\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{laft-nil},+}_{X/{R}}=\mathrm{Art}^{,\mathrm{op}}_{B/{R}}.

In particular, each
S\in\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{laft-nil},+}_{X/{R}}
is a coherent affine derived scheme as well (Corollary
4.5
).

(2)

Let
\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{nil},+}_{X/{R}}
be the
\infty
-category of affine
{R}
-schemes equipped with a nilpotent embedding
X\to S
such that
\mathcal{O}(S)\to\mathcal{O}(X)
has eventually coconnective fibre.

(3)

Let
\mathrm{Aff}_{/X_{\mathrm{inf}}}
be the
\infty
-category of affine schemes over
X_{\mathrm{inf}}
, i.e.
S\in\mathrm{Aff}_{{R}}
together with a map
S\hookleftarrow S_{\mathrm{prored}}\to X
.

Our goal will be to first identify formal moduli problems with certain functors on the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{nil},+}_{X/{R}}
. Next, we will relate functors on
\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{nil},+}_{X/{R}}
with prestacks
X\to Y\to X_{\inf}
in between
X
and its infinitesimal prestack. The
\infty
-category of formal moduli stacks can then be identified with a natural subcategory of this
\infty
-category.

Definition 5.5
.

We will say that a functor
F\colon(\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{nil},+}_{X/{R}}\big)^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathscr{S}

has deformation theory
if:

(1)

its value on the initial object is contractible,

(2)

it sends pushouts along nilpotent embeddings to pullbacks of spaces.

Let
u\colon\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{laft-nil},+}_{X/{R}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{nil},+}_{X/{R}}
be the evident fully faithful embedding and consider the induced adjoint pair between
\infty
-categories of presheaves:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{!}\colon\mathscr{P}\big(\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{laft-nil},+}_{X/{R}}\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{P}\big(\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{nil},+}_{X/{R}}\big)\colon u^{*}.}

The main technical step in the proof of
Theorem

5.3
is the following:

Proposition 5.6
.

Let
F\colon\big(\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{laft-nil},+}_{X/{R}}\big)^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathscr{S}
be a formal moduli problem. Then the left Kan extension
u_{!}F\colon\big(\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{nil},+}_{X/{R}}\big)^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathscr{S}
has deformation theory.

The proof will require some preliminaries:

Lemma 5.7
.

Let
F\colon\big(\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{laft-nil},+}_{X/{R}}\big)^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathscr{S}
be a formal moduli problem and consider a pushout diagram in
\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{nil},+}_{X/{R}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{0}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

such that
S_{0}
and
S^{\prime}_{0}
are contained in
\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{laft-nil},+}_{X/{R}}
and
S_{0}\to S
is a square zero extension. Then the induced map

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{!}F(S^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{!}F(S)\times_{u_{!}F(S_{0})}u_{!}F(S_{0}^{\prime})}

is an equivalence.

Proof.

We will compare the fibres over a fixed
x\colon S^{\prime}_{0}\to u_{!}F
. Suppose
S_{0}\to S
is a square zero extension by an (necessarily eventually coconnective) ideal
I
and let
S_{0,I[1]}
be the trivial square zero extension of
S_{0}
by its suspension. Since
S_{0}\in\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{laft-nil},+}_{X/{R}}
is a coherent affine derived scheme (Corollary
4.5
), we can choose a filtered system
I_{*}\colon\mathscr{K}\to\mathrm{Coh}(S_{0})_{\geq 0}
with
\colim_{\alpha}I_{\alpha}=I
.

Having fixed this, let
\mathscr{C}^{\prime}
be the
\infty
-category of factorizations

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{\prime}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{\prime}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{!}F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}

with
T^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{laft-nil},+}_{X/{R}}
. A map is a natural transformation that restricts to the identity on the solid part of the diagram. The geometric realization of this
\infty
-category computes the fibre
u_{!}F(S^{\prime})_{x}
. Likewise, let
\mathscr{C}
be the
\infty
-category of factorizations

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{!}F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x|S_{0}}

which computes the fibre
u_{!}F(S)_{x}
. The restriction
u_{!}F(S^{\prime})_{x}\to u_{!}F(S)_{x}
then arises as the realization of the functor
\pi\colon\mathscr{C}^{\prime}\to\mathscr{C}
that restricts a factorization along
S\to S^{\prime}
.

We therefore have to prove that
\pi
is a weak equivalence. To do this, we will replace
\mathscr{C}^{\prime}
and
\mathscr{C}
be weakly equivalent categories
\mathscr{D}^{\prime}
and
\mathscr{D}
. First, let us consider the functor
\mathscr{K}\to\mathrm{Cat}
sending each
\alpha\in\mathscr{K}
to the
\infty
-category
\mathscr{D}(\alpha)
of diagrams of the form

(18)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{S_{0,I[1]}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{S_{0,I_{\alpha}[1]}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{!}F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x|S_{0}}

where
T\in\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{laft-nil},+}_{X/{R}}
and a morphism is a natural transformation which is the identity on the solid part of the diagram. This depends functorially on
\alpha
by restriction along the map of trivial square zero extensions
S_{0,I[1]}\to S_{0,I_{\beta}[1]}\to S_{0,I_{\alpha}[1]}
for each map of ideals
I_{\alpha}\to I_{\beta}
. We let
\mathscr{D}=\int_{\alpha\in\mathscr{K}}\mathscr{D}(\alpha)
be the (cocartesian) unstraightening.

There is a natural functor
q\colon\mathscr{D}\to\mathscr{C}
restricting to the right face of the cube in (
18
). This map is a cocartesian fibration (by postcomposition with
T\to T^{\prime}
) whose fibre over a factorization
S\to T\to u_{!}F
is given as follows. Note that the map
S\to T
exhibits
S
as a square zero extension of
S_{0}
by
I

relative to
T
, i.e. it is classified by a map
L_{S_{0}/T}\to I[1]
. One can then identify

q^{-1}(T)=\int_{\alpha\in\mathscr{K}}\Map_{\mathrm{QC}(S_{0})/I[1]}\big(L_{S_{0}/T},I_{\alpha}[1]\big)

with the unstraightening of the functor sending each
\alpha
to the space of lifts of the map
L_{S_{0}/T}\to I[1]
to
I_{\alpha}[1]
. Since
S_{0}
and
T
are in
\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{laft-nil},+}_{X/{R}}
, the map
S_{0}\to T
is almost finitely presented (Corollary
4.6
). Using that
L_{S_{0}/T}
is an almost perfect
S_{0}
-module, we find that

|q^{-1}(T)|\simeq\colim_{\alpha\in K}\Map_{\mathrm{QC}(S_{0})/I[1]}\big(L_{S_{0}/T},I_{\alpha}[1]\big)\simeq\Map_{\mathrm{QC}(S_{0})/I[1]}\big(L_{S_{0}/T},\colim_{\alpha}I_{\alpha}[1]\big)=\ast.

We conclude that
q\colon\mathscr{D}\to\mathscr{C}
is a weak equivalence.

Next, we define
\mathscr{D}^{\prime}
to the the
\infty
-category of diagrams of the form

(19)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{S_{0,I[1]}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{\prime}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{S_{0,I_{\alpha}[1]}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{\prime}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{!}F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}

where again,
T^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{laft-nil},+}_{X/{R}}
and a morphism is a natural transformation which is the identity on the solid part of the diagram. Restriction to the right face determines a cocartesian fibration
q^{\prime}\colon\mathscr{D}^{\prime}\to\mathscr{C}^{\prime}
whose fibre over
T^{\prime}
satisfies

|q^{\prime-1}(T^{\prime})|\simeq\colim_{\alpha\in K}\Map_{\mathrm{QC}(S_{0})/I[1]}\big(L_{S_{0}/T^{\prime}},I_{\alpha}[1]\big)\simeq\Map_{\mathrm{QC}(S_{0})/I[1]}\big(L_{S_{0}/T^{\prime}},\colim_{\alpha}I_{\alpha}[1]\big)=\ast.

We thus obtain a commuting square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{D}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{C}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{C}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}

where the horizontal functors are weak equivalences and
\pi^{\prime}
forgets the copies of
S_{0}^{\prime}
and
S^{\prime}
in Diagram (
19
). It therefore remains to verify that
\pi^{\prime}
is a weak equivalence.

Now let
\mathscr{E}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{D}
and
\mathscr{E}^{\prime}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{D^{\prime}}
be the full subcategories of diagrams of the form (
18
), resp. (
19
), whose front face is a pushout in
\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{laft-nil},+}_{X/{R}}
. These inclusions admit left adjoints
L,L^{\prime}
that replace the object
T
by the pushout of the front face; in particular,
\mathscr{E}
and
\mathscr{E}^{\prime}
are weakly equivalent to
\mathscr{D}
and
\mathscr{D}^{\prime}
. Furthermore, there is a natural functor
\phi\colon\mathscr{E}\to\mathscr{E}^{\prime}
sending each
T
as in (
18
) to
T^{\prime}=S^{\prime}_{0}\sqcup_{S_{0}}T
, which fits naturally into a diagram of the form (
19
).

Now note that for any
T\in\mathscr{E}\subseteq\mathscr{D}
, there is a natural transformation
T\to\pi^{\prime}\circ\phi(T)=S^{\prime}_{0}\sqcup_{S_{0}}T
. In particular, the map
\pi^{\prime}\colon|\mathscr{D}^{\prime}|\to|\mathscr{D}|
admits a right inverse (presented by
\phi
). Conversely, for every object
T^{\prime}\in\mathscr{D}^{\prime}
as in (
19
), there is a natural map

\phi\circ L\circ\pi^{\prime}(T)=S^{\prime}_{0}\sqcup_{S_{0,I_{\alpha}[1]}}S_{0}\to T

which shows that
\pi^{\prime}\colon|\mathscr{D}^{\prime}|\to|\mathscr{D}|
admits a left inverse (presented by
\phi\circ L
). We conclude that
\pi^{\prime}
, and therefore the map
u_{!}F(S^{\prime})_{x}\to u_{!}F(S)_{x|S_{0}}
is an equivalence.
∎

Lemma 5.8
.

Let
F\colon\big(\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{laft-nil},+}_{X/{R}}\big)^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathscr{S}
be a formal moduli problem and consider a pushout square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{0}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

in
\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{nil},+}_{X/{R}}
such that
S^{\prime}_{0}\in\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{laft-nil},+}_{X/{R}}
and
S_{0}\to S
is a square zero extension. Then the induced map

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{!}F(S^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{!}F(S)\times_{u_{!}F(S_{0})}u_{!}F(S_{0}^{\prime})}

is an equivalence.

Proof.

We fix
x\colon S\to u_{!}F
with
x_{0}=x\big|S_{0}
. Consider the
\infty
-category
\mathscr{D}
of diagrams of the form

(20)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{\prime}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{!}F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x^{\prime}}

where the square is cocartesian and
S_{0}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{laft-nil},+}_{X/{R}}
. A map is a natural transformation that is constant on the solid part of the diagram. Likewise, let
\mathscr{C}_{0}
be the
\infty
-category of factorizations

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{0}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{!}F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x^{\prime}_{0}}

where
S_{0}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{laft-nil},+}_{X/{R}}
. There is an evident right fibration
\pi\colon\mathscr{D}\to\mathscr{C}_{0}
sending
x^{\prime}\mapsto x^{\prime}\big|S^{\prime}_{0}
. The lemma asserts that the fibres of
\pi
are contractible.

By
Lemma

5.7
, the functor
\pi
is also a left fibration. Furthermore, the
\infty
-category
\mathscr{C}_{0}
is contractible by definition of
u_{!}F
as a left Kan extension. Consequently, it will suffice to prove that the
\infty
-category
\mathscr{D}
itself is contractible.

To see this, let
\mathscr{D}^{\prime}
be the
\infty
-category of diagrams of the form (
20
) where the square is not necessarily cocartesian, but where
S^{\prime}
is contained in
\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{laft-nil},+}_{X/{R}}
. There is an evident functor
\pi\colon\mathscr{D}^{\prime}\to\mathscr{D}
sending a factorization
x^{\prime}\colon S^{\prime}\to u_{!}F
with
S^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{laft-nil},+}_{X/{R}}
to
S\sqcup_{S_{0}}S^{\prime}_{0}\to S^{\prime}\to u_{!}F
. To prove the lemma it will thus suffice to show that:

(a)

\mathscr{D}^{\prime}
is weakly contractible.

(b)

\pi\colon\mathscr{D}^{\prime}\to\mathscr{D}
is a weak equivalence.

Let us start with (a): notice that restriction of the diagram (
20
) to the bottom row defines a functor
\phi\colon\mathscr{D}^{\prime}\to\mathscr{C}^{\prime}
to the
\infty
-category of factorizations

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{!}F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x^{\prime}}

with
S^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{laft-nil},+}_{X/{R}}
. Note that
\mathscr{C}^{\prime}
is contractible by definition of
u_{!}F
as a left Kan extension and that
\phi
admits a right adjoint sending each factorization
S\to S^{\prime}\to u_{!}F
to

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{!}F.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x^{\prime}}

This implies that
\mathscr{D}^{\prime}
is weakly contractible.

For (b), pick a factorization
x^{\prime}\colon S^{\prime}=S\sqcup_{S_{0}}S^{\prime}_{0}\to u_{!}F
in
\mathscr{D}
and consider the comma
\infty
-category
x^{\prime}/\mathscr{D}^{\prime}
. Unraveling the definitions, one sees that
x^{\prime}/\mathscr{D}^{\prime}
is just the
\infty
-category
\mathscr{D}^{\prime}
considered in our proof of (a), but with
S_{0}\to S
replaced
S^{\prime}_{0}\to S^{\prime}
. The proof of (a) then shows that
x^{\prime}/\mathscr{D}^{\prime}
is contractible, so that
\pi
is a weak equivalence.
∎

Corollary 5.9
.

Let
F\colon\big(\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{laft-nil},+}_{X/{R}}\big)^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathscr{S}
be a formal moduli problem and consider a pushout square in
\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{nil},+}_{X/{R}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{0}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

such that
S_{0}\to S
is a square zero extension. Then the induced map

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{!}F(S^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{!}F(S)\times_{u_{!}F(S_{0})}u_{!}F(S_{0}^{\prime})}

is an equivalence.

Proof.

Let us fix a map
x\colon S^{\prime}_{0}\to u_{!}F
. It suffices to show that the induced map on fibres
u_{!}F(S^{\prime})_{x}\to u_{!}F(S)_{x|S_{0}}
is an equivalence. Now
x
factors as
S^{\prime}_{0}\to T_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle t_{0}}}{{\to}}u_{!}F
with
T_{0}\in\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{laft-nil},+}_{X/{R}}
. Letting
T=T_{0}\sqcup_{S_{0}}S
, we then have maps

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{!}F(T)_{t_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{!}F(S^{\prime})_{t_{0}|S^{\prime}_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{!}F(S)_{t_{0}|S_{0}}.}

The left map and the composite are equivalences by
Lemma

5.8
, so that the right map is an equivalence as well.
∎

Proof of
Proposition

5.6
.

Every nilpotent embedding of eventually coconnective affine derived schemes can be decomposed into a finite sequence of square zero extensions, so this follows from Corollary
5.9
.
∎

Definition 5.10
.

Let
f\colon G\to G^{\prime}
be a natural transformation between functors
\big(\mathrm{Aff}_{X/{R}}^{\mathrm{nil},+}\big)^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathscr{S}
. We will say that
f
is
locally almost finitely presented
if for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
and any cofiltered diagram
S_{*}\colon\mathscr{K}\to\mathrm{Aff}_{X/{R}}^{\mathrm{nil},+}
with limit
S
such that all
\mathcal{O}(S_{\alpha})\to\mathcal{O}(X)=B
have
(n-1)
-coconnective fibre, the map

\colim_{\alpha\in\mathscr{K}^{\mathrm{op}}}G(S_{\alpha})\to G(S)\times_{G^{\prime}(S)}\big(\colim_{\alpha\in\mathscr{K}^{\mathrm{op}}}G^{\prime}(S_{\alpha})\big)

is an equivalence.

Remark 5.11
.

Suppose that
S_{*}\colon\mathscr{K}\to\mathrm{Aff}_{X/{R}}^{\mathrm{nil},+}
is a cofiltered diagram of affine schemes under
X
such that all
\mathcal{O}(S_{\alpha})\to\mathcal{O}(X)
have
(n-1)
-coconnective fibre for some fixed
n
. Then

S_{\alpha}\simeq S_{\alpha}^{\leq n}\sqcup_{X^{\leq n}}X

where we write
X^{\leq n}=\Spec(\tau_{\leq n}B)
for the corresponding truncated affine derived scheme.

Corollary 5.12
.

The adjoint pair
(u_{!},u^{*})
restricts to an equivalence between the full subcategories of:

(1)

formal moduli problems
F\colon\big(\mathrm{Aff}_{X/{R}}^{\mathrm{laft-nil},+}\big)^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathscr{S}
.

(2)

functors
G\colon\big(\mathrm{Aff}_{X/{R}}^{\mathrm{nil},+}\big)^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathscr{S}
with deformation theory such that the canonical map
X\to G
is locally almost finitely presented.

Proof.

It will suffice to show that
X\to u_{!}(F)
is indeed locally almost finitely presented. Assuming this, one has that
u_{!}
is fully faithful because it takes the left Kan extension along the fully faithful functor
u
. A map
G\to G^{\prime}
between functors with deformation theory is an equivalence if and only if it for every square zero extension
X\to X_{I}
with
I\in\mathrm{QC}(X)^{+}_{\geq 0}
, the induced map

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G(X_{I})\simeq G(X)\times_{G(X_{I[1]})}G(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G^{\prime}(X)\times_{G^{\prime}(X_{I[1]})}G^{\prime}(X)=G^{\prime}(X_{I})}

is an equivalence. If
X\to G
and
X\to G^{\prime}
are both locally almost finitely presented, it suffices to verify this when
I\in\mathrm{Coh}(X)
, in which case
X_{I}\in\mathrm{Aff}_{X/}^{\mathrm{laft-nil},+}
. It follows that
u^{*}
detects equivalences such functors, so that
(u_{!},u^{*})
restricts to the desired equivalence.

To see that
X\to u_{!}(F)
is indeed locally almost finitely presented, let
S_{*}\colon\mathscr{K}\to\mathrm{Aff}_{X/{R}}^{\mathrm{nil},+}
be a cofiltered diagram as in Definition
5.10
, with limit
S
. By Remark
5.11
, we can assume that
S_{\ast}=S^{\leq n}_{\ast}\sqcup_{X^{\leq n}}X
arises as the pushout of the cofiltered diagram of
n
-coconnective schemes
S_{\alpha}^{\leq n}
under
X^{\leq n}
. Consequently, we have natural equivalences
S_{\alpha}\simeq S_{\alpha}^{\leq n}\sqcup_{S^{\leq n}}S
. We will need to prove that the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\colim_{\alpha}X(S_{\alpha})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\colim_{\alpha}u_{!}F(S_{\alpha})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{!}F(S)}

is cartesian. To this end, note that
u_{!}F(S)
arises as the classifying space of the
\infty
-category
\mathscr{C}
of factorizations
S\to T\to j_{!}F
with
T\in\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{laft-nil},+}_{X/{R}}
. Likewise, for each
\alpha
let
\mathscr{C}(\alpha)
be the
\infty
-category of factorizations
S_{\alpha}\to T\to u_{!}F
. This determines a diagram of categories
\mathscr{K}\to\mathrm{Cat}
whose unstraightening

\mathscr{C}^{\prime}=\int_{\alpha\in\mathscr{K}}\mathscr{C}(\alpha)

has the property that
|\mathscr{C}^{\prime}|=\colim_{\alpha}u_{!}F(S_{\alpha})
.

We now consider the functor
\pi\colon\mathscr{C}^{\prime}\to\mathscr{C}
sending each
S_{\alpha}\to T\to u_{!}F
to the composite
S\to S_{\alpha}\to T\to u_{!}F
. This is a cocartesian fibration (by postcomposition with
T\to T^{\prime}
) whose fibre over
x=\big(S\to T\to u_{!}F\big)\in\mathscr{C}
is given by the
\infty
-category

\pi^{-1}(x)=\int_{\alpha\in\mathscr{K}}\Map_{S/}\big(S_{\alpha},T\big)\simeq\int_{\alpha\in\mathscr{K}}\Map_{S^{\leq n}/}\big(S^{\leq n}_{\alpha},T\big).

The classifying space of this
\infty
-category can be identified with
|\pi^{-1}(x)|=\colim_{\alpha}\Map_{S^{\leq n}/}(S^{\leq n}_{\alpha},T)
. For every map in
\mathscr{C}
of the form

S\to T\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\to}}T^{\prime}\to u_{!}F

the induced change-of-fibres map
f_{*}\colon\pi^{-1}(x)\to\pi^{-1}(x^{\prime})
is then given at the level of classifying spaces by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\colim_{\alpha}\Map_{S^{\leq n}/}(S^{\leq n}_{\alpha},T)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\colim_{\alpha}\Map_{S^{\leq n}/}(S^{\leq n}_{\alpha},T^{\prime}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f\circ-}

This map is a weak equivalence since every
T\to T^{\prime}
in
\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{laft-nil},+}_{X/{R}}
is locally almost finitely presented (Corollary
4.6
). Consequently, given a point
x\in|\mathscr{C}|=u_{!}F(S)
, corresponding to a factorization
x\colon S\to T\to u_{!}F
, there is an equivalence

\big(\colim_{\alpha}u_{!}F(S_{\alpha})\big)\times_{u_{!}F(S)}\{x\}\simeq|\pi^{-1}(x)|.

Applying this to each point
x\colon S\to X\to u_{!}F
, one sees that

\big(\colim_{\alpha}u_{!}F(S_{\alpha})\big)\times_{u_{!}F(S)}\{x\}\simeq\colim_{\alpha}\Map_{S/}(S_{\alpha},X)\simeq\big(\colim_{\alpha}X(S_{\alpha})\big)\times_{X(S)}\{x\}

which yields the result.
∎

Next, let
\mathrm{PrStk}_{X_{\inf}}^{\mathrm{conv}}
be the
\infty
-category of convergent prestacks over
X_{\inf}
. Every
S\in\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{nil},+}_{X/{R}}
in gives rise to a convergent prestack
S\to X_{\inf}
over
X_{\inf}
, where the map is adjoint to the canonical map
S_{\mathrm{prored}}=X_{\mathrm{prored}}\to X
. Let us now consider the adjoint pairs

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v_{!}\colon\colon\mathscr{P}\big(\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{nil},+}_{X/{R}}\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(\mathrm{PrStk}^{\mathrm{conv}}_{X_{\inf}}\big)\colon v^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{v}_{!}\colon\mathscr{P}\big(\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{nil},+}_{X/{R}}\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(\mathrm{PrStk}^{\mathrm{conv}}_{X_{\inf}}\big)_{X/}\colon\tilde{v}^{*}}

where
v_{!}
and
\tilde{v}_{!}
are the unique colimit-preserving functors sending each nilpotent embedding
S\in\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{nil},+}_{X/{R}}
to the diagrams of convergent prestacks
S\to X_{\inf}
and
X\to S\to X_{\inf}
, respectively. At the level of the right adjoints, given a diagram of convergent
{R}
-prestacks
X\to Y\to X_{\inf}
, the presheaf
\tilde{v}^{*}Y
sends each nilpotent embedding
X\hookrightarrow S
in
\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{nil},+}_{X/{R}}
to the space of dotted lifts

(21)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{\inf}}

in
\mathrm{PrStk}^{\mathrm{conv}}_{{R}}
. Let us point out that, since
X\hookrightarrow S
is a nilpotent embedding, this space is equivalent to the space of dotted lifts where we forget compatibility with the projection to
X_{\inf}
.

Remark 5.13
.

The functor
v_{!}
can be computed explicitly as follows: if
x\colon S\to X_{\inf}
is a map from an
eventually coconnective
affine derived scheme, then

\Map_{/X_{\inf}}(S,v_{!}F)\simeq F(X\sqcup_{S_{\mathrm{prored}}}S)=\colim_{\alpha}F\big(S\amalg_{S_{\alpha}}X\big)

with the colimit taken over the filtered
\infty
-category of nilpotent ideals in
\pi_{0}(\mathcal{O}(S))
. Indeed, both of these functors preserve colimits, as colimits of convergent prestacks are computed pointwise on eventually coconnective affine derived schemes. Furthermore, they are naturally equivalent for functors
F
represented by
T\in\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{nil},+}_{X/{R}}
, because
\Map_{/X_{\inf}}(S,v_{!}T)
is naturally equivalent to the diagram of dotted extensions in
\mathrm{Aff}_{{R}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathrm{prored}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}

Lemma 5.14
.

The adjoint pair
(\tilde{v}_{!},\tilde{v}^{*})
restricts to an equivalence between the full subcategories of:

(1)

functors
F\colon\big(\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{nil},+}_{X/{R}}\big)^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathscr{S}
with deformation theory.

(2)

diagrams of nil-isomorphic convergent prestacks
X\to Y\to X_{\inf}
where
Y
has deformation theory.

Proof.

Let us first prove that
\tilde{v}_{!}
and
\tilde{v}^{*}
preserve these two subcategories. First, given
X\to Y\to X_{\inf}
where
Y
has deformation theory, the diagram (
21
) directly shows that
\tilde{v}^{*}(Y)
sends pushouts in
\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{nil},+}_{X/{R}}
along nilpotent embeddings to pullbacks of spaces and sends
X
to a contractible space.

Next, we consider the functor

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{P}\big(\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{nil},+}_{X/{R}}\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(\mathrm{PrStk}^{\mathrm{conv}}_{X_{\inf}}\big)_{X/}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{PrStk}^{\mathrm{conv}}_{X_{\inf}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{v}_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{forget}}

and the unique colimit-preserving functor

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v_{!}\colon\mathscr{P}\big(\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{nil},+}_{X/{R}}\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{PrStk}^{\mathrm{conv}}_{X_{\inf}}}

that sends
X\hookrightarrow S
to the map
S\to X_{\inf}
adjoint to
S_{\mathrm{prored}}=X_{\mathrm{prored}}\to X
. Both functors preserve colimits indexed by contractible diagrams and are naturally equivalent on representables. Consequently, they are naturally equivalent on all presheaves arising as colimits of contractible diagrams of representables. Since every functor
F\colon\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{nil},+}_{X/{R}}\to\mathscr{S}
with deformation theory has
F(X)\simeq\ast
, it is such a colimit of a contractible diagram, i.e. we can identify
\tilde{v}_{!}(F)\to X_{\inf}
simply with
v_{!}(F)\to X_{\inf}
. Using the formula from Remark
5.13
and Lemma
B.7
, one now readily verifies that if
F
has deformation theory, then the convergent prestack
\tilde{v}_{!}(F)\simeq v_{!}(F)
has deformation theory and is nil-isomorphic to
X_{\inf}
(or equivalently, to
X
).

The adjoint pair
(\tilde{v}_{!},\tilde{v}^{*})
therefore restricts to an adjoint pair between functors
F
with deformation theory and nil-isomorphisms
X\to Y\to X_{\inf}
where
Y
has deformation theory. When
F
has deformation theory, the unit
F\to\tilde{v}^{*}\tilde{v}_{!}F
is given at each nilpotent embedding
X\hookrightarrow S
as follows. Write
x_{0}\colon X\to F
for the canonical map and suppose that
\mathcal{O}(S)\to\mathcal{O}(X)
has
(n-1)
-coconnective fibre for some
n
, so that
S=S^{\leq n}\sqcup_{X^{\leq n}}X
is the pushout of the corresponding
n
-coconnective affine derived schemes. Then the unit map can be identified with

(22)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(S)\simeq F(S^{\leq n}\sqcup_{X^{\leq n}}X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(S^{\leq n}\amalg_{S_{\mathrm{prored}}}X)\times_{F(X^{\leq n}\amalg_{X_{\mathrm{prored}}}X)}\{\nabla^{*}x_{0}\}}

where
f\colon X^{\leq n}\sqcup_{X_{\mathrm{prored}}}X\to X
arises from the codiagonal map. Diagrammatically, this sends the dotted map
x\colon S\to F
in the following diagram to its restrictions:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\leq n}\amalg_{X_{\mathrm{prored}}}X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{\leq n}\amalg_{S_{\mathrm{prored}}}X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nabla}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}

where
X^{\leq n}\amalg_{X_{\mathrm{prored}}}X
is a pro-object in
\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{nil},+}_{X/{R}}
using the inclusion of
X
as the right component. Using that
X_{\mathrm{prored}}\to S_{\mathrm{prored}}
is an equivalence and that
S\simeq S^{\leq n}\sqcup_{X^{\leq n}}X
, one sees that the above square is a pushout square of pro-objects in
\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{nil},+}_{X/{R}}
. Consequently, the unit map (
22
) is an equivalence, since
F
had deformation theory.

On the other hand, for
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle y_{0}}}{{\to}}Y\to X_{\inf}
, the counit
\tilde{v}_{!}\tilde{v}^{*}Y\to Y
is given at each
x\colon S\to X_{\inf}
, where
S
is an eventually coconnective affine derived scheme, by the map

(23)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map_{/X_{\inf}}\big(S\amalg_{S_{\mathrm{prored}}}X,Y\big)\times_{\Map_{/X_{\inf}}(X,Y)}\{y_{0}\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map_{/X_{\inf}}(S,Y).}

In terms of diagrams, the domain can be identified with the space of dotted extensions in the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathrm{prored}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S\sqcup_{S_{\mathrm{prored}}}X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{\inf}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y}

The counit sends such
y
to its restriction to
S
. Notice that the pushout
S\sqcup_{S_{\mathrm{prored}}}X
arises as a pro-system of pushout squares
S\sqcup_{S_{\alpha}}X
, where each
S_{\alpha}\to S
is a nilpotent embedding. Since
Y
has deformation theory, it follows that the map

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y(X\sqcup_{S_{\mathrm{prored}}}S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y(X)\times_{Y(S_{\mathrm{prored}})}Y(S)}

is an equivalence. Consequently, the counit map (
23
) can be identified with the map

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y(S)\times_{(Y(S_{\mathrm{prored}})\times_{X(S_{\mathrm{prored}})}\{x\})}\{y_{0}\big|_{S_{\mathrm{prored}}}\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y(S)}

projecting onto the first factor. The fact that
Y\to X_{\inf}
is a nil-isomorphism is equivalent to the fact that
Y(S_{\mathrm{prored}})\simeq X(S_{\mathrm{prored}})
, so that
Y(S_{\mathrm{prored}})\times_{X(S_{\mathrm{prored}})}\{x\}
is contractible and the above map is indeed an equivalence as desired.
∎

Finally, we note:

Lemma 5.15
.

The functor
\mathrm{oblv}\colon\big(\mathrm{PrStk}_{/X_{\inf}}\big)_{X/}\to\mathrm{PrStk}_{X/{R}}
sending each
X\to Y\to X_{\inf}
to
X\to Y
restricts to an equivalence between the full subcategories of:

(1)

Diagrams
X\to Y\to X_{\inf}
where
Y
has deformation theory and
X\to Y
is a laft nil-isomorphism.

(2)

Formal moduli stacks
X\to Y
in the sense of Definition
5.1
.

Proof.

Note that if
f\colon X\to Y
is locally almost finitely presented, then
f
is a nil-isomorphism if and only if it restricts to an equivalence on reduced schemes, by Remark
B.13
. The forgetful functor then restricts to an equivalence because for any nil-isomorphism
X\to Y
, space
\Map_{X/}(Y,X_{\inf})
is contractible, as all three functors are naturally equivalent on pro-reduced affine schemes
S_{\mathrm{prored}}
.
∎

Proof of
Theorem

5.3
.

Proposition

5.6
and
Lemma

5.14
imply that the composite adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w_{!}\colon\mathscr{P}\big(\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{laft-nil},+}_{X/{R}}\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{P}\big(\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{nil},+}_{X/{R}}\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(\mathrm{PrStk}^{\mathrm{conv}}_{X_{\inf}}\big)_{X/}\colon w^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{v}_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{v}^{*}}

restricts to an adjunction
(w_{!}^{\mathrm{def}},w^{*}_{\mathrm{def}})
between formal moduli problems on
\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{laft-nil},+}_{X/{R}}\simeq\mathrm{Art}^{,\mathrm{op}}_{B/{R}}
and nil-isomorphisms
X\to Y\to X_{\inf}
where
Y
has deformation theory.

The resulting adjoint pair
(w_{!}^{\mathrm{def}},w^{*}_{\mathrm{def}})
has a fully faithful left adjoint:
u_{!}
is fully faithful by Corollary
5.12
and
\tilde{v}_{!}
was fully faithful on functors with deformation theory by
Lemma

5.14
. It therefore remains to show that the essential image of
w_{!}^{\mathrm{def}}
consists precisely of nil-isomorphisms
X\to Y\to X_{\inf}
which exhibit
X
as a laft
Y
-prestack. Lemma
5.15
then implies that
w^{*}_{\mathrm{def}}
induces an equivalence between formal moduli stacks
X\to Y
and formal moduli problems. Unravelling the definitions, one sees that
w^{*}_{\mathrm{def}}
is precisely the functor from
Theorem

5.3
.

Let us first verify that
X\to w_{!}^{\mathrm{def}}(F)
is indeed laft, i.e. for each map
T\to w_{!}^{\mathrm{def}}(F)
from an eventually coconnective affine derived
{R}
-scheme
T
, the pullback
X\times_{w_{!}^{\mathrm{def}}(F)}T
is a laft
T
-prestack. Note that
w_{!}^{\mathrm{def}}(F)
is the colimit of a contractible diagram of nilpotent extensions
S\in\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{laft-nil},+}_{X/{R}}
, and that such colimits in
\mathrm{PrStk}^{\mathrm{conv}}_{{R}}
are computed pointwise on eventually coconnective affine derived schemes. Hence it suffices to treat the case where
T\to w_{!}^{\mathrm{def}}(F)
fits into a diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w_{!}^{\mathrm{def}}(F)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w_{!}(x)}

where
X\to S
is contained in
\mathrm{Aff}^{\mathrm{laft-nil},+}_{X/{R}}
and
x\in F(S)
. It therefore suffices to treat the case where
T=S
. In that case,

X\times_{w_{!}^{\mathrm{def}}(F)}S=w_{!}^{\mathrm{def}}\big(X\times_{F}S\big)

arises as the image of a formal moduli problem over the base
S
, i.e. a formal moduli problem
\big(\mathrm{Aff}_{X/S}^{\mathrm{laft-nil},+}\big)^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathscr{S}
. This uses that
u_{!}
preserves pullbacks of formal moduli problems (by Corollary
5.12
) and that
v_{!}
preserves pullbacks by the formula from Remark
5.13
.

Since
X\to S
is a nilpotent embedding which is almost finitely presented, each
S^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Aff}_{X/S}^{\mathrm{laft-nil},+}
is almost finitely presented over
S
(Corollary
4.6
). Consequently, the prestack
X\times_{w_{!}^{\mathrm{def}}(F)}T
is a colimit of almost finitely presented affine derived
T
-schemes; this implies that it is a laft
T
-prestack, as required.

To conclude, it now suffices to verify that
w^{*}_{\mathrm{def}}
detects equivalences between laft nil-isomorphisms
X\to Y
where
Y
has deformation theory. This follows from the fact that
\tilde{v}^{*}
detects weak equivalences by
Lemma

5.14
and that
u^{*}
detects weak equivalences by Corollary
5.12
.
∎

5.2.
Formal integration of partition Lie algebroids

Let us again fix a coherent animated base ring
{R}
and a locally coherent qcqs derived
{R}
-scheme
X
.
To prove our main theorem
Theorem

5.26
, we will proceed in two steps: first, we will show that the
shifted tangent complex functor

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{ModuliStk}_{X/{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X})_{/T_{X/{R}}[1]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X\rightarrow Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(T_{X/Y}[1]\rightarrow T_{X/{R}}[1])}

is monadic; next, we will
identify the resulting monad with the partition Lie algebroid monad constructed in
Theorem

3.1
.
In our proof, we will need to relate formal moduli stacks on different schemes.

Construction 5.16

(Functorial moduli stacks and their tangent fibres)
.

Let
X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
be a laft map of prestacks with deformation theory. Given a formal moduli stack
X\rightarrow Y
, consider the composite map

X^{\prime}\rightarrow X\rightarrow Y

and let
Y^{\wedge}_{X^{\prime}}
be the formal completion of
Y
along
X^{\prime}\rightarrow Y
as defined in Construction
B.9
. Via the natural map
X^{\prime}\rightarrow Y^{\wedge}_{X^{\prime}}
, the prestack
Y^{\wedge}_{X^{\prime}}
becomes a formal moduli stack under
X^{\prime}
by
Lemma

B.14
. The construction
(X\rightarrow Y)\longmapsto(X^{\prime}\rightarrow Y^{\wedge}_{X^{\prime}})
defines a functor

(-)^{\wedge}_{X^{\prime}}\colon\mathrm{ModuliStk}_{X/{R}}\rightarrow\mathrm{ModuliStk}_{X^{\prime}/{R}}.

More formally, let us write

\mathrm{PrStk}_{{R}}^{\mathrm{def},\mathrm{laft-map}}
for the
\infty
-category of
{R}
-prestacks with deformation theory and laft maps between them.
Consider the full subcategory

\mathrm{ModuliStk}_{{R}}\subset\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathrm{PrStk}_{{R}}^{\mathrm{def},\mathrm{laft-map}})

whose objects are maps of
{R}
-prestacks
X\rightarrow Y
with
Y
a formal moduli stack under
X
(Definition
5.1
) and whose morphisms are squares

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}}

for which the vertical maps are laft and have deformation theory.
Evaluation at
0
defines a cartesian fibration

\mathrm{ModuliStk}_{R}\to\mathrm{PrStk}_{{R}}^{\mathrm{def},\mathrm{laft-map}},

which we straighten to obtain a functor
(\mathrm{PrStk}_{{R}}^{\mathrm{def},\mathrm{laft-map}})^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
. It sends an object
X
to
\mathrm{ModuliStk}_{X/{R}}
and a morphism
X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
to the induced

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\wedge}_{X^{\prime}}\colon\mathrm{ModuliStk}_{X/{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{ModuliStk}_{X^{\prime}/}\ ;\quad(X\rightarrow Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X^{\prime}\rightarrow Y^{\wedge}_{X^{\prime}}).}

Construction
B.23
and Proposition
B.32
then define (after suspending) the functorial shifted relative tangent complex

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T[1]\colon\mathrm{ModuliStk}_{{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{/T_{-/{R}}[1]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hskip 20.0pt(X\rightarrow Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(T_{X/Y}[1]\rightarrow T_{X/{R}}[1])}

This defines a map of cartesian fibrations by
Proposition

B.32
and
Proposition

B.28
(4). In other words, we obtain functors
T_{X/-}[1]\colon\mathrm{ModuliStk}_{X/{R}}\to(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X})_{/T_{X/{R}}[1]}
that are natural in
X
with respect to laft maps, i.e., each laft map

f\colon X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
gives rise to a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{ModuliStk}_{X/{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X})_{/T_{X/{R}}[1]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{ModuliStk}_{X^{\prime}/{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X^{\prime}})_{/T_{X^{\prime}/{R}}[1]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{X/-}[1]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\wedge}_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{X^{\prime}/-}[1]}

where
f^{\sharp}(\mathcal{F}\rightarrow T_{X/{R}}[1])\simeq f^{\ast}(\mathcal{F})\times_{(f^{*}T^{\mathrm{pre}}_{X/{R}})^{\mathrm{aft}}[1]}T_{X^{\prime}/{R}}[1]
. If
f
is formally étale, then this simplifies to
f^{\sharp}(\mathcal{F}\rightarrow T_{X/{R}}[1])\simeq f^{*}\mathcal{F}
by
Proposition

B.28
(3).

Proposition 5.17

(Conservativity)
.

Let
X
be a prestack with deformation theory and
Y_{1}\rightarrow Y_{2}
a map of formal moduli stacks under
X
. If

T_{X/Y_{1}}\rightarrow T_{X/Y_{2}}
is an equivalence, then so is

Y_{1}\rightarrow Y_{2}
.

Proof.

Since
Y_{1}
and
Y_{2}
are both convergent, it suffices to verify that
Y_{1}(A)\to Y_{2}(A)
is an equivalence for every eventually coconnective animated ring
A
. To see this, consider the ind-ring

A_{\mathrm{prored}}=``\colim_{I}"\pi_{0}(A)/I

indexed by the filtered poset of nilpotent ideals of
\pi_{0}(A)
. We then have a commuting diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{1}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{2}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(A_{\mathrm{prored}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{1}(A_{\mathrm{prored}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{2}(A_{\mathrm{prored}}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

The bottom maps are equivalences since
X\to Y_{1}
and
X\to Y_{2}
are nil-isomorphisms and laft (Remark
B.13
). We therefore have to show that for each point
x\in X(A_{\mathrm{prored}})=\colim_{I}X(\pi_{0}(A)/I)
, the map on fibres
Y_{1}(A)_{x}\to Y_{2}(A)_{x}
is an equivalence. To see this, it will suffice to show that for any nilpotent ideal
I\subseteq\pi_{0}(A)
and
x\in X(\pi_{0}(A)/I)
, the map

(24)

Y_{1}(A)\times_{Y_{1}(\pi_{0}(A)/I)}\{x\}\to Y_{2}(A)\times_{Y_{2}(\pi_{0}(A)/I)}\{x\}

is an equivalence. Indeed, the map
Y_{1}(A)_{x}\to Y_{2}(A)_{x}
is a filtered colimit of maps of the above form.

Now recall that
A\to\pi_{0}(A)/I
decomposes as a finite sequence
A=A_{n}\to A_{n-1}\to\dots\to A_{0}=\pi_{0}(A)/I
where each
A_{k}\to A_{k-1}
is a square zero extension by an
A_{0}
-module
J_{k}
(concentrated in a single degree, although we will not need this). To prove that (
24
) is an equivalence, we will proceed by induction on this tower, the case of
A_{0}
being evident. For the inductive step, note that there is a natural map of fibre sequences of spaces

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{1}(A_{k})\times_{Y_{1}(A_{0})}\{x\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{1}(A_{k-1})\times_{Y_{1}(A_{0})}\{x\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{1}(A_{0}\oplus J_{k}[1])\times_{Y_{1}(A_{0})}\{x\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{2}(A_{k})\times_{Y_{2}(A_{0})}\{x\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{2}(A_{k-1})\times_{Y_{2}(A_{0})}\{x\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{2}(A_{0}\oplus J_{k}[1])\times_{Y_{2}(A_{0})}\{x\}}

since
Y_{1}
and
Y_{2}
have deformation theory. By inductive hypothesis the middle vertical map is an equivalence, so it suffices to verify that the right vertical map is an equivalence. Unravelling
Definition

B.17
and using that the maps
X\to Y_{i}
are laft, one sees that the right vertical map fits into a pullback square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{1}(A_{0}\oplus J_{k}[1])\times_{Y_{1}(A_{0})}\{x\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{X/Y_{1},x}(J_{k}[2])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{2}(A_{0}\oplus J_{k}[1])\times_{Y_{2}(A_{0})}\{x\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{X/Y_{2},x}(J_{k}[2]).}

The right vertical map is an equivalence by the assumption that
T_{X/Y_{1}}\to T_{X/Y_{2}}
was an equivalence, so the result follows.
∎

Definition 5.18

(Zariski descent)
.

We say that a prestack
X\in\mathrm{PrStk}
satisfies
Zariski descent
if for all animated rings
A\in\CAlg^{\an}
and all families of animated rings

\{A\rightarrow A_{i}\}

for which the maps
\Spec(A_{i})\rightarrow\Spec(A)
are jointly surjective open immersions, we have

X(A)\xrightarrow{\sim}\lim X(S),

where the limit is indexed by all
S\in\CAlg^{\an}_{A}
such that
A\rightarrow S
factors through some
A_{i}
.

Theorem 7.3.5.2 in
[
HTT
]
implies the following ‘finite’ characterisation of Zariski descent:

Proposition 5.19
.

A prestack
X\in\mathrm{PrStk}
satisfies Zariski descent if and only if the following conditions hold:

(1)

X(0)\simeq\ast
is contractible;

(2)

Let us say that a square of animated rings

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{12}}

is a
Zariski square
if it is cocartesian (
A_{12}\simeq A_{1}\otimes_{A}A_{2}
) and the maps
\Spec(A_{i})\rightarrow\Spec(A)
are jointly surjective open immersions. Then
X
sends each Zariski square to a pullback square.

Remark 5.20
.

For any Zariski square, the induced square of
n
-truncations is a Zariski square as well, since
A\to A_{1}
and
A\to A_{2}
are flat. Consequently, each Zariski square is the limit of the Zariski squares of its
n
-truncations. A convergent prestack therefore satisfies Zariski descent if and only if it sends every Zariski square of eventually coconnective animated rings to a pullback.

Proposition 5.21
.

Let
X
be a prestack with deformation theory that satisfies Zariski descent, and let
X\rightarrow Y
be a formal moduli stack under
X
. Then the prestack
Y
satisfies Zariski descent as well.

Proof.

By Remark
5.20
, it suffices to verify that
Y(A)\simeq Y(A_{1})\times_{Y(A_{12})}Y(A_{2})
for any Zariski square of eventually coconnective animated rings. Let us fix such a Zariski square, as well as a filtered system of nilpotent ideals
I\subseteq\pi_{0}(A)
such that
\colim_{I}\pi_{0}(A)/I\cong A_{\mathrm{red}}
. For each such ideal, let us furthermore write
I_{i}=\pi_{0}(A_{i})\otimes_{\pi_{0}(A)}I_{i}
. For
i=\emptyset,1,2,12
, we then have that
\colim_{I}\pi_{0}(A_{i})/I_{i}\cong A_{i}\otimes_{A}A_{\mathrm{red}}\cong(A_{i})_{\mathrm{red}}
. Since
X\to Y
is laft, we have a cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underset{I}{\colim}X(\pi_{0}(A_{i})/I_{i})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underset{I}{\colim}Y(\pi_{0}(A_{i})/I_{i})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(A_{i,\mathrm{red}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y(A_{i,\mathrm{red}})}

for each
i=\emptyset,1,2,12
. The bottom map is an equivalence because
X\to Y
is a nil-isomorphism, so that the top map is an equivalence as well.
We therefore obtain a diagram of the form

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y(A_{1})\times_{Y(A_{12})}Y(A_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underset{I}{\colim}Y(\pi_{0}A/I)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underset{I}{\colim}\big(Y(\pi_{0}A_{1}/I_{1})\times_{Y(\pi_{0}A_{12}/I_{12})}Y(\pi_{0}A_{2}/I_{2})\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underset{I}{\colim}X(\pi_{0}A/I)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underset{I}{\colim}\big(X(\pi_{0}A_{1}/I_{1})\times_{X(\pi_{0}A_{12}/I_{12})}X(\pi_{0}A_{2}/I_{2})\big)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

in which the bottom map is an equivalence because
X
satisfies Zariski descent. Given a point
x\in\colim_{I}X(\pi_{0}(A)/I)
, we therefore need to check that the induced map on fibres
Y(A)_{x}\to Y(A_{1})_{x_{1}}\times_{Y(A_{12})_{x_{12}}}Y(A_{2})_{x_{2}}
is an equivalence, where
x_{i}
denotes the restriction of
x
to the corresponding Zariski open. For this it suffices to show that for any nilpotent ideal
I
and any point
x\in X(\pi_{0}(A)/I)
, the square

(25)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y(A)\times_{Y(\pi_{0}A/I)}\{x\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y(A_{1})\times_{Y(\pi_{0}A_{1}/I_{1})}\{x_{1}\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y(A_{2})\times_{Y(\pi_{0}A_{2}/I_{2})}\{x_{2}\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y(A_{12})\times_{Y(\pi_{0}A_{12}/I_{12})}\{x_{12}\}}

is cartesian. Indeed, one then finds that
Y(A)_{x}\simeq Y(A_{1})_{x_{1}}\times_{Y(A_{12})_{x_{12}}}Y(A_{2})_{x_{2}}
by taking the filtered colimit over all
I
.

We now proceed as in the proof of
Proposition

5.17
: the map
A\to\pi_{0}(A)/I
decomposes as a finite sequence
A=A(n)\to A(n-1)\to\dots\to A(0)=\pi_{0}A/I
where each
A(k)\to A(k-1)
is a square zero extension by an
A(0)
-module
J(k)
. Localising, we similarly have that
A_{i}\to\pi_{0}(A_{i})/I_{i}
decomposes as a finite sequence
A_{i}=A(n)_{i}\to\dots\to A(0)_{i}=\pi_{0}(A_{i})/I_{i}
of square zero extensions by the
A(0)_{i}
-modules
J(k)_{i}=J(k)\otimes_{A(0)}A(0)_{i}
. We will prove by induction on this tower that the square (
25
) is cartesian; when
k=0
, all spaces are contractible and the assertion is evident.

For the inductive step, note that for each
i=\emptyset,1,2,12
, there is a natural fibre sequence of spaces

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y(A(k)_{i})\times_{Y(A(0)_{i})}\{x_{i}\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y(A(k-1)_{i})\times_{Y(A(0)_{i})}\{x_{i}\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y(A(0)_{i}\oplus J(k)_{i}[1])\times_{Y(A(0)_{i})}\{x_{i}\}}

because
Y
has deformation theory. By inductive hypothesis, the middle terms for
i=\emptyset,1,2,12
fit into a cartesian square. It therefore suffices to show that the right terms also form a cartesian square. Since
Y
has deformation theory, there is a natural equivalence

Y(A(0)\oplus J(k)_{i}[1])\times_{Y(A(0))}\{x\}\simeq Y(A(0)_{i}\oplus J(k)_{i}[1])\times_{Y(A(0)_{i})}\{x_{i}\}.

and it suffices to verify that the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y(A(0)\oplus J(k)[1])\times_{Y(A(0))}\{x\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y(A(0)\oplus J(k)_{1}[1])\times_{Y(A(0))}\{x\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y(A(0)\oplus J(k)_{2}[1])\times_{Y(A(0))}\{x\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y(A(0)\oplus J(k)_{12}[1])\times_{Y(A(0))}\{x\}}

is cartesian. The functor
Y(A(0)\oplus-)\times_{Y(A(0))}\{x\}\colon\Mod_{A,\geq 0}\to\mathscr{S}
preserves all pullback diagrams preserved by the inclusion into
\Mod_{A}
, again because
Y
has deformation theory. We are therefore left with showing that
J(k)[1]\simeq J(k)_{1}[1]\times_{J(k)_{12}[1]}J(k)_{2}[1]
. Unravelling the definitions, this is nothing but Zariski descent for (the quasi-coherent sheaf associated to) the
A(0)
-module
J(k)
.
∎

Theorem 5.22

(Zariski descent for formal moduli stacks)
.

Given a derived scheme
X
, write
\mathfrak{U}
for the poset of affine opens of
X
.
Then there is canonical equivalence

\mathrm{ModuliStk}_{X/{R}}\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\lim_{U\in\mathfrak{U}^{{\mathrm{op}}}}\mathrm{ModuliStk}_{U/{R}}.

Proof.

Let us consider the cartesian fibration

\mathrm{ModuliStk}_{\mathfrak{U},{{R}}}\rightarrow\mathfrak{U}

obtained by restricting the cartesian fibration

\mathrm{ModuliStk}_{{R}}\rightarrow\mathrm{PrStk}_{{R}}^{\mathrm{def},\mathrm{laft-map}}

from
Construction

5.16

along the evident functor
\mathfrak{U}\to\mathrm{PrStk}_{{R}}^{\mathrm{def},\mathrm{laft-map}}
.
By
[
HTT
, Proposition 3.3.3.1]
, there is an equivalence

\lim_{U\in\mathfrak{U}}\mathrm{ModuliStk}_{U/{R}}\simeq\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathfrak{U}}^{\mathrm{cart}}(\mathfrak{U},\mathrm{ModuliStk}_{\mathfrak{U},{{R}}}),

where the right hand side denotes the
\infty
-category of cartesian sections of
\mathrm{ModuliStk}_{\mathfrak{U},{{R}}}\rightarrow\mathfrak{U}
.

We have a canonical functor

\Psi\colon\mathrm{ModuliStk}_{X/{R}}\longrightarrow\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathfrak{U}}^{\mathrm{cart}}(\mathfrak{U},\mathrm{ModuliStk}_{\mathfrak{U},{R}})

sending
(X\rightarrow Y)
to

\left\{U\longrightarrow Y^{\wedge}_{U}\right\}_{U\in\mathfrak{U}}.

Here the formal completion
Y^{\wedge}_{U}\simeq Y\times_{Y^{\sharp}}U^{\sharp}
is the prestack defined in
Definition

B.8
, where we have used the notation
F^{\sharp}(-):=F(-_{\mathrm{red}})
.
Note that each map
U\rightarrow Y
is laft by
[
DAG
, Proposition 5.3.10]
, so
Y^{\wedge}_{U}

agrees with
(Y/U)_{\inf}
.

The above functor is well-defined as each
U\longrightarrow Y^{\wedge}_{U}
is a formal moduli stack under
U
.
Indeed, the fact that
Y^{\wedge}_{U}\simeq(Y/U)_{\inf}
has deformation theory follows immediately from the definitions.
Since
U\rightarrow Y
is laft,
U\rightarrow Y^{\wedge}_{U}
is laft by
Lemma

B.14
and clearly an equivalence on reduced objects.

To construct an inverse to
\Psi
, we will need some notation. Write
\mathrm{PrStk}_{{R},<\infty}

for the
\infty
-category of prestacks defined only on coconnective objects. Let

\Stk_{R}^{\Zar}\subset\mathrm{PrStk}_{R}
(
\Stk_{{R},<\infty}^{\Zar}\subset\mathrm{PrStk}_{{R},<\infty}
) denote
the full subcategories spanned by all prestacks
Y
satisfying Zariski descent, i.e. satisfying
Y(0)\simeq\ast
and sending Zariski squares (of eventually coconnective rings) to pullbacks.
By Remark
5.20
, the functor

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{conv}\colon\mathrm{PrStk}_{{R},<\infty}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{PrStk}_{{R}};\quad X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lim_{n}X(\tau_{\leq n}-)}

sends sheaves to sheaves.

We then define

\Phi\colon\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathfrak{U}}^{\mathrm{cart}}(\mathfrak{U},\mathrm{ModuliStk}_{\mathfrak{U},{R}})\longrightarrow\mathrm{ModuliStk}_{X/{R}}

as the composite

\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathfrak{U}}^{\mathrm{cart}}(\mathfrak{U},\mathrm{ModuliStk}_{\mathfrak{U},{R}})\subset\mathrm{Fun}(\mathfrak{U},\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\Stk_{{R},<\infty}^{\Zar}))\xrightarrow{\colim}\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\Stk_{{R},<\infty}^{\Zar})\xrightarrow{\mathrm{conv}}\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\Stk_{{R}}^{\Zar}).

Note that
\colim
is computed by sheafifying the pointwise colimit. We have used
Proposition

5.21
to embed formal moduli stacks into Zariski sheaves.

We now want to verify that
\Phi
takes values in formal moduli stacks under
X
.
To this end, let us fix a family

\{U\rightarrow Y_{U})\}_{U\in\mathfrak{U}}\in\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathfrak{U}}^{\mathrm{cart}}(\mathfrak{U},\mathrm{ModuliStk}_{\mathfrak{U},{R}})

indexed by the poset
\mathfrak{U}
of affine opens
U
of
X
and let
(X\rightarrow Y)\in\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\Stk_{R}^{\Zar})
denote its image under
\Phi
. In a first step, we will prove that the natural map

\{U\rightarrow Y_{U}\}_{U\in\mathfrak{U}}\rightarrow\{U\rightarrow Y^{\wedge}_{U}\}_{U\in\mathfrak{U}}

is an equivalence, i.e. that for each
U\in\mathfrak{U}
, the following square in
\Stk^{\Zar}_{{R},<\infty}

is cartesian:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{U}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U^{\sharp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underset{U\in\mathfrak{U}}{\colim}Y_{U}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(\underset{U\in\mathfrak{U}}{\colim}Y_{U}\big)^{\sharp}.}

Note that the functor
(-)^{\sharp}\colon\mathrm{PrStk}_{{R},<\infty}\to\mathrm{PrStk}_{{R},<\infty}
preserves colimits and Zariski sheaves, and commutes with Zariski sheafification. For the last assertion, it suffices to observe that for any covering sieve
S\to\Spec(A)
for the Zariski topology and a map
f\colon\Spec(B)\to\Spec(A)^{\sharp}
, corresponding to
f_{\mathrm{red}}\colon\Spec(B_{\mathrm{red}})\to\Spec(A_{\mathrm{red}})
, the base change
f^{*}S^{\sharp}\to\Spec(B)
is a covering sieve as well: it is the unique covering sieve whose restriction to
\Spec(B_{\mathrm{red}})
coincides with
f_{\mathrm{red}}^{*}S
. Consequently, the natural map of Zariski sheaves
\colim_{U\in\mathfrak{U}}Y^{\sharp}_{U}\to(\colim_{U\in\mathfrak{U}}Y_{U})^{\sharp}
is an equivalence.

To see that the above square is cartesian, it then suffices to consider the natural transformation
\alpha
of
\mathfrak{U}
-indexed diagrams in
\Stk^{\Zar}_{{R},<\infty}
given by

Y_{U}\rightarrow(Y_{U})^{\sharp}

Write
\overline{\alpha}
for the induced transformation between the
\mathfrak{U}^{\rhd}
-indexed colimit cones.
The transformation
\alpha
is cartesian as for each
U\rightarrow V
, we have
Y_{U}\simeq(Y_{V})^{\wedge}_{U}\simeq Y_{V}\times_{V^{\sharp}}U^{\sharp}
, which implies by
[
HTT
, Theorem 6.1.0.6]
that the above square is also cartesian.

We will use this to show that
X\to Y
is a formal moduli problem. The prestack
Y
is convergent by construction and the map
X\rightarrow Y
is laft by (the derived analogue of)
[
SAG
, 17.4.3.3]
. To verify it has deformation theory, it suffices to check that for any pullback square of eventually coconnective animated rings

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}

with
B\rightarrow A
a nilpotent extension and any
f\in Y(B)
with image
f_{A}\in Y(A)
, the map

Y(\mathcal{O}_{B^{\prime}})\times_{Y(\mathcal{O}_{B})}\{f\}\rightarrow Y(\mathcal{O}_{B}\otimes_{B^{\prime}}A^{\prime})\times_{Y(\mathcal{O}_{B}\otimes_{B}A)}\{f_{A}\}

of sheaves on the topological space
|\Spec(B)|\cong|\Spec(B^{\prime})|
is an equivalence on global sections. Taking global sections, this implies that the fibres
Y(B^{\prime})\times_{Y(B)}\{f\}\to Y(A^{\prime})\times_{Y(A)}\{f_{A}\}
are equivalent, so that
Y(B^{\prime})\simeq Y(B)\times_{Y(A)}Y(A^{\prime})
.

Since
Y
is a Zariski sheaf, it suffices to check this after restricting to a
Zariski cover of
|\Spec(B)|
.
Let us pick a cover of
\Spec(B)
by affine opens
V_{i}\subset\Spec(B)
corresponding to affine opens
V_{i,\mathrm{red}}\subset\Spec(B_{\mathrm{red}})
such that
each composite

f_{i,\mathrm{red}}\colon V_{i,\mathrm{red}}\subset\Spec(B_{\mathrm{red}})\rightarrow Y_{\mathrm{red}}\simeq X_{\mathrm{red}}

factors through some affine open
U_{i}\subset X
. This implies that each restriction
f_{i}\colon V_{i}\rightarrow Y
of the map
f
factors through some
Y^{\wedge}_{U_{i}}
.
The claim follows as
Y^{\wedge}_{U_{i}}\simeq Y_{U_{i}}
by the above argument, and
Y_{U_{i}}
has deformation theory.

Finally, to see that
X\to Y
is an equivalence on reduced affine schemes, we note that precomposing with
A\mapsto A_{\mathrm{red}}
gives rise to a limit-preserving functor
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathrm{CR}^{\mathrm{red}},\mathcal{S})\rightarrow\mathrm{Fun}(\CAlg^{\an},\mathcal{S})
,
F\mapsto F(-_{\mathrm{red}})

which sends sheaves to sheaves.
Here
\mathrm{CR}^{\mathrm{red}}\subset\CAlg^{\an}
denotes the full subcategory of reduced discrete rings.
Passing to left adjoints, we see that restriction to

\mathrm{CR}^{\mathrm{red}}
commutes with sheafification. As each object
U\rightarrow Y_{U}
induces an equivalence on reduced affine schemes, we deduce that
X=\colim_{U\in\mathfrak{U}}U\rightarrow\colim_{U\in\mathfrak{U}}Y_{U}=Y
does so as well.

We may therefore conclude that the functor
\Phi
lands in formal moduli stacks, and the above argument also shows that
\Psi\circ\Phi
is equivalent to the identity.

To see that
\Phi\circ\Psi
is equivalent to the identity, let us fix a formal moduli stack
X\rightarrow Y
under
X
. It suffices to verify that the canonical map

\colim_{U\in\mathfrak{U}}Y^{\wedge}_{U}\rightarrow Y

is an equivalence of sheaves on eventually coconnective objects.
Since colimits in the
\infty
-topos
\Stk_{{R},<\infty}^{\Zar}
are universal in the sense of
[
HTT
, Definition 6.1.1.2]
, it suffices to check that for all maps
\Spec(A)\rightarrow Y
, the induced map

\colim_{U\in\mathfrak{U}}\left(Y^{\wedge}_{U}\times_{Y}\Spec(A)\right)\rightarrow\Spec(A)

is an equivalence.
But

Y^{\wedge}_{U}\times_{Y}\Spec(A)\simeq\Spec(A)\times_{Y^{\sharp}}U^{\sharp}\simeq\Spec(A)\times_{X^{\sharp}}U^{\sharp}

and hence the above map is an equivalence since

\colim_{U\in\mathfrak{U}}U^{\sharp}\rightarrow X^{\sharp}

is an equivalence.
∎

Proposition 5.23

(Monadicity of the tangent fibre)
.

Let
X
be a locally coherent qcqs derived scheme over an animated ring
{R}
.
Then the functor

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{X/-}[1]\colon\mathrm{ModuliStk}_{X/{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X})_{/T_{X/{R}}[1]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X\rightarrow Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(T_{X/Y}[1]\rightarrow T_{X/{R}}[1])}

in
Construction

5.16
is a monadic right adjoint.

The proof uses the following
\infty
-categorical result:

Lemma 5.24
.

Let
F\colon\mathscr{C}\leftrightarrows\mathscr{D}\colon U
be a monadic adjunction and let
G\colon\mathscr{D}^{\prime}\to\mathscr{D}
be a functor from an
\infty
-category
\mathscr{D}^{\prime}
with geometric realisations. If
UG
admits a left adjoint, then
G
admits a left adjoint.

Proof.

As in
[
HA
, Lemma 4.7.3.13]
, let
\mathscr{X}\subseteq\mathscr{D}
be the full subcategory of objects
D\in\mathscr{D}
such that
\Map_{\mathscr{D}}(D,G(-))
is corepresentable and let
L\colon\mathscr{X}\to\mathscr{D}^{\prime}
be the corresponding partial left adjoint. For any simplicial diagram
D_{\bullet}
in
\mathscr{X}
, the colimit
|L(D_{\bullet})|
corepresents
\Map_{\mathscr{D}}(|D_{\bullet}|,G(-))
, so that
\mathscr{X}
is closed under geometric realisations. Since
UG
admits a left adjoint,
\mathscr{X}
contains all the free objects
F(C)
with
C\in\mathscr{C}
. As every object in
\mathscr{D}
is the geometric realisation of a (
U
-split) simplicial diagram of free objects,
\mathscr{X}=\mathscr{D}
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
5.23
.

When
X=\Spec(B)
is affine, the tangent functor
T_{X/-}[1]
from
Construction

5.16
can be identified with the composite

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{X/-}[1]\colon\mathrm{ModuliStk}_{X/{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Moduli}_{B/{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{B})_{/T_{B/{R}}[1]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{B/}[1]}

where the first functor is the equivalence from Theorem
5.3
, restricting formal moduli stacks to Artinian extensions of
B
, and the second functor takes the tangent complex of the corresponding formal moduli problem. It then follows from Proposition
3.12
and Theorem
4.19
that
T_{X/-}[1]
is a monadic right adjoint preserving sifted colimits.

For non-affine
X
, let us first prove that
\mathrm{ModuliStk}_{X/{R}}
has sifted colimits and that
T_{X/-}[1]
preserves these. To this end, let us again write
\mathfrak{U}
for the category of Zariski open affine subsets of
X
.
By
Proposition

B.32
, have a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{ModuliStk}_{X/{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X})_{/T_{X/{R}}[1]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lim_{U\in\mathfrak{U}}\mathrm{ModuliStk}_{U/{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lim_{U\in\mathfrak{U}}(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{U})_{/T_{U/{R}}[1]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

where the vertical functors are equivalences by
Theorem

5.22
and
Corollary
