A.39
, respectively.
Each functor
\mathrm{ModuliStk}_{U/{R}}\rightarrow(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{U})_{/T_{U/{R}}[1]}
preserves sifted colimits, and this also holds true for the transition maps associated to inclusions
j\colon U\hookrightarrow V
of Zariski open subsets of
X

\mathrm{ModuliStk}_{V/{R}}\xrightarrow{(-)^{\wedge}_{U}}\mathrm{ModuliStk}_{U/{R}}\qquad(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{V})_{/T_{V/{R}}[1]}\xrightarrow{j^{*}}(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{U})_{/T_{U/{R}}[1]}.

For the functor
(-)^{\wedge}_{U}
, we use that the functors
T_{U/}[1]\colon\mathrm{ModuliStk}_{U/{R}}\rightarrow(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{U})_{/T_{U/{R}}[1]}
are conservative and hence
detect
sifted colimits.
It then follows from
[
HTT
, Corollary 5.3.6.10]
that
T_{X/-}[1]
is also a sifted colimit preserving functor between
\infty
-categories with sifted colimits.

Next, we will show that
T_{X/-}[1]
admits a left adjoint. By Lemma
5.24
, it suffices to verify that

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{ModuliStk}_{X/{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X})_{/T_{X/{R}}[1]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{X/-}[1]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{fib}}

admits a left adjoint, since taking the fibre is a monadic right adjoint. This follows immediately from Proposition
B.33
, using that the left adjoint
F\mapsto X_{F}
constructed there takes values in the full subcategory of formal moduli stacks since
X\to X_{F}
is a nil-isomorphism.

The claim now follows from the Barr–Beck–Lurie theorem
[
HA
, Theorem 4.7.3.5]

as the tangent fibre is conservative by
Proposition

5.17
.
∎

Proposition 5.25

(Identification of the monad)
.

The right adjoint in
Proposition

5.23
induces the partition Lie algebroid monad from
Definition

3.2
on the
\infty
-category
(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X})_{/T_{X/{R}}[1]}
.

Proof.

Consider the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{ModuliStk}_{X/{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underset{U\in\mathfrak{U}}{\lim}\mathrm{ModuliStk}_{U/{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underset{U\in\mathfrak{U}}{\lim}\mathrm{Moduli}_{\mathcal{O}(U)/{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underset{U\in\mathfrak{U}}{\lim}\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,\mathcal{O}(U)/{R})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X})_{/T_{X/{R}}[1]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underset{U\in\mathfrak{U}}{\lim}(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{U})_{/T_{U/{R}}[1]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{X/-}[1]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{U/-}[1]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{\mathcal{O}(U)/}[1]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{oblv}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

where the left square is defined as in
Proposition

5.23
and the two triangles arise from
Theorem

5.3
and
Theorem

4.19
. The claim then follows from the construction of the monad
\Lie_{\Delta,X/{R}}^{\pi}
in the proof of
Theorem

3.1
.
∎

We deduce:

Theorem 5.26

(Main theorem)
.

Let
X
be a locally coherent qcqs derived
{R}
-scheme over an animated ring
R
.

(1)

The shifted tangent fibre functor

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{ModuliStk}_{X/R}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X})_{T_{X/{R}}[1]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X\rightarrow Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(T_{X/Y}[1]\rightarrow T_{X/{R}}[1])}

lifts to an equivalence of
\infty
-categories

T_{X/-}[1]\colon\mathrm{ModuliStk}_{X/R}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta,X/{R}}.

Hence every partition Lie algebroid on
X
integrates uniquely to a formal moduli
stack under
X
.

(2)

This equivalence is functorial with respect to almost finitely presented maps. More precisely, if

X\rightarrow Y
is a locally almost finitely presented map, there is a commuting diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{ModuliStk}_{Y/}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lie_{\Delta,Y/R}^{\pi}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{ModuliStk}_{X/-}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lie_{\Delta,X/R}^{\pi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{Y/-}[1]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\wedge}_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{X/-}[1]}

where
f^{\sharp}(\mathfrak{g}\to T_{Y/{R}}[1])=f^{*}\mathfrak{g}\times_{(f^{*}L_{Y/{R}})^{\vee}[1]}T_{X/{R}}[1]
as pro-coherent sheaves.

Appendix A
Derived algebra recollections

The goal of this section is to introduce some elements of (homological) algebra that will be used throughout the text. We will start in Section
A.1
by reviewing the theory of derived commutative rings, the non-connective analogues of animated (or simplicial) rings. We will work in a somewhat general setting that also includes the derived filtered and graded rings studied in Section
2
. Section
A.2
discusses the theory of
pro-coherent modules
over an animated ring.

A.1.
Derived commutative rings

To set the stage, we will briefly recall the method of right-left extending monads from
[
BM
, Section 3.2]
and
[
BCN
, Section 2.2]
in the generality needed for us, see also
[
Rak20
, Section 4.2]
for the case of the commutative algebra monad.

Definition A.1
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be a presentable stable
\infty
-category with a left complete
t
-structure
(\mathscr{C}_{\geq 0},\mathscr{C}_{\leq 0})
. We call
\mathscr{C}
is
stably projectively generated
if there exists a full additive subcategory
\mathrm{Vect}^{\ft}(\mathscr{C})\subseteq\mathscr{C}_{\geq 0}
whose objects are compact in
\mathscr{C}
and projective in
\mathscr{C}_{\geq 0}
, generating
\mathscr{C}_{\geq 0}
under sifted colimits.

In this situation, there is an equivalence

\mathscr{C}\simeq\mathrm{Fun}^{\oplus}(\mathrm{Vect}^{\ft}(\mathscr{C})^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{Sp})

identifying
t
-structures
[
Rak20
, Remark 4.2.2]
. We define the subcategory of
flat objects
in
\mathscr{C}
as the ind-completion of
\mathrm{Vect}^{\ft}(\mathscr{C})
:

\mathrm{Flat}(\mathscr{C}):=\mathrm{Ind}(\mathrm{Vect}^{\ft}(\mathscr{C}))\subseteq\mathscr{C}.

Example A.2
.

For
A\in\Alg(\mathrm{Sp})

a connective algebra object in spectra, the stable
\infty
-category
\Mod_{A}
of (left)
A
-module spectra with its usual
t
-structure is stably projectively generated. In this case, we will take
\mathrm{Vect}^{\ft}_{A}
to be the
\infty
-category consisting of finite direct sums of
copies of
A
. Then
\mathrm{Flat}_{A}
is the
\infty
-category of flat
A
-modules in the sense of
[
HA
, Definition 7.2.2.10]
, by Lurie’s version of Lazard’s theorem (cf. Theorem 7.2.2.15 of [op.cit]).

Definition A.3
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be a projectively generated stable
\infty
-category with
t
-structure
(\mathscr{C}_{\geq 0},\mathscr{C}_{\leq 0})

and
\mathscr{D}
a presentable
\infty
-category. We will say that a functor
F\colon\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{D}
is
right-left extended
if it preserves sifted colimits and totalisations of finite cosimplicial diagrams with values in
\mathrm{Vect}^{\ft}(\mathscr{C})\subseteq\mathscr{C}
.

The following follows from
[
BCN
, Remark 2.45]
and the definition of
\mathrm{Flat}(\mathscr{C})
as an ind-completion:

Proposition A.4
.

The restriction functor

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathrm{RL}}(\mathscr{C},\mathscr{D})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}^{\omega}(\mathrm{Flat}(\mathscr{C}),\mathscr{D})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}(\mathrm{Vect}^{\ft}(\mathscr{C}),\mathscr{D})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

is fully faithful. Here
\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathrm{RL}}(\mathscr{C},\mathscr{D})\subseteq\mathrm{Fun}(\mathscr{C},\mathscr{D})
denotes the full subcategory of right-left extended functors and
\mathrm{Fun}^{\omega}(\mathrm{Flat}(\mathscr{C}),\mathscr{D})\subset\mathrm{Fun}(\mathrm{Flat}(\mathscr{C}),\mathscr{D})
is the full subcategory of functors preserving filtered colimits.

Definition A.5
.

Let us say that a functor
F\colon\mathrm{Vect}^{\ft}(\mathscr{C})\to\mathscr{D}
, or equivalently, a filtered-colimit preserving functor
F\colon\mathrm{Flat}(\mathscr{C})\to\mathscr{D}
,
admits a right-left extension
or is
right-left extendable
, if it is contained in the essential image. In that case, we will refer to its (unique) inverse image
\mathbb{L}F\colon\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{D}
as the
derived functor
, or
right-left extension
, of
F
. Note that this coincides with the non abelian derived functor in the sense of Dold–Puppe on the connective part
\mathscr{C}_{\geq 0}\simeq\mathscr{P}_{\Sigma}(\mathrm{Vect}^{\ft}(\mathscr{C}))
.

Example A.6
.

When
\mathscr{D}
is stable, every functor
F\colon\mathrm{Vect}^{\ft}(\mathscr{C})\to\mathscr{D}
which is a filtered colimit of finite degree functors is right-left extendable. See
[
BCN
, Corollary 2.49]
for details.

Example A.7
.

Let
\End_{\mathrm{RL}}^{\mathrm{Flat}(\mathscr{C})}(\mathscr{C})\subset\End_{\mathrm{RL}}(\mathscr{C})
be the full subcategory of endofunctors
\mathscr{C}\rightarrow\mathscr{C}
which preserve the full subcategory
\mathrm{Flat}(\mathscr{C})\subset\mathscr{C}
. Restriction
\End^{\mathrm{Flat}(\mathscr{C})}_{\mathrm{RL}}(\mathscr{C})\to\End^{\omega}(\mathrm{Flat}(\mathscr{C}))
is a monoidal equivalence onto the full subcategory of
\End^{\omega}(\mathrm{Flat}(\mathscr{C}))
spanned by right-left extendable functors. Consequently, any right-left extendable and filtered-colimit preserving monad
T\colon\mathrm{Flat}(\mathscr{C})\to\mathrm{Flat}(\mathscr{C})
induces a
derived monad

\mathbb{L}T\colon\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{C}
.

As an example of this kind of derived monad, consider the derived symmetric algebra monad. To define this, assume that
\mathscr{C}
is a derived algebraic context in the sense of
[
Rak20
, Section 4.2]
:

Definition A.8
.

We will say that a stably projectively generated
\infty
-category
\mathscr{C}
is a
derived algebraic context
if it comes equipped with a closed symmetric monoidal structure such that:

(1)

\mathrm{Vect}^{\ft}(\mathscr{C})
is stable under tensor products and contains the monoidal unit.

(2)

\mathrm{Vect}^{\ft}(\mathscr{C})
is an ordinary category.

(3)

For each
M\in\mathrm{Vect}^{\ft}(\mathscr{C})
, the object
\mathrm{Sym}^{n}(M)=\tau_{\leq 0}((M^{\otimes n}_{h\Sigma_{n}}))
is contained in
\mathrm{Vect}^{\ft}(\mathscr{C})
.

Condition (1) implies that
\mathscr{C}_{\geq 0}
is stable under the tensor product. We include condition (2) for simplicity; it implies that
\mathrm{Flat}(\mathscr{C})
is an ordinary category and that
\mathscr{C}^{\heartsuit}
is a symmetric monoidal abelian category with enough projectives. Extending the functors
\mathrm{Sym}^{n}\colon\mathrm{Vect}^{\ft}(\mathscr{C})\to\mathrm{Vect}^{\ft}(\mathscr{C})
by filtered colimits and taking the sum over all
n\geq 0
, one then obtains a monad
\mathrm{Sym}\colon\mathrm{Flat}(\mathscr{C})\to\mathrm{Flat}(\mathscr{C})
preserving filtered colimits. Since each
\mathrm{Sym}^{n}\colon\mathrm{Vect}^{\ft}(\mathscr{C})\to\mathrm{Vect}^{\ft}(\mathscr{C})
is of degree
\leq n
, this admits a derived monad
\mathbb{L}\mathrm{Sym}\colon\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{C}
.

Definition A.9
.

If
\mathscr{C}
is a derived algebraic context, we will refer to
\mathbb{L}\mathrm{Sym}
-algebras as
derived commutative algebras
and to connective
\mathbb{L}\mathrm{Sym}
-algebras as
animated commutative algebras
in
\mathscr{C}
, and write
\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}(\mathscr{C}_{\geq 0})\subseteq\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{der}}(\mathscr{C})
for the corresponding
\infty
-categories.

The (ordinary) category
\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{der}}(\mathscr{C})^{\heartsuit}
of discrete derived algebras is equivalent to the category of commutative algebras in
\mathscr{C}^{\heartsuit}
.

Example A.10
.

Let
\mathscr{C}=\Mod_{\mathbb{Z}}
with the usual
t
-structure, so that
\mathrm{Vect}^{\ft}(\mathscr{C})=\mathrm{Vect}^{\ft}_{\mathbb{Z}}
is the (ordinary) category of finite free abelian groups and
\mathrm{Flat}(\mathscr{C})=\mathrm{Flat}_{\mathbb{Z}}
is the (ordinary) category of flat abelian groups. This is a derived algebraic context, and the resulting monad
\mathrm{Sym}\colon\mathrm{Flat}_{\mathbb{Z}}\to\mathrm{Flat}_{\mathbb{Z}}
is the usual symmetric algebra monad. One thus obtains the
\infty
-categories
\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}=\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}(\Mod_{\mathbb{Z}})
and
\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{der}}=\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{der}}(\Mod_{\mathbb{Z}})
of animated and derived rings.

Example A.11
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be a derived algebraic context and let
\mathscr{I}
be a small symmetric monoidal (ordinary) category. Then
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathscr{I},\mathscr{C})
is a derived algebraic context as well with respect to the Day convolution product and the
t
-structure in which a diagram is (co)connective if it is pointwise (co)connective in
\mathscr{C}
.

Remark A.12
.

There is a natural equivalence
\mathbb{L}\mathrm{Sym}(V)\otimes\mathbb{L}\mathrm{Sym}(W)\simeq\mathbb{L}\mathrm{Sym}(V\oplus W)
for all
V,W\in\mathscr{C}
: this follows by right-left extension, using that there is such a natural equivalence for
V,W\in\mathrm{Flat}(\mathscr{C})
by construction. Consequently, the coproduct of two derived algebras is simply given by their tensor product. Since
\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{der}}(\mathscr{C})\to\mathscr{C}
preserves sifted colimits by construction, it follows that pushouts of derived algebras are computed as relative tensor products, as usual.

Remark A.13
.

For every
M\in\mathrm{Vect}^{\ft}(\mathscr{C})
, there is a natural map
(M^{\otimes n})_{h\Sigma_{n}}\to\mathrm{Sym}^{n}(M)
from the coinvariants in
\mathscr{C}
. By right-left extension, this induces a map
\Free_{\mathbb{E}_{\infty}}\to\mathbb{L}\mathrm{Sym}
from the
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-monad. In particular, any derived commutative algebra has an underlying
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebra and a module over a derived commutative algebra is simply a module over its underlying
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebra.

Definition A.14
.

For
A\in\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{der}}(\mathscr{C})
, we define the
\infty
-category of
derived (commutative)
A
-algebras
to be the under-category

\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{der}}_{A}(\mathscr{C})=\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{der}}(\mathscr{C})_{A/}.

The forgetful functor
\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{der}}_{A}(\mathscr{C})\to\Mod_{A}(\mathscr{C})
is monadic and we will write
\mathbb{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{A}\colon\Mod_{A}(\mathscr{C})\to\Mod_{A}(\mathscr{C})
for the associated monad.

Remark A.15
.

If
A\in\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{der}}(\mathscr{C})
is connective, then
\Mod_{A}(\mathscr{C})
is stably projectively generated for the
t
-structure inherited from
\mathscr{C}
. The monad
\mathbb{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{A}
is then derived from the restricted monad
\mathbb{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{A}\colon\mathrm{Flat}(\Mod_{A}(\mathscr{C}))\to\mathrm{Flat}(\Mod_{A}(\mathscr{C}))
.

The usual cotangent complex formalism for animated rings carries over to this setting. Below we will recall one way to see this, using an abstract method for constructing functors out of the
\infty
-category of
T
-algebras over some monad
T
(see also
[
Hol23
]
).

Proposition A.16
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be a stably projectively generated stable
\infty
-category. Suppose that
T\colon\mathrm{Flat}(\mathscr{C})\to\mathrm{Flat}(\mathscr{C})
is a filtered colimit preserving and right-left extendable monad, and let
\mathbb{L}T\colon\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{C}
be the derived monad
T
. Let
\mathrm{Kl}_{T}(\mathrm{Flat}(\mathscr{C}))
denote the full subcategory of
\mathrm{Alg}_{T}(\mathrm{Flat}(\mathscr{C}))
spanned by the free algebras. Assume that

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\colon\mathrm{Kl}_{T}(\mathrm{Flat}(\mathscr{C}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{D}}

is a functor such that

(1)

the composite
F\circ\mathrm{Free}_{T}\colon\mathrm{Flat}(\mathscr{C})\to\mathscr{D}
preserves filtered colimits and is right-left extendable,

(2)

F
preserves geometric realizations of simplicial objects in
\mathrm{Kl}_{T}(\mathrm{Flat}(\mathscr{C}))
that become split after applying
\mathrm{Forget}_{T}
.

Then there exists a unique sifted colimit preserving functor
\mathbb{L}F\colon\Alg_{T}(\mathscr{C})\to\mathscr{D}
that extends
F
, together with a natural equivalence
\mathbb{L}F\circ\mathrm{Free}\simeq\mathbb{L}(F\circ\mathrm{Free})
.

Proof.

Let
\mathrm{Fun}^{\omega}_{\mathrm{RL}}(\mathrm{Flat}(\mathscr{C}),\mathscr{D})
denote the
\infty
-category of filtered-colimit preserving functors that are right-left extendable, and notice that there is a right action

\mathrm{Fun}^{\omega}_{\mathrm{RL}}(\mathrm{Flat}(\mathscr{C}),\mathscr{D})\curvearrowleft\mathrm{Fun}^{\omega}_{\mathrm{RL}}(\mathrm{Flat}(\mathscr{C}),\mathrm{Flat}(\mathscr{C})).

In the above situation, we have an algebra object
T\in\mathrm{Fun}^{\omega}_{\mathrm{RL}}(\mathrm{Flat}(\mathscr{C}),\mathrm{Flat}(\mathscr{C}))
, and the functor
F\circ\mathrm{Free}_{T}\in\mathrm{Fun}^{\omega}_{\mathrm{RL}}(\mathrm{Flat}(\mathscr{C}),\mathscr{D})
is a right module for
T
. Using the same logic as
[
Hol23
, Proposition 2.2.15]
, taking right-left extension is lax monoidal and consequently, the functor
\mathbb{L}(F\circ\mathrm{Free}_{T})\colon\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{D}
is a right module for the derived monad
\mathbb{L}T
. On the other hand,
\mathrm{Forget}_{T}\colon\Alg_{T}(\mathscr{C})\to\mathscr{C}
is a left
T
-module. Since
\mathscr{D}
admits sifted colimits, we can define
\mathbb{L}F
by the formula:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{L}F:=\big|\mathrm{Bar}\big(\mathbb{L}(F\circ\mathrm{Free}_{T}),T,\mathrm{Forget}_{T}\big)\big|\colon\Alg_{T}(\mathscr{C})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{D}.}

One readily verifies all the desired properties.
∎

Construction A.17

(Cotangent complex as a derived functor)
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be a derived algebraic context and write
\Mod(\mathscr{C})
for the
\infty
-category of tuples
(A,M)
consisting of a derived commutative algebra
A
and an
A
-module
M
in
\mathscr{C}
. Notice that
\Mod
arises as the
\infty
-category of algebras over a derived monad
T_{\mathrm{mod}}
on
\mathscr{C}
, such that
\mathrm{Kl}_{T_{\mathrm{mod}}}(\mathscr{C})
is simply the ordinary
\infty
-category of free derived algebras
A=\mathrm{Sym}(V)
generated by
V\in\mathrm{Flat}(\mathscr{C})
, together with a free
A
-module
A\otimes W
on
W\in\mathrm{Flat}(\mathscr{C})
. We now have:

(1)

A functor between ordinary categories
\triv\colon\mathrm{Kl}_{T_{\mathrm{mod}}}(\mathrm{Flat}(\mathscr{C}))\to\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{der}}(\mathscr{C})
sending each
(A,M)
to the trivial square zero extension
A\oplus M
.

(2)

A functor between ordinary categories
\Omega^{1}\colon\mathrm{Kl}_{\mathrm{Sym}}(\mathrm{Flat}(\mathscr{C}))\to\mathrm{Kl}_{T_{\mathrm{mod}}}(\mathscr{C})
defined by the universal property that
\Map(\Omega^{1}_{A},(B,M))\simeq\Map(A,B\oplus M)
. One readily verifies that such a universal object exists for each free algebra
A=\mathrm{Sym}(V)
and is given by the tuple
(\mathrm{Sym}(V),\mathrm{Sym}(V)\otimes V)
, which is indeed in
\mathrm{Kl}_{T_{\mathrm{mod}}}(\mathscr{C})
.

Both of these functors satisfy the conditions of
Proposition

A.16
and hence admit derived functors
L\colon\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{der}}(\mathscr{C})\to\Mod(\mathscr{C})
and
\triv\colon\Mod(\mathscr{C})\to\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{der}}(\mathscr{C})
. The universal property of
\Omega^{1}
then implies that
L
is left adjoint to
\triv
. We refer to
L_{A}\in\Mod_{A}(\mathscr{C})
as the
cotangent complex
of
A
.

As usual, every map
f\colon A\to B
in
\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{der}}(\mathscr{C})
induces a map
(A,L_{A})\to(B,L_{B})
in
\Mod(\mathscr{C})
. Since the projection
\Mod(\mathscr{C})\to\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{der}}(\mathscr{C})
is a cocartesian fibration, as
\mathscr{C}\in\CAlg(\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}})
, this is equivalent to a map
f^{*}L_{A}\to L_{B}
in
\Mod_{B}
, whose cofibre is the
relative cotangent complex

L_{B/A}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}L_{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{B/A}.}

If
B=\mathbb{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{A}(M)
is the free derived
A
-algebra on
M\in\Mod_{A}(\mathscr{C})
, then
L_{B/A}\simeq B\otimes_{A}M
.

We conclude with some observations about finite type conditions on maps between animated commutative algebras, well-known for animated rings.

Definition A.18
.

If
\mathscr{C}
is a derived algebraic context and
A\in\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}(\mathscr{C})
, then we will say that:

(1)

a map
A\to B
in
\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}(\mathscr{C})
is
almost of finite presentation
if
B
is an almost compact object of
\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{A}(\mathscr{C})
, that is, each
\tau_{\leq n}B
is a compact object of
\tau_{\leq n}\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{A}(\mathscr{C})

[
HA
, Definition 7.2.4.8]
.

(2)

an
A
-module
M\in\Mod_{A}(\mathscr{C})
is
almost perfect
if
M\in\Mod_{A}(\mathscr{C})_{\geq k}
and
M
defines an almost compact object of
\Mod_{A}(\mathscr{C})_{\geq k}
.

Proposition A.19
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be a derived algebraic context and
f\colon A\to B
be a map of animated commutative algebras in
\mathscr{C}
. Then the following are equivalent:

(1)

f
is almost of finite presentation.

(2)

The map
\pi_{0}(A)\to\pi_{0}(B)
of commutative algebras in
\mathscr{C}^{\heartsuit}
is of finite presentation and
L_{B/A}
is an almost perfect
B
-module.

In addition, the following assertions hold:

(3)

If
B
is almost perfect as an
A
-module, then
f
is almost of finite presentation.

(4)

If
f
is almost of finite presentation and
\pi_{0}(A)\to\pi_{0}(B)
is surjective, then
B
is almost perfect as an
A
-module.

The proof of
Proposition

A.19
requires some preliminaries about cell decompositions and the resulting filtrations.

Construction A.20
.

Let
A\to B
be a map of animated commutative algebras in
\mathscr{C}
. By a
cell decomposition
of
B
, we will mean a sequence of animated filtered
A
-algebras
A=B_{-1}\to B_{0}\to B_{1}\to\dots\to B
constructed inductively according to the following procedure:

•

Having constructed
B_{n}
, choose a map
\chi_{n}\colon M_{n}\to(B/B_{n})[-1]
from a connective
B_{n}
-module
M_{n}
such that the cofibre is
(n+1)
-connective.

•

Define
B_{n+1}
as the pushout of derived filtered algebras

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{B_{n}}(M_{n})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\chi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}

where the top map sends
M_{n}
to zero. The map
B_{n}\to B
then factors canonically over
\iota\colon B_{n}\to B_{n+1}
.

Let us record some properties of such cell decompositions:

(a)

If
\pi_{0}(A)\to\pi_{0}(B)
is surjective, one can take
B_{0}=B_{-1}=A
.

(b)

Each
B_{n+1}
admits an increasing filtration whose associated graded is
\mathbb{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{B_{n}}(M_{n}[1])
: this filtration is the base change along
\chi
of the filtration on
B_{n}
obtained by applying
\mathbb{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{B_{n}}
to the zero object with increasing filtration given by
F_{0}(0)=M_{n}
and
F_{i}(0)=0
for
i>0
. Here we use Example
A.11
in the case
\mathscr{I}=(\mathbb{Z},\leq)
.

(c)

Each cofibre
B/B_{n}
is
(n+1)
-connective and hence each
M_{n}
is
n
-connective. To see this, we proceed by induction, the case
n=-1
being evident. For the inductive step, note that the map
\chi_{n}
factors as

\chi_{n}\colon M_{n}[1]\to B_{n+1}/B_{n}\to B/B_{n}.

Since the cofibre of the composite is
(n+2)
-connective by construction, to show that the cofibre
B/B_{n+1}
of the second map is
(n+2)
-connective it will suffice to show that
M_{n}[1]\to B_{n+1}/B_{n}
has
(n+2)
-connective cofibre. Using the filtration from
(b)
, this map decomposes as

M_{n}[1]\simeq F_{1}(B_{n+1})/F_{0}(B_{n+1})\to F_{2}(B_{n+1})/F_{0}(B_{n+1})\to\dots\to B_{n+1}/F_{0}(B_{n+1}).

It therefore suffices to verify that the cofibre of each of these maps is
(n+2)
-connective. These cofibres are given by
\mathbb{L}\mathrm{Sym}^{m}_{B_{n}}(M_{n}[1])
with
m\geq 2
, which fit into a cofibre sequence

(M_{n}[1]\otimes_{B_{n}}\dots\otimes_{B_{n}}M_{n}[1])_{h\Sigma_{m}}\to\mathbb{L}\mathrm{Sym}^{m}_{B_{n}}(M_{n}[1])\to F_{m}(M_{n}[1]).

The result now follows because the outer terms are both
(n+2)
-connective. For the right term, this follows because
F_{m}
is right-left extended from a functor on
\mathrm{Vect}^{\ft}(\mathscr{C})
with
1
-connective values.

(d)

For each
n
, consider the natural
B_{n+1}
-linear maps

B_{n+1}\otimes_{B_{n}}M_{n}[1]\to B_{n+1}\otimes_{B_{n}}(B_{n+1}/B_{n})\to L_{B_{n+1}/B_{n}}

where the first map is induced by
\chi
and the last map arises from the universal derivation
B_{n+1}\to B_{n+1}\oplus L_{B_{n+1}/B_{n}}
. The composite map is an equivalence of
(n+1)
-connective modules and the cofibre of the first map is
(n+2)
-connective, so that the cofibre of the natural map
B_{n+1}\otimes_{B_{n}}(B_{n+1}/B_{n})\to L_{B_{n+1}/B_{n}}
is
(n+3)
-connective. An inductive argument then shows that each
B\otimes_{B_{n}}(B/B_{n})\to L_{B/B_{n}}
has
(n+3)
-connective cofibre as well.

Lemma A.21
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be a derived algebraic context and let
\mathrm{Fun}((\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0},=),\mathscr{C})
be the
\infty
-category of non-negatively graded objects in
\mathscr{C}
, as in Example
A.11
. Given a graded algebra
A:=A^{\bullet}
and a graded module
M:=M^{\bullet}
, the following hold:

(1)

If
A^{0}\otimes_{A}M\simeq 0
, then
M\simeq 0
.

(2)

If
A
and
M
are connective and
A^{0}\otimes_{A}M
is an almost perfect
A^{0}
-module, then
M
is almost perfect.

Proof.

For (1), we prove the claim by induction on the weight. Clearly,
M^{0}=0
. Assuming that
M^{i}=0
for
i<n
, we have
M^{n}\simeq(A^{0}\otimes_{A}M)^{n}
, so that
M^{n}=0
as well.
For (2), note that

\pi_{0}(A^{0})\otimes_{\pi_{0}(A)}\pi_{0}(M)\cong\pi_{0}(A^{0}\otimes_{A}M)

is the module of indecomposables associated to the graded
\pi_{0}(A)
-module
\pi_{0}(M)
. Since this module is finitely generated, it follows that
\pi_{0}(M)
is a finitely generated
\pi_{0}(A)
-module. Lifting the generators provides a map
P\to M
from a finite free graded
A
-module, which is surjective on
\pi_{0}
. The cofibre
N
is then a
1
-connective
A
-module such that
A^{0}\otimes_{A}N
is an almost perfect
A^{0}
-module. Proceeding by induction, one finds that
M
is indeed almost perfect.
∎

Lemma A.22
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be a derived algebraic context and let
\mathrm{Fun}((\mathbb{Z},\leq),\mathscr{C})
be the
\infty
-category of objects in
\mathscr{C}
with an unbounded increasing filtration, as in Example
A.11
. Let
A=F_{\star}A
be a connective filtered algebra which is complete, i.e.
\lim_{n\to-\infty}F_{n}A=0
. Then a filtered
A
-module
M=F_{\star}M
is almost perfect if and only if it is complete and
\mathrm{Gr}(M)
is an almost perfect left
\mathrm{Gr}(A)
-module.

Proof.

If
M
is almost perfect, then
\mathrm{Gr}(M)
is almost perfect because taking the associated graded preserves compact objects and is
t
-exact up to a shift. To see that
M
is complete, let
n\geq 0
and pick a perfect filtered
A
-module
N_{n}
with a map
N_{n}\to M
whose cofibre is
n
-connective. Since
A
is complete,
N_{n}
is complete and hence
\lim F^{i}M\simeq\lim F^{i}(M/N_{n})
. By the Milnor sequence, the latter is
(n-1)
-connective. Since
n
was arbitrary, we conclude that
\lim F^{i}M=0
.

For the converse, it suffices that if
M
is
n
-connective, there exists a cofibre sequence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigoplus_{i=1}^{k}A\otimes F_{\geq w_{i}}V_{i}[n]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

where
M^{\prime}
is
(n+1)
-connective and the first term consists of free filtered
A
-modules with
V_{i}\in\mathrm{Vect}^{\ft}(\mathscr{C})
, concentrated in filtration weight
\geq w_{i}
. Indeed, this implies that
M^{\prime}
is a complete
(n+1)
-connective module such that
\mathrm{Gr}(M^{\prime})
is almost perfect; it follows by induction that each
\tau_{\leq m}(M)
is equivalent to the
m
-truncation of a perfect
A
-module, so that
M
is almost perfect.

Since
\mathrm{Gr}(M)
is an almost perfect
n
-connective
\mathrm{Gr}(A)
-module, the desired cofibre sequence exists at the graded level. In particular, there exists a map
f^{\prime}\colon\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\mathrm{Gr}(A)\otimes V_{i}(w_{i})[n]\to\mathrm{Gr}(M)
, where the domain is a sum of free
\mathrm{Gr}(A)
-modules in various weights
w_{i}
. Since the
V_{i}\in\mathrm{Vect}^{\ft}(\mathscr{C})
are projective in
\mathscr{C}_{\geq 0}
, we can lift
f^{\prime}
to a map
f
of filtered modules. The cofibre
M^{\prime}
of
f
is then a complete module with
(n+1)
-connective associated graded, and is thus itself
(n+1)
-connective by the Milnor sequence.
∎

Proof of
Proposition

A.19
.

For (1)
\Rightarrow
(2), note that
\pi_{0}(B)
is a compact object in
\pi_{0}(A)
-algebras and hence finitely presented. The cotangent complex
L_{B/A}
is almost perfect because for any filtered system of
k
-coconnective
B
-modules
M_{\alpha}
, the map
\colim\Map_{B}(L_{B/A},M_{\alpha})\to\Map_{B}(L_{B/A},\colim M_{\alpha})
is the base change of the equivalence

\colim\Map_{\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{A}(\mathscr{C})}\big(B,B\oplus M_{\alpha}\big)\to\Map_{\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{A}(\mathscr{C})}\big(B,\colim(B\oplus M_{\alpha})\big).

For (2)
\Rightarrow
(1), we claim that there exists a cell decomposition for
B
with the property that each
B_{n}
is almost of finite presentation over
A
. It then follows that
B
is the colimit of a sequence of almost finitely presented algebras that stabilises upon
k
-truncation for any
k
. This implies that
A\to B
almost finitely presented as well.

To construct this cell decomposition, we fix a finite presentation of
\pi_{0}(A)\to\pi_{0}(B)
. We can then choose a map
M_{0}=A\otimes V_{0}\to B
from a free
A
-module on
V_{0}\in\mathrm{Vect}^{\ft}(\mathscr{C})
such that
\pi_{0}(M_{0})\to\pi_{0}(B)
picks out the generators of
\pi_{0}(B)
. We then set
B_{0}=\mathbb{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{A}(M_{0})
. Next, we choose a map
M_{1}=A\otimes V_{1}\to\fib(B_{0}\to B)
from a free
B_{0}
-module on
V_{1}\in\mathrm{Vect}^{\ft}(\mathscr{C})
such that
\pi_{0}(M_{1})\to\pi_{0}(B_{0})=\mathrm{Sym}_{\pi_{0}(A)}(\pi_{0}(M_{0}))
picks out the relations of this presentation. The resulting
B_{1}
is almost finitely presented and
\pi_{0}(B_{1})=\pi_{0}(B)
. Now suppose we have constructed the desired cell decomposition up to
B_{n}
, for some
n\geq 1
. Since
A\to B_{n}
is a almost of finite presentation, the first part of the proof shows that
L_{B_{n}/A}
is an almost perfect
B_{n}
-module. Consequently,
L_{B/B_{n}}
is an almost perfect
B
-module. Since

B\otimes_{B_{n}}(B/B_{n})\to L_{B/B_{n}}

has
(n+3)
-connective cofibre, it follows that we can choose an
n
-connective free
B_{n}
-module
M_{n}=B_{n}\otimes V_{n}[n]
with
V_{n}\in\mathrm{Vect}^{\ft}(\mathscr{C})
, together with a map
\chi\colon M_{n}\to(B/B_{n})[-1]
that is surjective on
\pi_{n}
. The resulting map
B_{n}\to B_{n+1}
is then almost finitely presented, so that the composite
A\to B_{n+1}
is almost finitely presented as well.

For assertion (4), suppose that
f
is almost finitely presented and that
\pi_{0}(A)\to\pi_{0}(B)
is surjective, and consider the above cell decomposition. Then
B_{0}=A
, and we claim that each
B_{n}
is an almost perfect
A
-module. The colimit
B
is then an almost perfect
A
-module as well, because the cofibre of each
B_{n}\to B_{n+1}
is
(n+1)
-connective. Note that by part
(b)
of Construction
A.20
,
B_{n+1}
comes with an increasing filtration by
B_{n}
-modules whose associated graded
\mathbb{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{B_{n}}(M_{n}[1])
is almost perfect: indeed, each
\mathbb{L}\mathrm{Sym}^{k}_{B_{n}}(M_{n}[1])
is almost perfect and (at least)
k
-connective, since
M_{n}
was a connective almost perfect
B_{n}
-module. It follows that
B_{n+1}
is an almost perfect
B_{n}
-module, so that an inductive argument shows that
B_{n}\in\mathrm{APerf}_{A}(\mathscr{C})
(the case
n=0
being evident).

Finally, let us prove assertion (3). Consider the cell decomposition for
B
defined inductively by setting
B_{-1}=A
and

M_{n}=(B/B_{n})[-1].

We claim that
B
is almost perfect as a
B_{n}
-module for each
B_{n}
. Assuming this, it follows by induction that each
B_{n}\to B_{n+1}
is almost finitely presented, so that
A\to B_{n+1}
is almost finitely presented as well. Since the sequence of truncations
\tau_{\leq k}B_{n}
is eventually constant, the colimit
B
is then an almost finitely presented
A
-algebra as well.

We prove our claim by induction, the case
n=-1
being evident. Assuming
B
is an almost perfect
B_{n}
-module, we endow
B_{n+1}
with the increasing filtration from part
(b)
of Construction
A.20
and
B
with the increasing filtration where
F_{0}B=B_{n}
and
F_{i}B=B
for
i\geq 1
. Then
F_{\star}B
is an
F_{\star}B_{n+1}
-module, and it it suffices to verify that it is almost perfect as a filtered module. By
Lemma

A.21
, it suffices to verify that the associated graded is an almost perfect module over
\mathrm{Gr}(B_{n+1})=\mathbb{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{B_{n}}(B/B_{n})
. We then have a cofibre sequence of
\mathrm{Gr}(B_{n+1})
-modules
B_{n}(0)\to\mathrm{Gr}(B)\to(B/B_{n})(1)
of where the outer terms are concentrated in weight
0
and
1
. By
Lemma

A.21
, it therefore suffices to verify that the graded
B_{n}
-modules

\displaystyle B_{n}\otimes_{\mathbb{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{B_{n}}(B/B_{n})}B_{n}

\displaystyle\simeq\mathbb{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{B_{n}}(B/B_{n}[1])

\displaystyle B_{n}\otimes_{\mathbb{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{B_{n}}(B/B_{n})}(B/B_{n})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathbb{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{B_{n}}(B/B_{n}[1])\otimes_{B_{n}}B/B_{n}

are almost perfect
B_{n}
-modules. This follows from the fact that
B/B_{n}
is an almost perfect
B_{n}
-module and that
\mathbb{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{B_{n}}(B/B_{n}[1])
is almost perfect, because each
\mathbb{L}\mathrm{Sym}^{k}_{B_{n}}(B/B_{n}[1])
is almost perfect and
k
-connective.
∎

A.2.
Pro-coherent modules

As highlighted in
[
GR17a
]
, the usual
\infty
-category of quasi-coherent sheaves on a derived scheme (or prestack) is not very well-adapted to deformation theory. For schemes that are locally almost finitely presented over a field, it is better to consider their categories of ind-coherent sheaves instead. In this situation, Serre duality provides a natural identification between ind-coherent sheaves and the dual notion of pro-coherent sheaves: on an almost finitely presented scheme
X
over a field
k
, this is simply given by
\mathrm{Ind}(\mathrm{Coh}_{X})\simeq\mathrm{Ind}(\mathrm{Coh}_{X}^{\mathrm{op}})
.
To also deal with situations without Serre duality, where we cannot directly use the machinery of ind-coherent sheaves, let us introduce the following provisional modification of the
\infty
-category of pro-coherent sheaves over an affine derived scheme:

Definition A.23
.

Given an animated ring
A
, we will write
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}
for the
\infty
-category of exact functors
F\colon\Mod_{A}\to\mathrm{Sp}
satisfying the following two conditions:

(1)

They are
convergent
, in the sense that they preserve limits of Postnikov towers in
\Mod_{A}
.

(2)

They are
almost finitely presented
, in the sense that for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
and each filtered diagram of
n
-coconnective
A
-modules
M_{\alpha}
, the map
\colim_{\alpha}F(M_{\alpha})\to F(\colim M_{\alpha})
is an equivalence.

Warning A.24
.

For a general animated ring, the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}
is not very manageable and one should view this as an ad-hoc definition. The definition will essentially only become useful when
A
is a coherent animated ring: in that case
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}\simeq\mathrm{Ind}(\mathrm{Coh}_{A}^{\mathrm{op}})
can be identified with the compactly generated
\infty
-category of pro-coherent sheaves on
A
.

Keeping this warning in mind, let us nonetheless record some formal properties of
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}
.

Notation A.25
.

If
\mathscr{C}
is a stable
\infty
-category with a
t
-structure, we let
\mathscr{C}^{+}=\bigcup_{n}\mathscr{C}_{\leq n}
,
\mathscr{C}^{-}=\bigcup_{n}\mathscr{C}_{\geq n}
and
\mathscr{C}^{b}=\mathscr{C}^{+}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-}
be the full subcategories of eventually coconnective, eventually connective and bounded objects
[
HA
]
.

Lemma A.26
.

Let
A
be an animated ring. Then the following
\infty
-categories are naturally equivalent:

(1)

The
\infty
-category
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}
.

(2)

The
\infty
-category of exact functors
\Mod^{+}_{A}\to\mathrm{Sp}
that are almost finitely presented.

(3)

The
\infty
-category of left exact functors
F\colon\Mod^{+}_{A}\to\mathscr{S}
that are almost finitely presented.

(4)

The
\infty
-category of functors
F\colon\Mod^{+}_{A,\geq 0}\to\mathscr{S}
that are locally almost finitely presented and preserve the terminal object and pullbacks along a
\pi_{0}
-surjection.

(5)

The
\infty
-category of functors
F\colon\Mod_{A,\geq 0}\to\mathscr{S}
preserving limits of almost eventually constant towers and satisfying the conditions from (4).

Proof.

The equivalence between (1) and (2) is given by restriction and right Kan extension along
j\colon\Mod^{+}_{A}\hookrightarrow\Mod_{A}
. Recall that the right Kan extension is given explicitly by
j_{*}F(M)=\lim F(\tau_{\leq n}M)
. The equivalence between (2) and (3) is given by postcomposition with
\Omega^{\infty}
. The equivalence between (3) and (4) is given by restriction along the inclusion
i_{1}\colon\Mod^{b}_{A}\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\Mod^{+}_{A}
and
i_{2}\colon\Mod^{+}_{A,\geq 0}\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\Mod_{A}^{b}
of bounded (resp. bounded connective) objects. The inverse is given by right Kan extension along
i_{2}
followed by left Kan extension along
i_{1}
. The equivalence between (4) and (5) follows by restriction and right Kan extension along
\Mod^{+}_{A,\geq 0}\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\Mod_{A,\geq 0}
.
∎

Lemma A.27
.

Let
A
be an animated ring and let
\kappa
be a regular cardinal such that
\tau_{\leq n}\colon\Mod_{A}\to\Mod_{A}
preserves
\kappa
-compact objects. Then restriction and left Kan extension along
i\colon\Mod_{A}^{+,\kappa}\hookrightarrow\Mod^{+}_{A}
induces an equivalence between
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}
and the
\infty
-category of exact functors
F\colon\Mod^{+,\kappa}_{A}\to\mathrm{Sp}
preserving colimits of
\kappa
-small filtered diagrams of
n
-coconnective objects, for each
n
.

Proof.

Since
\tau_{\leq n}
preserves
\kappa
-small objects, for any
n
-coconnective
A
module
M
the inclusion
\big(\Mod_{A,\leq n}^{\kappa}\big)_{/M}\hookrightarrow\big(\Mod^{+,\kappa}_{A}\big)_{/M}
is cofinal. Using that
\big(\Mod_{A,\leq n}^{\kappa}\big)_{/M}
is filtered, one readily sees that
i_{!}i^{*}F\simeq F
for any
F\in\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}
. The identification of its essential image follows from the fact that any filtered colimit can be written as a
\kappa
-filtered colimit of
\kappa
-small filtered colimits.
∎

Corollary A.28
.

Let
A
be an animated ring
A
. Then
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}
is a stable presentable
\infty
-category.

Proof.

By
Lemma

A.27
,
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}\subseteq\mathrm{Fun}\big(\Mod^{+,\kappa}_{A},\mathrm{Sp}\big)
is the full subcategory of functors preserving certain types of colimits. It is hence presentable by the proof of
[
HTT
, Proposition 5.5.3.8]
.
∎

Corollary A.29
.

Let
F\colon\Mod_{A}\to\mathrm{Sp}
be an exact functor which is accessible and convergent and consider the
\infty
-category
\big(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}\big)_{/F}
of functors in
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}
equipped with a natural transformation to
F
. This
\infty
-category admits a terminal object
F^{\mathrm{aft}}
, which we will refer to as the
almost finitely presented approximation
to
F
.

Proof.

Since
F
is convergent and
\kappa
-accessible for some uncountable
\kappa
, it suffices to verify that there exists a universal object
F^{\mathrm{aft}}\in\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}
equipped with a natural transformation to the restriction
F\colon\Mod_{A}^{+,\kappa}\to\mathrm{Sp}
. The previous proof shows that for large enough
\kappa
, the inclusion
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}\subseteq\mathrm{Fun}\big(\Mod_{A}^{+,\kappa},\mathrm{Sp}\big)
is accessible and preserves colimits, and hence admits a right adjoint.
∎

For any
A
, there is an adjoint pair

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\upiota\colon\Mod_{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}\penalty\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\mskip 2.0mu\upupsilon}

where the left adjoint sends an
A
-module
M
to the exact functor
M\otimes_{A}(-)\colon\Mod^{+}_{A}\to\mathrm{Sp}
and the right adjoint sends any
F\colon\Mod^{+}_{A}\to\mathrm{Sp}
to the
A
-module
\upupsilon(F)=\lim_{n}F(\tau_{\leq n}A)
.

Observation A.30
.

The
\infty
-category
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}
comes equipped with a left complete
t
-structure whose connective part consists of those
F\colon\Mod^{+}_{A}\to\mathrm{Sp}
that are right
t
-exact. This induces an equivalence

\Mod_{A,\geq 0}\simeq\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A,\geq 0}.

Indeed, the equivalences of
Lemma

A.26
identify
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A,\geq 0}
with the
\infty
-category of colimit-preserving functors
F\colon\Mod_{A,\geq 0}\to\mathscr{S}
, which is equivalent to
\Mod_{A,\geq 0}
via sending such a functor
F
to the
A
-module
F(A)\in\Mod_{A,\geq 0}
.

Consequently,
\Mod_{A}
is the right completion of
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}
. In particular, we will typically identify eventually connective
A
-modules with the corresponding object in
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}
. If
A
is eventually coconnective, then
\mathrm{QC}_{A}\to\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}
is fully faithful on all modules (not just the eventually connective ones).

Let
A
be an animated ring and
F\in\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}
. For each
N\in\Mod^{+}_{A}
, the spectrum
F(N)
inherits a natural
A
-module structure from the
A
-module structure on
N
. More precisely, one can this describe this
A
-module structure on
F(N)
by the exact functor
\mathrm{Perf}(A)^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{Sp}
sending
M
to
F(M^{\vee}\otimes_{A}N)
.
Using this, the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}
is tensored over
\Mod_{A}
via

(26)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\otimes_{A}\colon\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}\times\Mod_{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A};\quad(M\otimes_{A}F)(N)=M\otimes_{A}(F(N))}

for each
N\in\Mod^{+}_{A}
. This preserves colimits in each variable and the functor
\upiota\colon\Mod_{A}\to\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}
is compatible with the tensoring.

Definition A.31
.

Let
M\in\Mod_{A}
be an
A
-module. Then the tensoring
M\otimes_{A}-\colon\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}\to\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}
admits a right adjoint. We define the
pro-coherent dual

M^{\vee}
of
M
to be the value of this right adjoint on
A
.

Example A.32
.

If
M
is a perfect
A
-module, then the adjoint to
M\otimes_{A}-\colon\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}\to\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}
is given by tensoring with the dual perfect module. Consequently, the pro-coherent dual of a perfect
A
-module is simply its usual
A
-linear dual. In terms of functors, one can also identify this with the functor
\mathrm{Hom}_{A}(M,-)\colon\Mod_{A}\to\mathrm{Sp}
. Using this and the fact that
(-)^{\vee}
sends colimits in
\Mod^{-}_{A}
to limits in
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}
, it follows that for any eventually connective
M\in\Mod^{-}_{A}
, its pro-coherent dual
M^{\vee}
is the almost finitely presented approximation to
\mathrm{Hom}_{A}(M,-)
in the sense of Corollary
A.29
.

We will be particularly interested in the pro-coherent duals of
almost perfect

A
-modules: in this case,
M^{\vee}
is given by
\mathrm{Hom}_{A}(M,-)\colon\Mod_{A}\to\mathrm{Sp}
, which is already almost finitely presented.

Definition A.33
.

We will say that a pro-coherent module
F\in\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}
is
dually almost perfect
if
F\colon\Mod_{A}\to\mathrm{Sp}
is corepresented by an almost perfect
A
-module. Taking pro-coherent duals therefore determines an equivalence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\vee}\colon\mathrm{APerf}_{A}^{\mathrm{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{dAPerf}_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

between almost perfect
A
-modules and dually almost perfect pro-coherent
A
-modules.

Lemma A.34
.

Let
M\in\Mod_{A}
be an
A
-module and let
M^{\vee}
be its pro-coherent
A
-linear dual. Then pro-coherent duality determines an equivalence

(27)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\vee}\colon\big((\mathrm{APerf}_{A})_{M/}\big)^{\mathrm{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{dAPerf}_{A})_{/M^{\vee}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

Proof.

The functor (
27
) defines a map of right fibrations covering the equivalence
\mathrm{APerf}_{A}^{\mathrm{op}}\simeq\mathrm{dAPerf}_{A}
. It therefore suffices to verify that it induces an equivalence on fibres, i.e. that the map
\Map_{\Mod_{A}}(M,N)\to\Map_{\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}}(N^{\vee},M^{\vee})
is an equivalence for every
N\in\mathrm{APerf}_{A}
. To see this, recall that
N^{\vee}\colon\Mod_{A}\to\mathrm{Sp}
is corepresented by
N
and
M^{\vee}\colon\Mod_{A}\to\mathrm{Sp}
is the almost finitely presented approximation to the functor corepresented by
M
, so that
\Map_{\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}}(N^{\vee},M^{\vee})
is equivalent to the space of maps between the functors corepresented by
N
and
M
. Unravelling the definitions, one then sees that
\Map_{\Mod_{A}}(M,N)\to\Map_{\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}}(N^{\vee},M^{\vee})
is an equivalence by the Yoneda lemma.
∎

Definition A.35
.

Let
A
be an animated ring. For all
-\infty\leq a\leq b\leq\infty
, we will say that an object
F\in\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}
has
tor-amplitude
in
[a,b]
if for any discrete
A
-module
M\in\Mod_{A}^{\heartsuit}
,

\pi_{n}F(M)\neq 0\qquad\Longrightarrow\qquad a\leq n\leq b.

Write
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee,[a,b]}_{A}\subseteq\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}
for the full subcategory on the objects with tor-amplitude in
[a,b]
.

Example A.36
.

We have the following special cases:

(1)

A pro-coherent module
F
has tor-amplitude in
[0,\infty]
if and only if
F
is connective. In particular,
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee,[0,\infty]}_{A}\simeq\Mod_{A,\geq 0}
is equivalent to the ordinary
\infty
-category of connective
A
-modules.

(2)

\mathrm{QC}^{\vee,[0,0]}_{A}\simeq\mathrm{Flat}_{A}
coincides with the full subcategory of
\Mod_{A,\geq 0}
spanned by the
flat

A
-modules.

(3)

Let
F=M^{\vee}
be dually almost perfect. Then
M^{\vee}
has tor-amplitude in
[-b,-a]
if and only if
M
has tor-amplitude in
[a,b]
.

Let us now turn to the functoriality of
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}
in the animated ring
A
.

Definition A.37
.

Let
f\colon A\to B
be a map of rings. We will write
f^{*}\colon\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}\leftrightarrows\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{B}\penalty\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\mskip 2.0muf_{*}
for the adjoint pair whose left adjoint is given by precomposition with
f_{*}\colon\Mod^{+}_{B}\to\Mod^{+}_{A}
.

Note that
f^{*}
fits in a commuting square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Mod_{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Mod_{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{B}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\upiota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\upiota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}

In the presence of Serre duality,
f^{*}
is Serre dual to the
f^{!}
-functor on ind-coherent sheaves
[
Gai11
, Section 9.2.3]

Lemma A.38
.

If
f\colon A\to B
has finite tor-amplitude, then the right adjoint
f_{*}
preserves colimits. Furthermore, for any pullback square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spec(B^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spec(B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spec(A^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spec(A).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

where
f
has finite tor-amplitude, the Beck–Chevalley map
g^{*}f_{*}\to f^{\prime}_{*}g^{\prime*}
is an equivalence.

Proof.

If
f
has finite tor-amplitude, then
f_{*}
is simply given by restriction along
f^{*}\colon\Mod^{+}_{A}\to\Mod^{+}_{B}
. Using this, one sees that for every
F\in\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A^{\prime}}
and
M\in\Mod^{+}_{B}
, the Beck–Chevalley map
(g^{*}f_{*}F)(M)\to(f^{\prime}_{*}g^{\prime*}F)(M)
is given by the image under
F
of the Beck–Chevalley map
g^{*}f_{*}M\to f^{\prime}_{*}g^{\prime*}M
, which is an equivalence.
∎

Corollary A.39
.

The functor
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}\colon\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{{R}}\to\mathrm{Pr^{L}}
satisfies descent with respect to universal descent morphisms of finite tor-amplitude, in the sense of
[
SAG
, Definition D.3.1.1]
. In particular, it satisfies fppf descent.

Proof.

Using that
A\mapsto\Mod_{A}
preserves products, one readily sees that
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}
preserves finite products of animated rings. Next, let
f\colon A\to B
be a universal descent morphism of finite tor-amplitude. Let
B^{\bullet}
be the corresponding Čech nerve and consider the map
\theta\colon\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}\to\lim\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{B^{\bullet}}
. Using
Lemma

A.38
and
[
HA
, Corollary 4.7.5.3]
, we see that
\theta
is an equivalence if
f^{*}\colon\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}\to\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{B}
is conservative. To see this, take
F\in\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}
such that
f^{*}F\simeq 0
. The objects
M\in\Mod_{A}
such that
F(M)\simeq 0
form a stable subcategory of
\Mod_{A}
that contains
f_{*}(\Mod_{B})
and is closed under retracts; since
f
was a universal descent morphism, this means that it contains every
A
-module.
∎

We now specialise to the case where
A
is a coherent animated ring.

Definition A.40
.

An animated ring
A
is said to be
coherent
if the standard
t
-structure on
\Mod_{A}
restricts to a
t
-structure on
\mathrm{APerf}_{A}
. Equivalently,
\pi_{0}(A)
is coherent and each
\pi_{n}(A)
is a finitely presented
\pi_{0}(A)
-module.

Definition A.41
.

Assume
A
is a coherent animated ring. An almost perfect
A
-module
M
is
coherent
if it is eventually coconnective. Equivalently, an
A
-module
M
is coherent if all homotopy groups
\pi_{n}M
are finitely presented over
\pi_{0}A
, and only finitely many of
\pi_{n}M
are non-zero. We denote
\mathrm{Coh}_{A}\subset\mathrm{APerf}_{A}
the full subcategory of coherent modules.

Lemma A.42
.

If
A\in\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}
is coherent, then
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}
is equivalent to the categories of:

(1)

Exact functors
F\colon\mathrm{Coh}_{A}\to\mathrm{Sp}
.

(2)

Functors
F\colon\mathrm{Coh}_{A,\geq 0}\to\mathscr{S}
that preserve the terminal object and pullbacks along
\pi_{0}
-surjections.

(3)

Functors
F\colon\mathrm{APerf}_{A,\geq 0}\to\mathscr{S}
that preserve almost eventually constant towers, the terminal object and pullbacks along
\pi_{0}
-surjections.

In particular,
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}\simeq\mathrm{Ind}(\mathrm{Coh}_{A}^{\mathrm{op}})
is compactly generated.

Proof.

This follows from
Lemma

A.27
with
\kappa=\omega
, as well as the argument from
Lemma

A.26
.
∎

Remark A.43
.

Let
A
be a coherent animated ring with dualising complex
\omega_{A}
. Then the
t
-structure on
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}
from Observation
A.30
is Serre dual to the
t
-structure on
\mathrm{Ind}(\mathrm{Coh}_{A})
whose connective part is generated by
\omega_{A}
.

Corollary A.44
.

The following holds for a map of coherent animated rings
f\colon A\to B
:

(1)

If
f
has finite tor-amplitude, then the functor
f^{*}\colon\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}\to\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{B}
coincides with
\mathrm{Ind}(f^{*})
, for
f^{*}\colon\mathrm{Coh}_{A}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{Coh}_{B}^{\mathrm{op}}
.

(2)

If
f
is finite, then
f^{*}
has a left adjoint
f_{!}\colon\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}(B)\to\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}(A)
given by the ind-completion of
f_{*}\colon\mathrm{Coh}_{B}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{Coh}_{A}^{\mathrm{op}}
.

Corollary A.45
.

Let
A
be a coherent animated ring. Then
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}
carries a unique closed symmetric monoidal structure whose restriction to
\mathrm{dAPerf}_{A}\simeq\mathrm{APerf}_{A}^{\mathrm{op}}
is equivalent to the usual symmetric monoidal structure on almost perfect
A
-modules, i.e.
M^{\vee}\otimes_{A}N^{\vee}=(M\otimes_{A}N)^{\vee}
for all
M,N\in\mathrm{APerf}_{A}
. Every map
f\colon A\to B
between coherent animated rings induces a symmetric monoidal functor
f^{*}\colon\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}\to\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{B}
.

Proof.

Using part (
3
) of
Lemma

A.42
, it suffices to verify that
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}\subseteq\mathrm{Fun}(\mathrm{APerf}_{A,\geq 0},\mathscr{S})
is a monoidal left Bousfield localisation with respect to the Day convolution product. This follows from the fact that if
F
is a functor as in (
3
) and
M\in\mathrm{APerf}_{A,\geq 0}
, then
F(M\otimes_{A}-)
satisfies the conditions from (
3
) as well.
∎

Remark A.46
.

One readily verifies that
\upiota\colon\Mod_{A}\to\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}
is symmetric monoidal and that the tensoring from (
26
) is induced by this symmetric monoidal functor. It follows that the pro-coherent dual of an
A
-module
M
from
Definition

A.31
coincides with the dual of
\upiota(M)
with respect to the symmetric monoidal structure from Corollary
A.45
.

Appendix B
Derived algebraic geometry recollections

We recall the following terminology:

Definition B.1

(Prestacks)
.

A
prestack
is an accessible functor
X\colon\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}\to\mathscr{S}
from animated rings to spaces. We will write
\mathrm{PrStk}
for the
\infty
-category of prestacks. Given a prestack
S
, we let

(1)

\mathrm{PrStk}_{/S}
be the
\infty
-category of
S
-prestacks
, that is, prestacks
X
endowed with a map
X\to S
.

(2)

\mathrm{Aff}_{/S}
be the
\infty
-category of
affine derived schemes
with a map to
S
, i.e. all
\Spec(A)\to S
where
\Spec(A)
is the functor corepresented by an animated ring
A
.

The
\infty
-category of
S
-prestacks can then also be identified with the
\infty
-category of accessible functors
(\mathrm{Aff}_{/S})^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathscr{S}
.

Example B.2
.

When
S=\Spec({R})
is affine,
\mathrm{Aff}_{/S}\simeq\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{op}}_{{R}}
can be identified with the opposite of the
\infty
-category of animated commutative
{R}
-algebras. Consequently, a prestack
X
over
{R}
is simply an accessible functor
X\colon\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{{R}}\to\mathscr{S}
; we will write
\mathrm{PrStk}_{R}
for the resulting
\infty
-category of
{R}
-prestacks.

The purpose of this section is to briefly recall some of the results and constructions from
[
GR17a
]
in this setting.

B.1.
Finiteness and deformation-theoretic properties of prestacks

We begin with a recollection of some of the conditions that one can impose on a prestack, following
[
GR17a
]
and
[
SAG
, Section 17]
. As in
[
GR17a
, Volume 1, Chapter 2]
and
[
SAG
, Section 17.4]
, the following finiteness condition will play a central role in this text:

Definition B.3
.

A map of prestacks
X\rightarrow S
is said to be

•

convergent
if for all
A\in\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}
, the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lim X(\tau_{\leq n}A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lim S(\tau_{\leq n}A)}

is cartesian.

•

locally almost finitely presented
if it is convergent and satisfies the following condition for each
n\geq 0
: for each filtered diagram
A_{\alpha}\colon I\to\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{\leq n}
of
n
-truncated animated rings with colimit
A
, the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\colim X(A_{\alpha})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\colim S(A_{\alpha})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S(A)}

is cartesian. Equivalently,
X
is convergent and for any
\Spec A\to S
and each
n\geq 0
, the base change
X\times_{S}\Spec(A)
restricts to a functor
\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{A,\leq n}\to\mathscr{S}
that preserves filtered colimits.

We will write
\mathrm{PrStk}_{S}^{\mathrm{laft}}\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\mathrm{PrStk}^{\mathrm{conv}}_{S}\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\mathrm{PrStk}_{S}
for the full subcategories spanned by the
S
-prestacks that are locally of finite presentation, resp. convergent.

Next, let us recall the conditions on a prestack that guarantee that it has a well-behaved infinitesimal structure, using the following algebraic terminology:

Definition B.4
.

A map of animated rings
A\to B
is said to be a
nilpotent extension
if
\pi_{0}(A)\to\pi_{0}(B)
is surjective and its kernel is a nilpotent ideal.

Lemma B.5

(
[
GR17a
, Volume 2, Chapter 1, Proposition 5.5.3]
)
.

Let
f\colon A\to B
be a nilpotent extension. Then
f
can be decomposed as the limit of an almost eventually constant tower

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A=A_{\infty}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{n-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{0}=B}

where each
A_{n}\to A_{n-1}
is a square zero extension by a
\pi_{0}(B)
-module
M_{n}
and for each
N
.

Definition B.6

(
[
GR17a
, Volume 2, Chapter 1, Definition 7.1.2]
)
.

An
S
-prestack
X
is said to
have deformation theory
(relative to
S
) if it is convergent (relative to
S
) and for each pullback diagram of animated rings on the left in which
B\to A
(and hence
B^{\prime}\to A^{\prime}
) is a nilpotent extension, the square on the right is cartesian

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(B^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(B)\times_{X(A)}X(A^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S(A^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S(B)\times_{S(A)}S(A^{\prime}).}

Equivalently, for each
s\colon\Spec(A)\to S
, the fibre
X_{s}\colon\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{A}\to\mathscr{S}
has deformation theory, i.e. preserves limits of Postnikov towers and pullbacks along nilpotent extensions of animated
A
-algebras.

Lemma B.7
.

Let
X
be a convergent
S
-prestack. Then
X
has deformation theory if and only if one of the following two equivalent conditions holds:

(1)

For each square in
(\mathrm{Aff}_{/S})^{\mathrm{op}}
opposite to a pullback square of animated rings

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\oplus I[1]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{id},0)}

with
I\in\mathrm{QC}(A)_{\geq 0}
, its image under
X\colon(\mathrm{Aff}_{/S})^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathscr{S}
is a pullback diagram of spaces.

(1’)

The condition of (1) holds for all squares such that
A,B
and
I
are eventually coconnective.

Proof.

The equivalence between
X
having deformation theory and condition (1) follows from
Lemma

B.5
by decomposing a general nilpotent extension as a tower of successive square-zero extensions. The equivalence between (1) and (1’) follows from
X
being convergent.
∎

Finally, let us introduce two versions of the formal completion of a prestack, based on the following algebraic constructions:

Definition B.8

(Reduction and pro-reduction)
.

Let
A\in\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{{R}}
be an animated
{R}
-algebra.

(1)

The
reduction

A_{\mathrm{red}}
is the quotient of
\pi_{0}(A)
by its nilradical.

(2)

The
pro-reduction

A_{\mathrm{prored}}
to be the ind-ring

A_{\mathrm{prored}}=``\colim_{I}"\pi_{0}(A)/I,

where the colimit is taken over all nilpotent ideals
I
of
\pi_{0}(A)
.

For
S=\Spec(A)
affine, we write
S_{\mathrm{red}}=\Spec(A_{\mathrm{red}})
for the underlying reduced scheme. We moreover define a pro-scheme
S_{\mathrm{prored}}
over
R
as the formal limit

\Spec(A_{\mathrm{prored}})=``\mathrm{lim}_{I}"\Spec(\pi_{0}(A)/I),

where
I
again ranges over all nilpotent ideals of
\pi_{0}(A)
. Given another
R
-prestack
X
, we set

X(A_{\mathrm{prored}}):=\Map_{\mathrm{Pro}(\mathrm{PrStk})}(S_{\mathrm{prored}},X)=\colim_{I}X(\pi_{0}(A)/I).

Definition B.9
.

Let
X\to Y
be a map of prestacks over
R
.

(1)

The
formal completion

Y^{\wedge}_{X}
is the
R
-prestack sending an animated
{R}
-algebra
A
to

Y^{\wedge}_{X}(A)=X(A_{\mathrm{red}})\times_{Y(A_{\mathrm{red}})}Y(A).

(2)

The
formal infinitesimal completion
of
Y
at
X
is the
R
-prestack sending
A
to

(X/Y)_{\inf}(A)=X(A_{\mathrm{prored}})\times_{Y(A_{\mathrm{prored}})}Y(A).

Remark B.10
.

When
X\rightarrow Y
is laft, then the canonical map
(X/Y)_{\inf}\rightarrow Y^{\wedge}_{X}
is an equivalence, as
X(A_{\mathrm{red}})\simeq X(A_{\mathrm{prored}})\times_{Y(A_{\mathrm{prored}})}Y(A_{\mathrm{red}})
.

Notation B.11
.

For
Y=\ast
the terminal prestack, we write
X_{\inf}=(X/\ast)_{\inf}
. In characteristic
0
, the prestack
X_{\inf}
is often called the
de Rham stack
of
X
.

Definition B.12
.

We will say that a map of
{R}
-prestacks
f\colon X\to Y
is a
nil-isomorphism
if for every
A\in\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{{R}}
, the map

X(A_{\mathrm{prored}})\to Y(A_{\mathrm{prored}})

is an equivalence.

Remark B.13
.

When
X\rightarrow Y
is laft, then it is a nil-isomorphism if and only if
X(A)\rightarrow Y(A)
is an equivalence for all reduced commutative rings.

Lemma B.14
.

Completion satisfies the following properties:

(1)

For any
X\to Y
, the map
Y^{\wedge}_{X}\to Y
has deformation theory. If
X\to Y
is laft, then
X\to Y^{\wedge}_{X}
and
Y^{\wedge}_{X}\to Y
are laft.

(2)

Let
X^{\prime}\to X
be an étale map between derived schemes and let
X\to Y
be a nil-isomorphism. Then the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\wedge}_{X^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}

is cartesian.

Proof.

The first part of (1) follows from the fact that for any nilpotent extension
A^{\prime}\to A
of animated rings,
Y^{\wedge}_{X}(A^{\prime})\simeq Y^{\wedge}_{X}(A)\times_{Y(A)}Y(A^{\prime})
. For the second part, it is enough to show that
Y^{\wedge}_{X}\to Y
is laft. This is the base change of the map
\ast^{\wedge}_{X}\to\ast^{\wedge}_{Y}
, which is laft because for any filtered diagram of
n
-coconnective animated rings
A_{\alpha}
with colimit
A
,
\colim X(A_{\alpha,\mathrm{red}})\simeq\colim Y(A_{\alpha,\mathrm{red}})\times_{Y(A_{\mathrm{red}})}X(A_{\mathrm{red}})
. For (2), the fact that
X^{\prime}\to X
is laft implies that
Y^{\wedge}_{X^{\prime}}\to Y
is the base change of
X^{\prime}_{\inf}\to X_{\inf}
(Remark
B.10
). It now suffices to verify that the map
X^{\prime}\to X^{\prime}_{\inf}\times_{X_{\inf}}X
is an equivalence. This follows from
X^{\prime}\to X
being étale, so that
X^{\prime}(A)\simeq X(A)\times_{X(\pi_{0}A/I)}X^{\prime}(\pi_{0}A/I)
for any nilpotent ideal
I
.
∎

B.2.
Pro-coherent sheaves

The theory of pro-coherent sheaves on affine derived schemes from Section
B.2
has an evident global analogue:

Definition B.15
.

For a prestack
X
, we define the stable presentable
\infty
-category
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X}
as

\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X}=\lim_{S\in\mathrm{Aff}_{/X}}\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{\mathcal{O}(S)}

where the limit is taken with respect to the
f^{*}
-functoriality. Every map
f\colon Y\to X
between prestacks induces an adjoint pair
f^{*}\colon\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X}\leftrightarrows\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{Y}\colon f_{*}
.

Let us point out that in the presence of Serre duality, this is Serre dual to the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{QC}^{!}_{X}
of ind-coherent sheaves on
X
defined using
f^{!}
-functoriality
[
Gai11
, 9.2.3]
.
The main features of
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}
carry over to pro-coherent sheaves on prestacks by naturality:

(1)

Since each
f^{*}\colon\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}\to\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{B}
is right
t
-exact, for any prestack
X
the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X}
admits a right complete
t
-structure such that all
f^{*}
-functors are right
t
-exact. Since the functor
\upiota\colon\Mod_{A}\to\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}
intertwines the
f^{*}
-functors, there is a natural transformation
\upiota\colon\mathrm{QC}_{X}\to\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X}
which induces an equivalence on connective objects.

(2)

Each
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X}
is tensored and cotensored over
\mathrm{QC}_{X}
and
\upiota
is compatible with the tensoring.

(3)

If
M
is an almost perfect
A
-module, then the natural map
f^{*}(M^{\vee})\to(f^{*}M)^{\vee}
is an equivalence. Using this, one sees that for any prestack
X
, pro-coherent duality induces an equivalence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\vee}\colon\mathrm{APerf}_{X}^{\mathrm{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{dAPerf}_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

between the full subcategories of pro-coherent sheaves
F
that are (
dually
)
almost perfect
in the sense that
f^{*}F
is (dually) almost perfect for each
f\colon\Spec(A)\to X
.

The
\infty
-category
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X}
is particularly well-behaved when
X
is locally coherent. In particular, we have the following generalisation of
Lemma

A.42
to derived schemes that are
locally coherent
, i.e. which admit an Zariski cover coherent affine open subschemes or equivalently, for which every affine open subscheme is coherent
[
Gla89
, Theorem 2.4.2, Corollary 2.4.5]
.

Proposition B.16
.

Let
X
be a qcqs derived scheme which is locally coherent and write write
\mathfrak{U}
for the poset of affine opens of
X
. Then the natural map

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Coh}_{X}^{\mathrm{op}}=\lim_{U\in\mathfrak{U}}\mathrm{Coh}_{U}^{\mathrm{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lim_{U\in\mathfrak{U}}\mathrm{QC}_{U}^{\vee}=\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X}}

induces an equivalence
\mathrm{Ind}(\mathrm{Coh}_{X}^{\mathrm{op}})\simeq\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X}
.

Proof.

By the usual reduction principle for qcqs schemes, it suffices to verify that for
U_{0}\subseteq X\supseteq U_{1}
an open cover such that the assertion holds on
U_{0}
,
U_{1}
and
U_{01}=U_{0}\cap U_{1}
, the assertion holds also for
X
. Since
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}
satisfies Zariski descent by Corollary
A.39
, it suffices to verify that
\mathrm{Ind}(\mathrm{Coh}^{\mathrm{op}})
also satisfies Zariski descent.

To see this, let us write
j^{*}\colon\mathrm{Ind}(\mathrm{Coh}_{X}^{\mathrm{op}})\leftrightarrows\mathrm{Ind}(\mathrm{Coh}_{U}^{\mathrm{op}})\colon j_{*}
for the adjoint pair associated to an open inclusion
j\colon U\hookrightarrow X
. Since
j_{*}
preserves colimits, this is an adjoint pair in
\mathrm{Pr^{L}}
. As such, it is the dual with respect to the Lurie tensor product of the adjoint pair
j^{*}\colon\mathrm{Ind}(\mathrm{Coh}_{X})\leftrightarrows\mathrm{Ind}(\mathrm{Coh}_{U})\colon j_{*}
considered in
[
Gai11
]
. Using this duality, one sees the counit
j^{*}j_{*}\to\mathrm{id}
is an equivalence
[
Gai11
, Lemma 4.1.1]
and that
\mathrm{Ind}(\mathrm{Coh}^{\mathrm{op}})
satisfies base change for open inclusions
[
Gai11
, Lemma 3.6.9]
. It follows that

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(j_{0}^{*},j_{01}^{*},j_{1}^{*})\colon\mathrm{Ind}(\mathrm{Coh}_{X}^{\mathrm{op}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Ind}(\mathrm{Coh}_{U_{0}}^{\mathrm{op}})\times_{\mathrm{Ind}(\mathrm{Coh}_{U_{01}}^{\mathrm{op}})}\mathrm{Ind}(\mathrm{Coh}_{U_{1}}^{\mathrm{op}})}

is an equivalence as in the proof of
[
Gai11
, Proposition 4.2.1]
: this functor has a colimit-preserving right adjoint sending a compatible triple
(F_{0},F_{1},F_{01})
to the fibre product
j_{0*}F_{0}\times_{j_{01*}F_{01}}j_{1*}F_{1}
. Using base change and the fact that
j_{i}^{*}j_{i*}\simeq\mathrm{id}
, one sees that the counit is an equivalence. To verify that the unit
F\to j_{0*}j_{0}^{*}F\times_{j_{01*}j_{01}^{*}F}j_{1*}j_{1}^{*}F
is an equivalence, it suffices to treat the case where
F\in\mathrm{Coh}(X)^{\mathrm{op}}
. In that case, the result follows from descent for coherent sheaves.
∎

B.3.
Tangent complex

In this section, we will discuss the tangent complex of a map of prestacks
X\to S
(if it exists). The main goal will be to establish a form of functoriality of the tangent complex, which is used in Section
5.2
.

Let us start by recalling the pointwise definition of the tangent and cotangent complex of an
S
-prestack. Let
\pi\colon X\to S
be an
S
-prestack and
x\colon\Spec(A)\to X
a point, and consider the functor

(28)

We then have the following:

Definition B.17
.

Let
X
be an
S
-prestack and let
x\colon\Spec(A)\to X
be a point.

(1)

We will say that
X
admits a
cotangent complex
at
x
(relative to
S
) if there exists a (necessarily unique) eventually connective module
L_{X/S,x}\in\Mod_{A,>\infty}
corepresenting the functor (
28
).

(2)

We will say that
X
admits a (pro-coherent)
tangent complex at
x
(relative to
S
) if the functor (
28
) defines an object
T_{X/S,x}\in\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}
.

If
X
is an
S
-prestack with deformation theory, then each map of rings
f\colon A\to B
induces a natural equivalence

X(A\oplus M)\times_{X(A)}\{x\}\simeq X(B\oplus f_{*}M)\times_{X(B)}\{f^{*}x\}

for each connective
A
-module
M
. This implies that
L_{X/S,f^{*}x}\simeq f^{*}L_{X/S,x}
and
T_{X/S,f^{*}x}\simeq f^{*}T_{X/S,x}
whenever
X
admits a (co)tangent complex at the point
x
. Consequently, if
X
has deformation theory and admits a (co)tangent complex at each of its points, then these (co)tangent complexes glue to global objects on
X
:

Definition B.18
.

Let
X
be an
S
-prestack with deformation theory.

(1)

If
X
admits a cotangent complex at each point, then the
cotangent complex

L_{X/S}\in\mathrm{QC}_{X}
is the unique quasi-coherent sheaf such that
L_{X/S,x}\simeq x^{*}L_{X/S}
for each point of
X
.

(2)

If
X
admits a tangent complex at each point, then the
tangent complex

T_{X/S}\in\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X}
is the unique pro-coherent sheaf such that
T_{X/S,x}\simeq x^{*}T_{X/S}
for each point of
X
.

Example B.19
.

Suppose that
X
is a laft
S
-prestack with deformation theory. Then
X
admits a relative tangent complex at each point.

Having a tangent complex at each point requires a finiteness condition on
X\to S
similar to Example
B.19
(but at the linear level). However, there are many prestacks for which one can make sense of their tangent complex, without requiring the existence of a tangent complex at each point. For example, for every affine derived
{R}
-scheme
X
, we can define the tangent complex
T_{X/{R}}=L_{X/{R}}^{\vee}
to be the pro-coherent dual of its cotangent complex. In order to define the tangent complex in such cases, and to make the functoriality of the tangent complex more explicit, we will need a somewhat technical argument.

Construction B.20
.

Let
\Mod_{\geq 0,*}\to\mathrm{Aff}
be the cocartesian fibration classifying the functor sending
f\colon\Spec(A)\to\Spec(B)
to the direct image
f_{*}\colon\Mod_{A,\geq 0}\to\Mod_{B,\geq 0}
and let us consider the following pullback

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}([1],\mathrm{PrStk})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Mod_{\geq 0,*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Aff}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{PrStk}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{ev}_{0}}

An object of
\mathscr{M}
corresponds a map of prestacks
x\colon\Spec(A)\to X
together with a connective
A
-module
M
. The functor
\mathscr{M}\to\mathrm{PrStk}
sending
(\Spec(A)\to X,M)\longmapsto X
is a cocartesian fibration and each fibre
\mathscr{M}_{X}
comes equipped with a cocartesian fibration
\mathscr{M}_{X}\to\mathrm{Aff}_{/X}
parametrising the
\infty
-category of connective modules at each point of
X
.

Let
\mathrm{Fun}_{/\mathrm{PrStk}}(\mathscr{M},\mathscr{S})\to\mathrm{PrStk}
be the relative functor
\infty
-category over
\mathrm{PrStk}
; by
[
HTT
]
this is a cartesian fibration, classified by the functor
\mathrm{PrStk}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
sending a prestack
X
to
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathscr{M}_{X},\mathscr{S})
and sending
f\colon X\to Y
to restriction along
\mathscr{M}_{X}\to\mathscr{M}_{Y}
. Let us write

\mathscr{D}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Fun}_{/\mathrm{PrStk}}(\mathscr{M},\mathscr{S})

for the full subcategory of tuples
(X,F)
consisting of a prestack
X
and a functor
F\colon\mathscr{M}_{X}\to\mathscr{S}
satisfying the following two properties:

(1)

F
sends cocartesian arrows with respect to
\mathscr{M}_{X}\to\mathrm{Aff}_{/X}
to equivalences.

(2)

For each
x\colon\Spec(A)\to X
, the restriction of
F
to the fibre

F_{x}\colon\mathscr{M}_{X,x}\simeq\Mod_{A,\geq 0}\to\mathscr{S}

is accessible and preserves the terminal object, pullbacks along
\pi_{0}
-surjections and limits of almost eventually constant towers.

Let us write
\mathscr{D}^{\mathrm{aft}}\subseteq\mathscr{D}
and
\mathscr{D}^{\mathrm{corep}}\subseteq\mathscr{D}
for the subcategories of tuples
(X,F)
where, in addition to condition (
2
), each restriction
F_{x}
is almost finitely presented, respectively corepresentable by an eventually connective
A
-module.

One readily verifies that for any map of prestacks
f\colon X\to Y
, restriction along
\mathscr{M}_{X}\to\mathscr{M}_{Y}
preserves these conditions, so that for any cartesian arrow
(X,G)\to(Y,F)
with
(Y,F)\in\mathscr{D}
,
(X,G)
is in
\mathscr{D}
as well (and similarly in the other cases). Consequently, we obtain a diagram of cartesian fibrations (and maps preserving cartesian arrows)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{D}^{\mathrm{aft}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{D}^{\mathrm{corep}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{PrStk}}

Proposition B.21
.

Each of the above three cartesian fibrations classifies a functor
\mathrm{PrStk}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
preserving limits. Furthermore, the cartesian fibration
\mathscr{D}^{\mathrm{aft}}\to\mathrm{PrStk}
is classified by the functor
X\longmapsto\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X}
and the cartesian fibration
\mathscr{D}^{\mathrm{corep}}\to\mathrm{PrStk}
is classified by the functor
X\longmapsto(\mathrm{QC}^{-}_{X})^{\mathrm{op}}
.

Proof.

Let
X
be a prestack and consider the cocartesian fibration
\mathscr{M}_{X}\to\mathrm{Aff}_{/X}
that classifies the diagram of categories
\mathrm{Aff}_{/X}\to\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
sending a map of affines
f\colon\Spec(A)\to\Spec(B)
over
X
to
f_{*}\colon\Mod_{A,\geq 0}\to\Mod_{B,\geq 0}
. The localisation of
\mathscr{M}_{X}
at the cocartesian arrows classifies the colimit of this diagram of categories. Condition (
1
) therefore implies that
\mathscr{D}_{X}
is a full subcategory of the limit

\lim_{\Spec(A)\in\mathrm{Aff}_{/X}}\mathrm{Fun}\big(\Mod_{A,\geq 0},\mathscr{S}\big)

where each morphism
f\colon\Spec(A)\to\Spec(B)
in
\mathrm{Aff}_{/X}
is sent to restriction along
f_{*}
. Using this, Condition (
2
) then identifies

\mathscr{D}_{X}\simeq\lim_{\Spec(A)\in\mathrm{Aff}_{/X}}\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathrm{rex},\mathrm{acc}}(\Mod^{+}_{A,\geq 0},\mathscr{S})

with the limit of the
\infty
-categories of accessible functors that are right exact (or equivalently, that preserve the terminal object and pullbacks along
\pi_{0}
-surjections). This identification is natural in
X
, so that
X\mapsto\mathscr{D}_{X}
evidently preserves limits. The categories
\mathscr{D}^{\mathrm{aft}}_{X}
and
\mathscr{D}^{\mathrm{corep}}_{X}
are the limits over
\mathrm{Aff}_{/X}
of the diagram of full subcategories of
\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathrm{rex},\mathrm{acc}}(\Mod^{+}_{A,\geq 0},\mathscr{S})
spanned the by almost finitely presented and corepresentable functors. It follows that there are natural equivalences
\mathscr{D}^{\mathrm{aft}}\simeq\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X}
and
\mathscr{D}_{X}^{\mathrm{corep}}\simeq(\mathrm{QC}^{-}_{X})^{\mathrm{op}}
.
∎

Observation B.22
.

Given a prestack
X
, the inclusion

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X}=\lim_{\Spec(A)\to X}\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lim_{\Spec(A)\to X}\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathrm{rex},\mathrm{acc}}(\Mod^{+}_{A,\geq 0},\mathscr{S})\simeq\mathscr{D}_{X}}

preserves colimits. Since
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X}
is presentable, it follows that there is a right adjoint
\mathscr{D}_{X}\to\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X}
. Given
F\in\mathscr{D}_{X}
, we will refer to its image under this right adjoint
F^{\mathrm{aft}}
as the
almost finitely presented approximation
to
F
. Let us point out that the right adjoints
\mathscr{D}_{X}\to\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X}
do
not
assemble into a right adjoint
\mathscr{D}\to\mathscr{D}^{\mathrm{aft}}
.

Let us now use this construction to give a functorial description of the tangent complex.

Construction B.23
.

Let us write
\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathrm{PrStk})^{\mathrm{def}}\subseteq\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathrm{PrStk})
for the full subcategory of maps
X\to S
that have deformation theory. We define a functor

\Phi\colon\mathscr{M}\times_{\mathrm{PrStk}}\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathrm{PrStk})^{\mathrm{def}}\to\mathscr{S};

as follows. The domain of
\Phi
is the fibre product of the cocartesian fibration
\mathscr{M}\to\mathrm{PrStk}
from Construction
B.20
and the functor
\mathrm{ev}_{0}\colon\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathrm{PrStk})^{\mathrm{def}}\to\mathrm{PrStk}
taking the domain. We then define
\Phi
by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{57.13866pt}{-13.55698pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 79.06 -18.76)} \pgfsys@hbox{58}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
