\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\right\}.

The functor
\Phi
is adjoint to

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathrm{PrStk})^{\mathrm{def}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}_{/\mathrm{PrStk}}(\mathscr{M},\mathscr{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{PrStk}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{ev}_{0}}

sending
X\to S
to the restriction
\Phi(-,X\to S)\colon\mathscr{M}_{X}\to\mathscr{S}
. The fact that
X\to S
has deformation theory implies that
\Phi(-,X\to S)
satisfies conditions (
1
) and (
2
) from Construction
B.20
. Consequently, we obtain a functor that we will denote by

(29)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathrm{PrStk})^{\mathrm{def}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{PrStk}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{ev}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{\mathrm{pre}}}

If
X\to S
is laft, then
T^{\mathrm{pre}}(X/S)
is contained in
\mathscr{D}^{\mathrm{aft}}
. Under the equivalence
\mathscr{D}^{\mathrm{aft}}_{X}\simeq\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}(X)
, this corresponds to the tangent complex
T_{X/S}
from
Definition

B.18
. If
X\to S
admits cotangent complexes at all points of
X
, then
T^{\mathrm{pre}}(X/S)\in\mathscr{D}^{\mathrm{corep}}
and corresponds under the equivalence
\mathscr{D}^{\mathrm{corep}}_{X}\simeq(\mathrm{QC}^{-}_{X})^{\mathrm{op}}
to the cotangent complex
L_{X/S}
from
Definition

B.18
.

From the formula for
\Phi
, one readily sees:

Lemma B.24
.

The functor
T^{\mathrm{pre}}
preserves pullbacks.

Since
\mathscr{D}^{\mathrm{corep}}\subseteq\mathscr{D}
is closed under pullbacks, this reproduces for example the well-known behaviour of the cotangent complex with respect to base change and composition.

Let us now use the functor
T^{\mathrm{pre}}
to define the tangent complex of a prestack with deformation theory:

Definition B.25
.

Let
X
be an
S
-prestack with deformation theory. We define the (pro-coherent)
tangent complex of
X
over
S
to be the almost finitely presented approximation
T_{X/S}=T_{X/S}^{\mathrm{pre},\mathrm{aft}}\in\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X}
from Observation
B.22
.

Example B.26
.

If
X
is a
laft

S
-prestack with deformation theory, this coincides with the pointwise definition of tangent complex from Definition
B.18
. Indeed, since
X\to S
is laft,
T_{X/S}^{\mathrm{pre}}
is already contained in
\mathscr{D}_{X}^{\mathrm{aft}}\subseteq\mathscr{D}_{X}
.

Example B.27
.

Suppose that
X
is a (not necessarily laft)
S
-prestack with deformation theory and a cotangent complex at each point. Then there is an equivalence
T_{X/S}=L_{X/S}^{\vee}
in
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X}
, where
L_{X/S}^{\vee}
is the pro-coherent linear dual of
L_{X/S}\in(\mathrm{QC}^{-}_{X})^{\mathrm{op}}
(see
Example

A.32
). Since the right adjoint
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X}\to\mathrm{QC}_{X}
preserves the linear dual of a quasi-coherent sheaf, one can think of
T_{X/S}\in\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X}
as a pro-coherent refinement of the
coherator
of the tangent sheaf on
X
, in the sense of
[
Sta19
, Tag 08D6]
.

The tangent complex
T_{X/S}
is much less well-behaved when
X\to S
is not laft. For example, given a map of
S
-prestacks with deformation theory
X\to Y
, there is no direct map from
T_{X/S}
to
f^{*}T_{Y/S}
. For instance, if
X
and
Y
admit a cotangent complex at each point, there is only a zig-zag of maps
L_{X/S}^{\vee}\to(f^{*}L_{Y/S})^{\vee}\leftarrow f^{*}(L_{Y/S}^{\vee})
because base change does not commute with duality without finiteness assumptions. Let us nonetheless record the following properties:

Proposition B.28
.

Let
f\colon X\to Y
be a map of
S
-prestacks with deformation theory. Then the following hold.

(1)

There is a fibre sequence
T_{X/Y}\to T_{X/S}\to(f^{*}T^{\mathrm{pre}}_{Y/S})^{\mathrm{aft}}
.

(2)

There is a zig-zag of maps
T_{X/S}\to(f^{*}T^{\mathrm{pre}}_{Y/S})^{\mathrm{aft}}\leftarrow f^{*}T_{Y/S}
in
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X}
.

(3)

If
f
is a formally étale, then there is a map
f^{*}T_{Y/S}\to T_{X/S}
.

(4)

T_{X/S}\simeq T_{X/S^{\wedge}_{X}}
.

If
X
and
Y
are furthermore
laft

S
-prestacks with deformation theory, then:

(5)

There is natural map
T_{X/S}\to f^{*}T_{Y/S}
that fits into a fibre sequence
T_{X/Y}\to T_{X/S}\to f^{*}T_{Y/S}
in
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X}
.

(6)

Given a morphism
S^{\prime}\to S
, there is a natural equivalence
T_{X\times_{S}S^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}=p^{*}T_{X/S}
where
p\colon X\times_{S}S^{\prime}\to X
is the evident projection.

Proof.

The first three assertions follow from the natural fibre sequence
T^{\mathrm{pre}}_{X/Y}\to T^{\mathrm{pre}}_{X/S}\to f^{*}T^{\mathrm{pre}}_{Y/S}
in
\mathscr{D}_{X}
by taking almost finitely presented approximations, using that
T^{\mathrm{pre}}_{X/Y}\simeq 0
if
X\to Y
is formally étale. Likewise, (4) follows from the fact that
T^{\mathrm{pre}}_{X/S}\simeq T^{\mathrm{pre}}_{X/S^{\wedge}_{X}}
. Assertion (5) follows from the fact that
T_{Y/S}=T^{\mathrm{pre}}_{Y/S}
and (6) follows from the equivalence
T^{\mathrm{pre}}_{X\times_{S}S^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}=p^{*}T^{\mathrm{pre}}_{X/S}
, which follows from Lemma
B.24
.
∎

The somewhat problematic behaviour of
T_{X/S}
when
X\to S
is not laft will not be a problem for us, because we are mainly interested in the
\infty
-category
(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X})_{/T_{X/S}}
of pro-coherent sheaves over
T_{X/S}
. This
\infty
-category is equivalent to the
\infty
-category
(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X})_{/T_{X/S}^{\mathrm{pre}}}
of pro-coherent sheaves equipped with a map
F\to T^{\mathrm{pre}}_{X/S}
in
\mathscr{D}_{X}
, and
T^{\mathrm{pre}}_{X/S}
is very well-behaved.

For instance, each laft map
X\to Y
of
S
-prestacks with deformation theory gives rise to a natural object
T_{X/Y}\in(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X})_{/T_{X/S}}
, or equivalently, in
(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X})_{/T_{X/S}^{\mathrm{pre}}}
. Let us conclude with a functorial description of this construction:

Construction B.29
.

Let us fix a base prestack
S
and write
\mathrm{PrStk}^{\mathrm{def},\mathrm{laft-map}}_{S}
for the subcategory of
\mathrm{PrStk}_{S}
whose objects are prestacks with deformation theory over
S
and whose morphisms are the laft morphisms between such
S
-prestacks. Let us write

\mathscr{E}=\mathscr{D}\times_{\mathrm{PrStk}}\mathrm{PrStk}^{\mathrm{def},\mathrm{laft-map}}_{S},\qquad\qquad\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}=\mathscr{D}^{\mathrm{aft}}\times_{\mathrm{PrStk}}\mathrm{PrStk}^{\mathrm{def},\mathrm{laft-map}}_{S}

for the evident restrictions. The cartesian fibration
\mathscr{E}\to\mathrm{PrStk}^{\mathrm{def},\mathrm{laft-map}}_{S}
then admits a section
T^{\mathrm{pre}}
sending each
X\longmapsto T^{\mathrm{pre}}_{X/S}
. Taking fibrewise over-categories then produces a diagram that we will denote by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{/T}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}\times_{\mathscr{E}}\mathscr{E}_{/T^{\mathrm{pre}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{E}_{/T^{\mathrm{pre}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{PrStk}^{\mathrm{def},\mathrm{laft-map}}_{S}.}

At the level of the fibres over a prestack
X
, this is given by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X})_{/T_{X/S}}\simeq(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X})\times_{\mathscr{D}_{X}}(\mathscr{D}_{X})_{/T^{\mathrm{pre}}_{X/S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathscr{D}_{X})_{/T^{\mathrm{pre}}_{X/S}}.}

Lemma B.30
.

The projection
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{/T}\to\mathrm{PrStk}^{\mathrm{def},\mathrm{laft-map}}_{S}
is a cartesian fibration.

Proof.

Let us first consider the cartesian fibration
\pi\colon\mathscr{E}\to\mathrm{PrStk}^{\mathrm{def},\mathrm{laft-map}}_{S}
. This classifies a functor sending
X
to
\mathscr{E}_{X}=\lim_{\Spec(A)\to X}\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathrm{ex},\mathrm{acc}}(\Mod^{+}_{A},\mathrm{Sp})
and
f\colon X\to Y
to the evident functor between limits

f^{*}\colon\mathscr{E}_{Y}=\lim_{\Spec(A)\to Y}\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathrm{ex},\mathrm{acc}}(\Mod^{+}_{A},\mathrm{Sp})\to\lim_{\Spec(A)\to X}\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathrm{ex},\mathrm{acc}}(\Mod^{+}_{A},\mathrm{Sp})=\mathscr{E}_{X}.

Note that each
\mathscr{E}_{X}
is a stable
\infty
-category and that
f^{*}
is exact. This implies that
\mathscr{E}
admits finite limits and that
\pi
admits a fully faithful right adjoint (taking the terminal object fibrewise).
The projection
\mathscr{E}_{/T^{\mathrm{pre}}}\to\mathrm{PrStk}^{\mathrm{def},\mathrm{laft-map}}_{S}
is then a cartesian fibration as well: indeed, given a map
f\colon X\to Y
and
F\to T^{\mathrm{pre}}_{Y/S}
in
\mathscr{E}_{Y}
, the cartesian lift of
f
in
\mathscr{E}_{/T^{\mathrm{pre}}}
corresponds to the following pullback square in
\mathscr{E}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{\mathrm{pre}}_{X/S}\times_{T^{\mathrm{pre}}_{Y/X}}F}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{\mathrm{pre}}_{X/S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{\mathrm{pre}}_{Y/X}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{f}}

More explicitly, this is given by the pullback
T^{\mathrm{pre}}_{X/S}\times_{f^{*}T^{\mathrm{pre}}_{Y/X}}f^{*}F
in the fibre
\mathscr{E}_{X}
.

Finally, it suffices that the full subcategory
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{/T}\subseteq\mathscr{E}_{/T^{\mathrm{pre}}}
is stable under these cartesian arrows. For this, suppose that
F\in\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{Y}\subseteq\mathscr{E}_{Y}
and consider the fibre sequence in
\mathscr{E}_{X}

T^{\mathrm{pre}}_{X/Y}\to T^{\mathrm{pre}}_{X/S}\times_{f^{*}T^{\mathrm{pre}}_{Y/X}}f^{*}F\to f^{*}F.

The base change functor
f^{*}
sends
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{Y}
to
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X}
, so
f^{\ast}F\in\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X}
. Since
f\colon X\to Y
was assumed to be laft,
T^{\mathrm{pre}}_{X/Y}=T_{X/Y}\in\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X}
as well so that the middle term is contained in
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X}
, as desired.
∎

Remark B.31
.

Informally, Lemma
B.30
asserts that there is a functor sending each
S
-prestack
X
with deformation theory to the
\infty
-category
(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X})_{/T_{X/S}}
, and each map
f\colon X^{\prime}\to X
to a functor

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\sharp}\colon(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X})_{/T_{X/S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X^{\prime}})_{/T_{X^{\prime}/S}};\quad(F\to T_{X/S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{X^{\prime}/S}\times_{f^{*}(T^{\mathrm{pre}}_{X/S})^{\mathrm{aft}}}F.}

In particular, when
f
is formally étale, then
f^{\sharp}(F\to T_{X/S})=f^{*}F
by Proposition
B.28
.

If
f\colon X\to Y
is a map of
S
-prestacks with deformation theory and a cotangent complex, Example
B.27
identifies the resulting base change functor with
f^{\sharp}(F)=T_{X/S}\times_{(f^{*}L_{Y/S})^{\vee}}f^{*}F
, using the natural map
f^{*}(T_{Y/S})=f^{*}(L_{Y/S}^{\vee})\to(f^{*}L_{Y/S})^{\vee}
.

Proposition B.32
.

There is a map of cartesian fibrations preserving cartesian arrows

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathrm{PrStk}_{S}^{\mathrm{def},\mathrm{laft-map}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{/T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{PrStk}_{S}^{\mathrm{def},\mathrm{laft-map}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{ev}_{0}}

sending each
X\to Y
to
T_{X/Y}\to T_{X/S}
. Furthermore, the induced functors between fibres preserve limits.

In other words, for every square of laft maps between
S
-prestacks with deformation theory

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}

there is a natural map
T_{X^{\prime}/Y^{\prime}}\to f^{\sharp}(T_{X/Y})
where
f^{\sharp}
is as in Remark
B.31
. The fact that
T
preserves cartesian arrows corresponds to this map being an equivalence if
Y^{\prime}=Y
. We are mostly interested for in the situation of (locally coherent) derived schemes
X
(over a base
S
): the above then provides a functor sending
X\to Y
a map
T_{X/Y}\to L_{X/Y}^{\vee}
in
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X}
, functorial with respect to laft maps of schemes
X^{\prime}\to X
.

Proof.

The desired functor arises formally from the functor
T^{\mathrm{pre}}
in (
29
), using that
T_{X/Y}=T^{\mathrm{pre}}_{X/Y}
if
X\to Y
is laft (Example
B.26
). To see that is preserves cartesian arrows, note that
T^{\mathrm{pre}}
sends

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{\mathrm{pre}}_{X^{\prime}/Y}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{\mathrm{pre}}_{X/Y}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{\mathrm{pre}}_{X^{\prime}/S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{\mathrm{pre}}_{X/S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}

Here the left square defines a cartesian arrow covering
f
in the domain. To see that its image is cartesian, it suffices to verify that the right square is cartesian in
\mathscr{E}
; this follows from the fact that the functor
T^{\mathrm{pre}}
from (
29
) preserves pullbacks by Lemma
B.24
.
∎

We conclude by recording the following property of the induced functors
T_{X/-}
between the fibres.

Proposition B.33
.

Let
X
be an
S
-prestack with deformation theory and consider the composite

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi\colon(\mathrm{PrStk}_{S}^{\mathrm{def},\mathrm{laft-map}})_{X/-}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X})_{/T_{X/S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{X/-}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{cofib}}

where the first functor is induced by the functor
T
from Proposition
B.32
and the second functor sends
F\to T_{X/S}
to its cofibre. This functor admits a left adjoint, sending each
F\in\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X}
to a map
X\to X_{F}
that is furthermore a nil-isomorphism. In particular, the functor
T_{X/-}
preserves limits.

Proof.

Unravelling the definitions,
\Psi(f\colon X\to Y)=(f^{*}T^{\mathrm{pre}}_{Y/S})^{\mathrm{aft}}
. To describe its left adjoint, recall from Construction
B.20
that each
F\in\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X}
defines a functor
F\colon\mathcal{M}_{X}\to\mathscr{S}
on the
\infty
-category of tuples
(x\colon\Spec(A)\to X,\ M\in\Mod_{A,\geq 0}^{+})
. Let
\mathscr{F}\to\mathcal{M}_{X}
be the (cocartesian) unstraightening of this functor. We then define

X_{F}=\colim_{(A,M)\in\mathscr{F}^{\mathrm{op}}}\Spec(A\oplus M)

as the colimit in convergent
S
-prestacks. There are natural maps
X\to X_{F}\to X
. The first map is the natural map from the colimit indexed by the subcategory of
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{op}}
on those
(A,M)
where
M\simeq 0
is the zero module. The second map is the colimit over the objects of
\mathcal{F}
, corresponding to tuples

(x\colon\Spec(A)\to X,\ M\in\Mod_{A,\geq 0}^{+},\ y\in F(M)),

of the maps
\Spec(A\oplus M)\to\Spec(A)\xrightarrow{x}X
. Given a map
f\colon X\to Y
to an
S
-prestack with deformation theory, a map
X_{F}\to Y
of
S
-prestacks under
X
corresponds to a natural transformation
F\to f^{*}T^{\mathrm{pre}}_{Y/S}
between functors
\mathcal{M}_{X}\to\mathscr{S}
, i.e. a map
F\to(f^{*}T^{\mathrm{pre}}_{Y/S})^{\mathrm{aft}}=\Psi(f\colon X\to Y)
in
\mathrm{QC}^{\vee}_{X}
.

Hence, it remains to show that
X\to X_{F}
is indeed a laft nil-isomorphism and that
X_{F}
has deformation theory. For this it suffices to check that for each
x\colon\Spec(A)\to X
with
A
eventually coconnective, the base change
x^{*}X_{F}
is a laft
A
-prestack with deformation theory that is nil-isomorphic to
\Spec(A)
. This base change can be identified as follows. Let
\mathcal{F}_{x}
denote the unstraightening of the restriction
x^{*}F\colon\Mod_{A,\geq 0}^{+}\to\mathscr{S}
of
F
to the fibre of
(\mathcal{M}_{X})\times_{\mathrm{Aff}_{/X}}\{x\}
. Then

(30)

x^{*}X_{F}=\colim_{M\in\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{op}}_{x}}\Spec(A\oplus M)

where the colimit is taken in convergent
A
-prestacks. This is clearly nil-isomorphic to
\Spec(A)
, as every
\Spec(A\oplus M)
is nil-isomorphic to
\Spec(A)
. The category
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{op}}_{x}
is filtered, so that
x^{*}X_{F}
has deformation theory, being a filtered colimit of
A
-prestacks with deformation theory.

To see that
x^{*}X_{F}
is laft, observe that for any eventually coconnective animated
A
-algebra
B
, one can either compute the value
x^{*}X_{F}(B)
using the colimit (
30
), or as follows. Let us write
\mathrm{Sqz}_{/B}=\Mod_{A,\geq 0}^{+}\times_{\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{A}}\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{A/B}
for the
\infty
-category of
A
-modules
M\in\Mod_{A,\geq 0}^{+}
equipped with a map of animated
A
-algebras
A\oplus M\to B
. Then

x^{*}X_{F}(B)=\colim_{M\in\mathrm{Sqz}_{/B}}x^{*}F(M).

If
B
is
n
-coconnective, it suffices to take the colimit over the cofinal subcategory
\mathrm{Sqz}_{\leq n/B}\subseteq\mathrm{Sqz}_{/B}
of
n
-coconnective
A
-modules
M
equipped with a map
A\oplus M\to B
. Note that
\mathrm{Sqz}_{\leq n/B}
is compactly generated with compact objects given by

(M,A\oplus M\to B),

where
M
is the
n
-truncation of a perfect connective
A
-module; let us refer to such
n
-coconnective modules as
n
-compact
. Since
x^{*}F
preserves colimits of filtered diagrams of
n
-coconnective
A
-modules, one can thus reduce the above colimit to the colimit over the full subcategory
\mathrm{Sqz}^{\omega}_{\leq n/B}\subseteq\mathrm{Sqz}_{\leq n/B}
of
n
-compact
A
-modules
M
with a map
A\oplus M\to B
. Equivalently, this means that

x^{*}X_{F}(B)=\colim_{M\in\mathcal{F}_{x,\leq n}^{\omega,\mathrm{op}}}\Map_{\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{A}}(A\oplus M,B)\simeq\colim_{M\in\mathcal{F}_{x,\leq n}^{\omega,\mathrm{op}}}\Map_{\tau_{\leq n}\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{A}}(\tau_{\leq n}A\oplus M,B)

where
\mathcal{F}_{x,\leq n}^{\omega}\subseteq\mathcal{F}_{x}
is the full subcategory of tuples
(M,y\in x^{*}F(M))
where
M
is
n
-compact.

We conclude that the restriction of
x^{*}X_{F}
to
\tau_{\leq n}\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{A}
is equivalent to a colimit of representables
\Spec(\tau_{\leq n}A\oplus M)
where
M
is an
n
-compact
A
-module. By Lemma
2.20
, each square zero extension
\tau_{\leq n}A\oplus M
by an
n
-compact
A
-module defines a compact object in
\tau_{\leq n}\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{A}
. It follows that
x^{*}X_{F}
restricts to a functor on
\tau_{\leq n}\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{an}}_{A}
preserving filtered colimits, so that
x^{*}X_{F}
is indeed laft.
∎
